<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the canonical topological cone of all extended lower-semicontinuous tracial weights determines a separable nuclear <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra after tensoring with the Razak–Jacelon algebra and the compact operators, answering Robert's trace-cone classification question positively. The isomorphism realizes the prescribed cone map, with arbitrary ideal structure and without a density assumption on the finite domains of the weights.

- **claim-main-classification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $A$ and $B$ be separable nuclear complex $C^*$-algebras. If $T(A)$ and $T(B)$ are isomorphic as topological cones, then $$A\otimes\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal K\ \cong\ B\otimes\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal K,$$ where $\mathcal K=\mathcal K(\ell^2(\mathbb N))$ and all tensor products are spatial.
  - Explanation: An isomorphism of the topological trace cones of two separable nuclear algebras forces their Razak–Jacelon stabilizations by the compact operators to be isomorphic. No simplicity, ideal restriction or density of finite domains is assumed.
- **claim-trace-realification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-realification)): For every $C^*$-algebra $C$, the trace cone with topology <a href="#intro:trace-topology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:trace-topology">[intro:trace-topology]</a> is compact Hausdorff, and it is second countable when $C$ is separable. If $C$ is exact, the assignment $\tau\mapsto d_\tau$ identifies this cone with the topological cone of functionals on $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)$.* *For every $C^*$-algebra $C$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C\otimes\mathcal W)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)_{\mathbb R}.$$ Realification is determined functorially by the topological functional cone. Its realization as functions on that cone has pointwise order, pointwise addition, and multiplication by positive real scalars. It has the same functional cone as $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)$.* *Consequently the given trace-cone isomorphism induces isomorphisms $$\label{st:transport} F:T(Q)\longrightarrow T(P),\qquad G:\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(P)\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q),\qquad d_\tau(Gx)=d_{F\tau}(x).$$ In particular, for $x,y\in\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q)$, $$\label{st:pointwise-order} x\leq y \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad d_\tau(x)\leq d_\tau(y)\quad\hbox{for every }\tau\in T(Q).$$ Multiplication by every positive real scalar is an order automorphism preserving increasing suprema and compact containment.
  - Explanation: The trace cone is compact Hausdorff, and for exact algebras it is the cone of functionals on the Cuntz semigroup. Tensoring with the Razak–Jacelon algebra realifies the semigroup. A cone isomorphism therefore transports ranks and Cuntz classes, and Cuntz order becomes pointwise rank order.
- **claim-ideal-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-ideals)): The maps $F,G$ identify the complete ideal lattices of $P$ and $Q$, and these identifications agree. They identify the finite and zero ideals of corresponding weights. The resulting homeomorphism of primitive ideal spaces will be used to regard $\mathop{\mathrm{Prim}}(P)=\mathop{\mathrm{Prim}}(Q)=X$. For an open $U\subseteq X$, write $P(U)$ and $Q(U)$ for the corresponding ideals. Then $$G(\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(P(U)))=\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q(U)),$$ and $$\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)=Q(U)\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(F\tau)=P(U), \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}(\tau)=Q(U)\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}(F\tau)=P(U).$$
  - Explanation: The transported maps identify the ideal lattices of the two stabilized algebras, together with the finite and zero ideals of corresponding weights. This gives a common primitive ideal space.
- **claim-countable-controllers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-controllers)): The open-set lattice of $X$ has a countable basis closed under finite unions such that every open $U$ is the union of basis opens $V\Subset U$. If $V\Subset U$, there is a compactly Cuntz supported $b_{V,U}\in Q(U)_+$ with $$Q(V)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_Q(b_{V,U}).$$ Every compactly Cuntz supported $c\in Q(V)_+$ then satisfies $$\label{st:controller-multiple} [c]\leq N[b_{V,U}] \quad\hbox{for some finite }N\in\mathbb N.$$ The element $b_{V,U}$ is independent of $c$; the integer $N$ need not be.
  - Explanation: The open sets have a countable compact-containment basis. Each pair of basis opens carries one fixed controller that dominates every compactly supported class up to a finite multiple depending on the class.
- **claim-trace-preserving-folding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-folding)): Finite matrix amplifications, including amplifications whose sizes vary with the coordinate, may be folded into $Q$ so as to preserve all trace and rank calculations with unnormalized internal matrix traces. The folding of a fixed first corner can be made arbitrarily close to the identity on any prescribed finite subset of $Q$, by a multiplier-unitary adjustment.* *If $h_1,\ldots,h_l$ are c.p.c. maps with values in finite matrix amplifications of $Q$, and $\lambda_i>0$ are rational with $\sum_i\lambda_i=1$, their normalized diagonal average is a c.p.c. map $h$ into $Q$ satisfying, for $s\geq0$, $$\begin{aligned} \tau(h(s))&=\sum_i\lambda_i\tau(h_i(s)), &\tau(h(s)^2)&=\sum_i\lambda_i\tau(h_i(s)^2), \label{st:average-traces}\\ d_\tau((h(s)-\varepsilon)_+) &=\sum_i\lambda_i d_\tau((h_i(s)-\varepsilon)_+). \label{st:average-ranks} \end{aligned}$$ The traces on the right include the unnormalized internal matrices. All equalities include zero and infinite values.
  - Explanation: Matrix amplifications and rational diagonal averages fold back into the algebra without changing unnormalized traces or ranks, and the folding is close to the identity on any finite set.
- **claim-regularized-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-seq-regularized)): Let $\omega$ be a free ultrafilter on $\mathbb N$, and let $\tau_n\in T(Q)$ be arbitrary. For a bounded positive sequence $(x_n)$, the formula $$\label{seq:regularization-formula} \rho([(x_n)])= \sup_{\varepsilon>0}\lim_{n\to\omega}\tau_n((x_n-\varepsilon)_+)$$ defines a lower-semicontinuous tracial weight on $D$. Its rank functional is $$\label{seq:regularized-rank} d_\rho([(x_n)])= \sup_{\varepsilon>0}\lim_{n\to\omega}d_{\tau_n}((x_n-\varepsilon)_+).$$ If $\sigma=\lim_{n\to\omega}\tau_n$ in the canonical topology on $T(Q)$, then $\rho\|_Q=\sigma$.
  - Explanation: An ultrafilter limit of coordinate weights, regularized by positive cuts, is a lower-semicontinuous trace on the sequence algebra, with the expected rank functional and the expected restriction to the constants.
- **claim-elementary-determination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-seq-elementary-tests)): A trace $\theta\in T(S_Y)$ equals $m\otimes\nu$ if its ranks on the preceding countable test collection satisfy <a href="#seq:product-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:product-rank">[seq:product-rank]</a>. In particular, checking those ranks for every regularized limit suffices for <a href="#seq:model-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:model-equation">[seq:model-equation]</a>.
  - Explanation: A trace on the cone algebra equals the product trace once its ranks agree on a countable family of elementary cut tensors, so the model equation need only be checked on those tests.
- **claim-bounded-witness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-seq-uniform-witness)): Let $a,b$ be positive elements in a matrix algebra over any $C^*$-algebra, and suppose that $a\precsim(b-\delta)_+$, where $\delta>0$. For every $\eta>0$ there is a comparison element $w$ such that $$\label{seq:witness-bound} \\|w^*bw-a\\|<\eta, \qquad \\|w\\|^2\leq\frac{\\|a\\|+\eta}{\delta}.$$ The bound is independent of the matrix size.
  - Explanation: A comparison into a cut target has a witness whose norm is bounded using only the norm of the source, the accuracy and the cut depth, independently of matrix size.
- **claim-strict-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-seq-strict-comparison)): Let $x,y\in D_+$. If there is $0<\alpha<1$ such that $$\label{seq:strict-rank-hypothesis} d_\rho(x)\leq\alpha d_\rho(y) \quad\hbox{for every regularized limit }\rho,$$ then $x\precsim y$. The same assertion holds after finite matrix amplification and after coordinatewise folding.
  - Explanation: Strict rank domination at every regularized limit implies Cuntz comparison in the sequence algebra, also after finite amplification and folding.
- **claim-sandwich-comparisons** (theorem, [source](#stmt-seq-sandwich)): Let $p:S_Y\to D$ be a model, let $s=f\otimes a\geq0$, and suppose that $0<m_0:=m(f>0)<\infty$. For $r>0$, choose $c_r\in Q_+$ representing $rG[a]$. Then $$\label{seq:uncut-sandwich} c_r\precsim p(s)\quad(0<r<m_0), \qquad p(s)\precsim c_r\quad(r>m_0).$$ The following versions have fixed target cuts.* *If $\eta,\delta>0$, $0<r<m(f>\eta)$, and $c$ represents $rG[(a-\delta)_+]$, then, for every $0<\kappa<\eta\delta$, $$\label{seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich} c\precsim(p(f\otimes a)-\kappa)_+.$$ If $r'>m_0$ and $\varepsilon>0$, then a representative $b$ of $r'G[a]$ has a positive cut $(b-\kappa)_+$ such that $$\label{seq:upper-trimmed-sandwich} (p(f\otimes a)-\varepsilon)_+\precsim(b-\kappa)_+.$$ The choices in these two displays can be made independently of the model $p$. The complete collection of such comparisons on the countable compact tests characterizes the model equation.
  - Explanation: A model is sandwiched between constant classes scaled slightly below and above the measure of the function support, with fixed-cut versions independent of the model. These comparisons characterize the model equation.
- **claim-constant-ideals-all-traces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-seq-all-traces)): For a model $p$ and every $s\in S$, $$\label{seq:constant-ideal} \mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(p(s))=\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U_s)).$$ Given countably many models $p_i:S\to D$, there is a separable subalgebra $D_0\subseteq D$ containing their ranges and the constants $Q$, such that, for every $\gamma\in T(D_0)$, $$\label{seq:all-trace-model-equation} \gamma\circ p_i=m\otimes F(\gamma\|_Q) \quad\hbox{for every }i.$$ It may also contain any prescribed countable subset of $D$.
  - Explanation: The ideal generated by the image of an element equals the constant ideal of its coarse label, and the model equation holds for all traces of a suitable separable subalgebra.
- **claim-local-error-ideal-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-seq-smallideal-properties)): The definition of $J(U)$ is independent of representatives and makes it a closed two-sided ideal of $D$. The assignment is order preserving, and $$\label{seq:smallideal-support} J(U)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U)).$$ If $\rho$ is a regularized limit and its restriction $\sigma=\rho\|_Q$ is densely finite on $Q(U)$, then $$\label{seq:smallideal-vanishing} \rho\|_{J(U)}=0.$$ All assertions use the same conventions in finite matrix algebras.
  - Explanation: The local error ideal is a well-defined closed two-sided ideal, monotone in the open set, contained in the ideal generated by the constants of that open set, and killed by every regularized limit that is densely finite there.
- **claim-local-diagonalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-seq-diagonal)): Let $\mathcal A$ be a countable dense $\mathbb Q+i\mathbb Q$ $*$-subalgebra of $S=S_Y$, containing the localized dense sets to be tested. For each $j$, let $L_j:\mathcal A\to D$ be a map, and choose bounded coordinate representatives $L_{j,n}(a)$ for every $a\in\mathcal A$. Assume that, for each fixed input, $$\limsup_{j\to\infty}\\|L_j(a)\\|\leq\\|a\\|,$$ and that the quotient errors in every fixed rational linearity, involution, and multiplicativity relation tend to zero as $j\to\infty$. Only bounds on each fixed input are required. Then one can choose $j(n)\to\infty$ such that $$L(a)=[(L_{j(n),n}(a))]$$ extends to a contractive homomorphism $L:S\to D$.* *The same diagonal choice can retain the following countable data.* 1. *For each $l$, fix basis opens $V_l\Subset U_l$ and row errors $e_l^{(j)}\in J(V_l)$, for all sufficiently large $j$, with chosen representatives $e_{l,n}^{(j)}$. Assume $\sup_j\\|e_l^{(j)}\\|<\infty$. Then their diagonal $e_l=[(e_{l,n}^{(j(n))})]$ belongs to $J(U_l)$.* 2. *For each prescribed comparison, let $a^{(j)},b^{(j)}\in D_+$ have uniformly bounded norms and chosen bounded positive representatives. Suppose $a^{(j)}\precsim(b^{(j)}-\delta)_+$, where $\delta>0$ is independent of $j$. If $a,b$ are the respective diagonals, then $a\precsim(b-\delta')_+$ for any preselected $0<\delta'<\delta$.* *For local errors arising from maps on $S$, it suffices to schedule dense localized inputs in these pairs $V\Subset U$. The conclusion then extends to every open $U$ and every input in $S(U)$ if the coordinate error maps are uniformly equicontinuous on bounded sets, or if their diagonals are the values of a continuous expression in the resulting quotient maps. This includes differences from fixed equicontinuous coordinate maps and continuous multilinear errors, with the other variables on countable dense lists.* *In particular, a diagonal of models is again a model when their data agree on the fixed compact tests and their enlarged versions. This also applies to models on increasing intervals with unchanged measure on these tests. Every subsequence of a model retains its model data.
  - Explanation: A row-by-row family of asymptotically multiplicative maps can be diagonalized into a homomorphism, while retaining membership in local error ideals and fixed-cut comparisons. Diagonals of models are models.
- **claim-small-stabilizers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-stabilizers)): Let $S=C_0(Y)\otimes P$, and prescribe countably many pairs $$x_l\in S_+, \qquad e_l\in J(U_{x_l}), \qquad l\in\mathbb N.$$ There is a homomorphism $\theta:S\to D$ satisfying $$\label{sm:local-and-lower} \theta(S(U))\subseteq J(U), \qquad e_l\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\theta(x_l)) \quad(U\subseteq X\text{ open},\ l\in\mathbb N),$$ in either of the following situations.* 1. *An existing model $\chi:S\to D$ is given. In this case $\theta$ can be a slowly vanishing rational UHF fraction of $\chi$, and it also satisfies $$\label{sm:coarse-fullness} \theta(S(U_x))\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\theta(x)) \qquad(x\in S_+).$$ For every fixed $N$, $N$ copies of its defining fraction fit in the defining copy of $\chi$ at all sufficiently late coordinates.* 2. *$Y=(0,1]$. No model is needed in this case, and <a href="#sm:coarse-fullness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:coarse-fullness">[sm:coarse-fullness]</a> is not asserted.* *In both cases, for every $l$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\delta_{l,\varepsilon}>0$ such that $$\label{sm:fixed-lower-cut} [ (\|e_l\|-\varepsilon)_+] \leq[(\theta(x_l)-\delta_{l,\varepsilon})_+] \quad\text{in }\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(D).$$ The construction permits the prescribed errors and the stabilizer to occupy separate matrix corners.
  - Explanation: There is a homomorphism with values in the local error ideals whose generated ideals contain a prescribed countable family of local errors, with a fixed lower cut. It exists either as a small fraction of a given model or from a cone construction without one.
- **claim-finite-cone-stage** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-cone-stage)): Let $S=C_0((0,1])\otimes P$. Fix finitely many upper tests <a href="#sm:upper-tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:upper-tests">[sm:upper-tests]</a>, finitely many lower requests <a href="#sm:error-controllers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:error-controllers">[sm:error-controllers]</a>, and a finite multiplicativity test set. For each lower request one can fix a number $h_q>0$, independently of all later stages, such that the following holds. For arbitrarily small multiplicativity error there is a c.p.c. map $\Phi:S\to M_k(Q)$, with finite constants $L_i$ and integers $M_q\geq1$, satisfying $$\label{sm:stage-bounds} \begin{split} d_\tau(\Phi(a_i))&\leq L_i d_\tau(b_i),\\ d_\tau(c_q)&\leq M_q d_\tau((\Phi(x_l)-4h_q)_+) \qquad(q=(l,\eta)), \end{split}$$ for every $\tau\in T(Q)$, with the unnormalized matrix extension of $\tau$.
  - Explanation: A finite matrix map on the cone satisfies upper rank bounds on finitely many tests and a lower rank signal at a fixed spectral cut for finitely many requests, while being multiplicative up to an arbitrarily small error.
- **claim-coefficient-domination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-coefficient-domination)): Suppose that one of the hypotheses of [sm:stabilizers](#sm:stabilizers) holds. Let $E_j$ be countably many bounded multilinear maps $$E_j:S\times Z_{j,1}\times\cdots\times Z_{j,r_j}\longrightarrow D,$$ where the parameter spaces are separable normed spaces. Assume that $$E_j(S(U),Z_{j,1},\ldots,Z_{j,r_j})\subseteq J(U) \quad\text{for every open }U.$$ Then the stabilizer may also be chosen so that, for every $x\in S_+$, $$\label{sm:all-coefficients} E_j(S(U_x),Z_{j,1},\ldots,Z_{j,r_j}) \subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\theta(x)).$$ The same conclusion holds for norm-continuous separably parametrized families of such maps, with local norm bounds, and for any choice of the localized multilinear input.
  - Explanation: A stabilizer can be chosen so that countably many bounded multilinear error maps, locally small, land in the ideal generated by its value at each element.
- **claim-norm-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-uniqueness)): Let $p,q:S_Y\to Q_\infty$ be models for the same data. For any c.p.c. lifts $p_n,q_n:S_Y\to Q$, there are $u_n\in\mathcal U(\mathcal M(Q))$ such that $$\lim_{n\to\infty}\\|u_np_n(a)u_n^*-q_n(a)\\|=0, \qquad a\in S_Y.$$
  - Explanation: Two models for the same data have coordinate lifts that are asymptotically unitarily equivalent in norm through multiplier unitaries.
- **claim-trace-cap-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-trace-cap)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra and $b\in(C\otimes\mathcal K)_+$. The set $$K_b=\{\gamma\in T(C):d_\gamma(b)\leq1\}$$ is compact and convex. If $x\in(C\otimes\mathcal K)_+$ satisfies $\sup_{\gamma\in K_b}d_\gamma(x)\leq L<\infty$, then $\gamma\mapsto\gamma(x)$ is finite, continuous, and affine on $K_b$. The constant $L$ may depend on $x$.* *Consequently this applies separately to all the first and square evaluations of a fixed completely positive candidate whose tested values have bounded rank on $K_b$. It also applies to $$\gamma\longmapsto \gamma\bigl(\lvert u x_1u^*-x_2\rvert^2\bigr), \qquad u\in\mathcal U(C^{\sim}),$$ whenever the positive elements $x_1,x_2$ have uniformly bounded ranks there, with a rank bound independent of $u$.
  - Explanation: Traces whose rank on a fixed element is at most one form a compact convex set, on which trace evaluations of elements with bounded rank are finite, continuous and affine.
- **claim-trace-cap-barycenter** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-barycenter)): Let $K_b$ be as in [un:trace-cap](#un:trace-cap). For every probability measure $\mu$ on $K_b$ there is $\bar\gamma\in K_b$ such that $$\label{un:barycenter-formula} v(\bar\gamma)=\int_{K_b}v\,d\mu$$ for every finite continuous affine real function $v$ on $K_b$. For a positive measure of finite mass $M>0$, its integral is instead $M v(\bar\gamma)$, with $\bar\gamma$ chosen for $\mu/M$; zero measures contribute zero. Signed measures may be treated by their two positive parts.
  - Explanation: Every probability measure on a trace cap has a barycenter in the cap reproducing integrals of finite continuous affine functions.
- **claim-finite-weight-alignment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-finite-traces)): Let $C$ be separable, let $S$ be separable and nuclear, and let $p,q:S\to C$ be homomorphisms with equal pullbacks on $T(C)$. For a finite list of pairs $(s_i,\gamma_i)$, where $s_i\in S_+$ and $$d_{\gamma_i}(p(s_i))+d_{\gamma_i}(q(s_i))<\infty,$$ and any $\delta>0$, there is $u\in\mathcal U(M_2(C)^{\sim})$ such that $$\label{un:finite-L2} (\gamma_i\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2) \bigl(\lvert u(p(s_i)\oplus0)u^*-(q(s_i)\oplus0)\rvert^2\bigr)<\delta \quad\text{for every }i.$$ The added matrix direction is internal and has unnormalized trace.
  - Explanation: Homomorphisms with equal pullbacks of all traces can be conjugated by a unitary so that they agree in square trace norm at finitely many weights with finite rank.
- **claim-tracial-alignment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-alignment)): If $p,q:S\to D$ are models with the same data, then rational diagonal averaging, coordinate multiplier conjugacy, and the foldings of [st:folding](#st:folding) give models $\widetilde p,\widetilde q:S\to D$ with $$\label{un:local-difference} (\widetilde p-\widetilde q)(S(U))\subseteq J(U) \qquad(U\subseteq X\text{ open}).$$ Each new model is coordinate approximately unitarily equivalent to its respective original model. The operations may be performed in one changing block while a prescribed spare block is carried along unchanged, apart from its common amplification and folding.
  - Explanation: Models with the same data can be averaged, conjugated and folded so that their difference lies in every local error ideal, staying approximately unitarily equivalent to the originals.
- **claim-ideal-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-absorption)): Let $S$ be separable, exact, and lower semicontinuous over $X$, and let $H$ be sigma-unital. Suppose $\theta:S\to H\otimes\mathcal K$ is a nuclear $X$-full homomorphism, meaning $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_{H\otimes\mathcal K}(\theta(a))=H(U_a)\otimes\mathcal K \qquad(a\in S_+).$$ Then its infinite repeat is weakly $X$-nuclear and absorbs every weakly $X$-nuclear representation on the standard module, by unitary equivalences with compact errors tending to zero on each input.
  - Explanation: The infinite repeat of a nuclear full homomorphism absorbs every weakly equivariant nuclear representation up to compact errors tending to zero on each input.
- **claim-multiplier-stable-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-stable-uniqueness)): Suppose $S$ is separable, nuclear, and lower semicontinuous over $X$, $H$ is sigma-unital, and $\theta:S\to H\otimes\mathcal K$ is an $X$-full homomorphism. Let $\phi,\psi:S\to\mathcal L(E_H)$ be weakly equivariant representations with compact difference and $[\phi,\psi]_X=0$. Writing $\theta^\infty$ for an infinite repeat, for every finite $\mathcal F\subset S$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there is a unitary $v\in1+\mathcal K(E_H\oplus E_H)$ such that $$\label{un:proper-uniqueness} \\|v(\phi\oplus\theta^\infty)(a)v^* -(\psi\oplus\theta^\infty)(a)\\|<\varepsilon, \qquad a\in\mathcal F.$$
  - Explanation: Weakly equivariant representations with compact difference and vanishing pair class become approximately unitarily equivalent after adding an infinite repeat of a full homomorphism.
- **claim-kk-nullity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-kk-nullity)): For the coarse $X$-structure on $S=C_0(Y)\otimes P$ and every sigma-unital coefficient $X$-algebra $H$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{KK}}(X;S,H)=0.$$ In particular every weakly equivariant multiplier pair with compact difference has zero class in [un:stable-uniqueness](#un:stable-uniqueness).
  - Explanation: With the coarse labels, the equivariant Kasparov group from the cone algebra to any sigma-unital coefficient algebra vanishes, because the algebra has the Razak–Jacelon factor. Every relevant pair class is therefore zero.
- **claim-hereditary-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-hereditary-support)): Let $I\subseteq C$ be a separable $C^*$-subalgebra with strictly positive element $e$, and let $k\in C_+$. Set $$H=\mathop{\mathrm{Her}}_{M_2(C)}(e\oplus k).$$ Then $H$ is sigma-unital, with no separability assertion required. If $r\in C$ idealizes $I$, then $r\oplus0$ idealizes $H$. If, in addition, $L$ is an ideal of $M_2(C)$ and $$(rI)\oplus0\subseteq L,\qquad (Ir)\oplus0\subseteq L,$$ then $$\label{un:support-bound} (r\oplus0)H+H(r\oplus0)\subseteq H\cap L.$$ For $x\in H$, one has $H\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_{M_2(C)}(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_H(x)$.
  - Explanation: The hereditary algebra generated by the two supports is sigma-unital, is idealized by the relevant multipliers, and its intersection with an ambient ideal is the ideal it generates.
- **claim-finite-truncation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-finite-truncation)): Let $H$ be sigma-unital and let $p,q:S\to\mathcal M(H)$ be representations with difference in $H$. Let $\theta:S\to H$ be a homomorphism, compact-valued on the module $H$, and form the diagonal representations $$A_0=p\oplus\theta\oplus\theta\oplus\cdots, \qquad B_0=q\oplus\theta\oplus\theta\oplus\cdots.$$ Identical zero summands may be inserted in these diagonals. If an identity-plus-compact unitary compares $A_0,B_0$ arbitrarily well on finite sets, then, for every finite $\mathcal F$ and $\varepsilon>0$, finitely many copies suffice: for some $N,M\geq0$ there is a unitary $v_N$ equal to the identity outside the initial summand, the first $N$ repeat summands, and finitely many zero slots, with $$\max_{a\in\mathcal F} \\|v_N(p\oplus\theta^{\oplus N}\oplus0^{\oplus M})(a)v_N^* -(q\oplus\theta^{\oplus N}\oplus0^{\oplus M})(a)\\|<\varepsilon.$$ All resulting errors are compact. If the representations are weakly equivariant, their compact errors on $S(U)$ belong to the compacts over $H(U)$.
  - Explanation: An identity-plus-compact conjugacy between infinite diagonal repeats can be truncated to finitely many copies and zero slots with the same finite-set accuracy, and the compact errors stay local.
- **claim-uniqueness-with-spare** (theorem, [source](#stmt-un-spare)): Let $p,q:S\to D$ be equal-data models satisfying $(p-q)(S(U))\subseteq J(U)$ for every open $U$. Let $\chi$ be a model for a strictly positive multiple of the same data. Then $p\oplus\chi$ and $q\oplus\chi$ are coordinate approximately unitarily equivalent, with the matrix and trace conventions of [st:folding](#st:folding).
  - Explanation: Two equal-data models whose difference is locally small become coordinate approximately unitarily equivalent after adding a common spare model.
- **claim-rough-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tc-rough-model)): There is a completely positive contraction $h:S\to D=Q_\infty$ with the following properties.* *For every open $U\subseteq X$, $s\in S(U)$, and $z\in S$, $$\label{tc:mixed} h(sz)-h(s)h(z),\quad h(zs)-h(z)h(s)\ \in J(U).$$ If $s=f\otimes a\in\mathcal E$ is controlled inside $U$, and $\rho$ is a regularized limit trace whose restriction $\sigma=\rho\|_Q$ is densely finite on $Q(U)$, then $$\label{tc:square} \rho(h(s)^2)=\left(\int_Y f^2\,dm\right)F(\sigma)(a^2).$$ Finally, for every $f\otimes a\in\mathcal E$ with $f\ne0$, $$\label{tc:lower-ideal} Q(U_a)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D\bigl(h(f\otimes a)\bigr).$$ The test family may be enlarged in advance by any countable family of the controlled elementary tests just described.
  - Explanation: There is a completely positive contraction into the sequence algebra that is multiplicative modulo the local error ideals, has prescribed square moments at weights densely finite on a controlling ideal, and keeps the constant ideals inside the ideal generated by its values.
- **claim-semifinite-embedding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tc-semifinite-embedding)): Let $N$ be a hyperfinite von Neumann algebra with separable predual and a faithful normal semifinite trace $T$. There is a normal injective homomorphism from $N$ into the hyperfinite factor $R_\infty$ of type $\mathrm{II}_\infty$ preserving $T$. Consequently, such an embedding exists into any prescribed infinite corner of a separably acting factor of type $\mathrm{II}_\infty$.
  - Explanation: A hyperfinite von Neumann algebra with separable predual and a faithful normal semifinite trace embeds trace-preservingly in the hyperfinite factor of type II-infinity, and into any infinite corner of a separably acting factor of that type.
- **claim-factor-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tc-factor-realization)): Let $\pi:Q\to M$ be a nondegenerate representation with weakly dense range in a separably acting factor. There is a homomorphism $l:S\to M$ with the following rank data. If $M$ is semifinite with faithful dimension trace $T$, then $$\label{tc:factor-rank} d_T(l(f\otimes a)) =m(\{f>0\})d_{F(T\circ\pi)}(a) \qquad(f,a\geq0).$$ In a type III factor the corresponding support projection is nonzero exactly when the transported ideal data prescribe a nonzero support.
  - Explanation: In a separably acting factor there is a homomorphism from the cone algebra with the prescribed rank data, treating the semifinite types and type III separately.
- **claim-measurable-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tc-measurable-realization)): Let $\pi$ be a nondegenerate separably acting representation of $Q$, and put $M=\pi(Q)''$. There is a homomorphism $l:S\to M$ whose factorwise ranks are those in [tc:factor-realization](#tc:factor-realization), on any prescribed countable elementary cut-test family. In particular, it may be chosen on the entire family $\mathcal E$ and its required cuts.
  - Explanation: The factorwise homomorphisms and the partial isometries witnessing the rank data assemble measurably over the central decomposition by measurable uniformization.
- **claim-finite-weight-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tc-finite-trace-realization)): Fix finitely many controlled tests $s_i=f_i\otimes a_i$ and finitely many traces $\tau$ for each $i$, each satisfying $d_\tau(b_i)<\infty$. Take a separably acting representation $\pi$ containing the induced trace representations of their densely finite ideals, as well as any prescribed finite family of representations for bounded functionals on $Q$. The homomorphism $l$ in [tc:measurable-realization](#tc:measurable-realization) may be chosen so that, for the induced normal trace $\widetilde\tau$ on $M=\pi(Q)''$, $$\label{tc:finite-values} \widetilde\tau(l(s_i^k)) =\left(\int f_i^k\,dm\right)F(\tau)(a_i^k),\qquad k=1,2.$$ This assertion requires no commutation of $F$ with a direct integral of extended weights.
  - Explanation: For finitely many controlled tests and finitely many traces, the measurable homomorphism can be chosen to attain the first and second moments through the induced normal traces.
- **claim-finite-convexity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tc-finite-convexity)): The target vector $$\label{tc:target-vector} \bigl((t_i^{(1)},t_i^{(2)},t_i^{(2)})_i;(y_j^2)_j\bigr)$$ belongs to the closed convex hull of the candidate vectors.
  - Explanation: The target vector of trace moments and lower norm signals lies in the closed convex hull of the candidate vectors, shown by reducing a separating functional to finitely many weights and realizing them in von Neumann algebras.
- **claim-rank-average** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tc-rank-average)): Fix lower data $y,g,\eta$ as in <a href="#tc:lower-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tc:lower-data">[tc:lower-data]</a>–<a href="#tc:fixed-slack" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tc:fixed-slack">[tc:fixed-slack]</a>. If a finite rational average satisfies $$\left\\|\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha yv_\alpha^*g(H_\alpha(s))v_\alpha y-y^2\right\\| <\varepsilon^2/2,$$ then for every extended trace on $Q$, $$\label{tc:rank-bound} d_\tau((y-\varepsilon)_+) \leq \frac{2\\|y\\|^2}{\varepsilon^2}\, d_\tau((H_{\mathrm{av}}(s)-\eta)_+).$$ The constant and the cut $\eta$ are independent of the number of candidates, their multiplicities, and their internal matrix sizes.
  - Explanation: A lower rank bound with a constant independent of the number of candidates, multiplicities and matrix sizes survives rational averaging at every extended trace.
- **claim-defect-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-defect-support)): Let $h:S\to D$ be c.p.c. and suppose that, for every open $U\subset X$, $$\label{cc:mixed-defects} h(sz)-h(s)h(z),\quad h(zs)-h(z)h(s)\in J(U) \qquad(s\in S(U),\ z\in S).$$ Let $E=C^*(h(S))$ and let $I\lhd E$ be the ideal generated by $h(ab)-h(a)h(b)$, for $a,b\in S$. Then $$\label{cc:annihilation} h(S(U))I+Ih(S(U))\subset J(U).$$ There is a locally small cone homomorphism $\theta:S\to D$ such that, after placing $h$ and $\theta$ in separate matrix corners, a $\sigma$-unital hereditary algebra $H\subset M_2(D)$ has the following properties. The first-corner map $h$ idealizes $H$, its multiplicative defects belong to $H$, and $\theta(S)\subset H$. Moreover, for every $x\in S_+$, $$\label{cc:strong-support} h(S(U_x))H+Hh(S(U_x))\subset H_x, \qquad H_x:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_H(\theta(x)).$$ Here $\theta(S(U))\subset J(U)$, with the matrix conventions of [st:folding](#st:folding).
  - Explanation: The multiplicative defects of a completely positive map that is multiplicative modulo local ideals lie in a sigma-unital hereditary algebra idealized by the map, with the product estimates holding for the entire projected ideal of each input.
- **claim-scaled-dilation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-scaled-dilation)): Let $d:S\to\mathcal L_H(H)=M(H)$ be the multiplier map induced by $h$ in [cc:defect-support](#cc:defect-support). There is a countably generated Hilbert $H$-module $\mathcal E'$ and a representation $$\label{cc:dilation} \Pi:S\longrightarrow\mathcal L_H(H\oplus\mathcal E'), \qquad \Pi(a)= \begin{pmatrix} d(a)&C(a)\\ B(a)&d'(a) \end{pmatrix},$$ whose off-diagonal entries are compact. The maps $d,d'$ are c.p.c. The module may be stabilized, retaining the first copy of $H$, so that $\mathcal E'$ is the standard countable module.* *Give $S$ its tight $\mathop{\mathrm{Prim}}(S)$-labels and give $H$ the order-preserving assignment $$\label{cc:fine-labels} H(O)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_H(\theta(S(O))) \qquad(O\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Prim}}(S)\text{ open}).$$ Let $\alpha_t$ be cone scaling, $$(\alpha_t a)(r)=a(tr),\qquad 0\le t\le1,$$ where $a(0)=0$. Every $\Pi_t:=\Pi\alpha_t$ is weakly $\mathop{\mathrm{Prim}}(S)$-nuclear for <a href="#cc:fine-labels" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cc:fine-labels">[cc:fine-labels]</a>. The same is true of its diagonal compressions $d_t,d'_t$.
  - Explanation: A module Stinespring dilation of the defect multiplier map has compact off-diagonal entries and is weakly equivariant for the finer labels, also along cone scaling.
- **claim-finite-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-finite-correction)): Under the hypotheses of [cc:defect-support](#cc:defect-support), let $\mathcal F$ be a finite subset of the unit ball of $S$ and let $\eta>0$. There are an integer $N$, a bounded linear $*$-preserving map $L:S\to M_{N+1}(M_2(D))$, and a bounded linear map $K_N:S\to M_{N+1}(H)$ such that $$\label{cc:finite-form} L(a)=h(a)\oplus\theta(a)^{\oplus N}+K_N(a),$$ and, for $a,b\in\mathcal F$, $$\label{cc:finite-estimates} \\|L(a)\\|\le\\|a\\|+\eta, \qquad \\|L(ab)-L(a)L(b)\\|\le3\eta+2\eta^2.$$ For every open $U\subset X$, $$\label{cc:finite-locality} K_N(S(U))\subset M_{N+1}(H\cap M_2(J(U))).$$ The first summand in <a href="#cc:finite-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cc:finite-form">[cc:finite-form]</a> is the original $h$, with only zero corner padding.
  - Explanation: On a finite set there is a finite matrix correction keeping the original map in the first corner, with small norm and multiplicativity errors and a correction locally small at every open set.
- **claim-cone-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-correction)): Suppose $h:S\to D$ is c.p.c. and satisfies <a href="#cc:mixed-defects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cc:mixed-defects">[cc:mixed-defects]</a>. After trace-preserving coordinate corner padding, there is a homomorphism $\lambda:S\to D$ such that $$\label{cc:corrected-difference} (\lambda-h^{\mathrm{pad}})(S(U))\subset J(U) \qquad(U\subset X\text{ open}).$$ The padding can be chosen to fix the constant copy of $Q$ in the sequence quotient. Consequently it preserves the constant ideal comparisons and the regularized trace formulas prescribed for $h$.
  - Explanation: After trace-preserving padding that fixes the constants, a map multiplicative modulo local ideals is corrected into a true homomorphism differing from it only inside the local error ideals.
- **claim-finite-ideal-determination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-finite-trace-determination)): Let $V\subset X$ be open. Suppose two lower-semicontinuous traces on $S(V)$ agree on all positive even integer powers of the rich elementary positive test supply of [tc:rough-model](#tc:rough-model), and these values are finite. If the cuts of that supply generate $S(V)$, the two traces are densely finite and equal on $S(V)$.
  - Explanation: Two lower-semicontinuous traces with equal finite even moments on a rich supply of elementary tests whose cuts generate the ideal are densely finite there and equal.
- **claim-cone-existence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-cone-existence)): Let $m$ be a finite full-support Radon measure on $(0,1]$. There is a homomorphism $p:S\to D$ such that, for every regularized limit trace $\rho$ with $\sigma=\rho\|_Q$, $$\label{cc:full-cone-formula} \rho\circ p=m\otimes F(\sigma).$$ This includes weights whose densely finite ideal is proper or zero.
  - Explanation: For a finite full-support measure on the half-open interval there is a homomorphism on the cone algebra realizing the product of the measure with the transported weight at every regularized limit, including weights with proper or zero finite ideal.
- **claim-covariant-line-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pt-suspension)): There is a model $$\lambda:C_0(\mathbb R)\otimes P\longrightarrow D$$ for Lebesgue measure and $F$. Let $\alpha(f)(t)=f(t-1)$, acting trivially on $P$. For c.p.c. coordinate lifts $\lambda_n$, there are unitaries $u_n\in M(Q)$ such that $$\label{pt:coordinate-covariance} \\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(u_n)(\lambda_n(s))-\lambda_n(\alpha(s))\\| \longrightarrow0\qquad(s\in C_0(\mathbb R)\otimes P).$$
  - Explanation: There is a model on the line for Lebesgue measure whose coordinate lifts are asymptotically covariant for unit translation through multiplier unitaries.
- **claim-corner-projection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pt-corner)): There are $\zeta\in C_c(\mathbb R)_+$ and a projection $p\in E$ such that $$\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}\zeta(t-n)^2=1, \qquad \int_\mathbb R\zeta(t)^2\,dt=1,$$ and, for any $C^*$-algebra $R_0$, any extended trace $\omega$ on $E\otimes R_0$, and every $a\in(R_0)_+$, $$\label{pt:corner-trace} \omega(p\otimes a)=\omega(\zeta^2\otimes a).$$ In particular, if the restriction of $\omega$ to $C_0(\mathbb R)\otimes R_0$ is Lebesgue measure tensored with $\nu$, then $\omega(p\otimes a)=\nu(a)$, including the value infinity. Moreover, $p$ is full and $pEp\cong C(\mathbb R/\mathbb Z)$.
  - Explanation: A partition-of-unity function gives a full projection in the crossed product whose trace equals that of the squared function. Under Lebesgue data the trace of the corner is exactly the point weight, including infinite values.
- **claim-point-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pt-point-model)): There is a homomorphism $\varphi:P\to Q_\infty$ such that $$\rho\circ\varphi=F(\rho\|_Q)$$ for every regularized limit $\rho$.
  - Explanation: Compressing the crossed-product map by the trace-normalized corner gives a homomorphism into the sequence algebra realizing the transported trace at every regularized limit.
- **claim-actual-homomorphism** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pt-actual-map)): There is a homomorphism $\phi:P\to Q$ satisfying $$\tau\circ\phi=F(\tau)\qquad(\tau\in T(Q)), \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(\phi)=G.$$ It is injective, and its constant sequence is a point model.
  - Explanation: Uniqueness makes late coordinate lifts of a point model Cauchy up to unitaries, so a conjugated subsequence converges to an injective homomorphism into the algebra itself realizing the transported trace and Cuntz data.
- **claim-trace-cone-classification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pt-intertwining)): There is an isomorphism $\Phi:P\to Q$ such that $$\tau\circ\Phi=F(\tau)\quad(\tau\in T(Q)), \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(\Phi)=G.$$ Consequently [thm:main](#thm:main) holds.
  - Explanation: A back-and-forth intertwining of the two actual homomorphisms, adjusted by unitaries using uniqueness, yields an isomorphism realizing the prescribed trace and Cuntz data. This proves the main classification.

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega$ be a free ultrafilter on $\\mathbb N$, and let $\\tau_n\\in T(Q)$ be arbitrary. Its rank functional is $$\\label{seq:regularized-rank}\n d_\\rho([(x_n)])=\n \\sup_{\\varepsilon>0}\\lim_{n\\to\\omega}d_{\\tau_n}((x_n-\\varepsilon)_+).$$ If $\\sigma=\\lim_{n\\to\\omega}\\tau_n$ in the canonical topology on $T(Q)$, then $\\rho|_Q=\\sigma$.",
          "source_id": "PM-301.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\omega$ be a free ultrafilter on $\\mathbb N$, and let $\\tau_n\\in T(Q)$ be arbitrary.       "explanation": "An ultrafilter limit of coordinate weights, regularized by positive cuts, is a lower-semicontinuous trace on the sequence algebra, with the expected rank functional and the expected restriction to the constants.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-seq-elementary-tests",
        "exact_quote": "A trace $\\theta\\in T(S_Y)$ equals $m\\otimes\\nu$ if its ranks on the preceding countable test collection satisfy <a href=\"#seq:product-rank\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"seq:product-rank\">[seq:product-rank]</a>.         "exact_quote": "Let $a,b$ be positive elements in a matrix algebra over any $C^*$-algebra, and suppose that $a\\precsim(b-\\delta)_+$, where $\\delta>0$.         "anchor": "stmt-seq-sandwich",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p:S_Y\\to D$ be a model, let $s=f\\otimes a\\geq0$, and suppose that $0<m_0:=m(f>0)<\\infty$. For $r>0$, choose $c_r\\in Q_+$ representing $rG[a]$. Then $$\\label{seq:uncut-sandwich}\n c_r\\precsim p(s)\\quad(0<r<m_0),\n \\qquad\n p(s)\\precsim c_r\\quad(r>m_0).$$ The following versions have fixed target cuts.*\n\n\n\n*If $\\eta,\\delta>0$, $0<r<m(f>\\eta)$, and $c$ represents $rG[(a-\\delta)_+]$, then, for every $0<\\kappa<\\eta\\delta$, $$\\label{seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich}\n c\\precsim(p(f\\otimes a)-\\kappa)_+.$$ If $r'>m_0$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, then a representative $b$ of $r'G[a]$ has a positive cut $(b-\\kappa)_+$ such that $$\\label{seq:upper-trimmed-sandwich}\n (p(f\\otimes a)-\\varepsilon)_+\\precsim(b-\\kappa)_+.$$ The choices in these two displays can be made independently of the model $p$. The complete collection of such comparisons on the countable compact tests characterizes the model equation.",
        "source_id": "PM-301.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-seq-sandwich",

The complete collection of such comparisons on the countable compact tests characterizes the model equation.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $p:S_Y\\to D$ be a model, let $s=f\\otimes a\\geq0$, and suppose that $0<m_0:=m(f>0)<\\infty$. For $r>0$, choose $c_r\\in Q_+$ representing $rG[a]$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-seq-all-traces",
        "exact_quote": "For a model $p$ and every $s\\in S$, $$\\label{seq:constant-ideal}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(p(s))=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U_s)).$$ Given countably many models $p_i:S\\to D$, there is a separable subalgebra $D_0\\subseteq D$ containing their ranges and the constants $Q$, such that, for every $\\gamma\\in T(D_0)$, $$\\label{seq:all-trace-model-equation}\n \\gamma\\circ p_i=m\\otimes F(\\gamma|_Q)\n \\quad\\hbox{for every }i.$$ It may also contain any prescribed countable subset of $D$.",
        "source_id": "PM-301.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-seq-all-traces",
          "exact_quote": "For a model $p$ and every $s\\in S$, $$\\label{seq:constant-ideal}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(p(s))=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U_s)).$$ Given countably many models $p_i:S\\to D$, there is a separable subalgebra $D_0\\subseteq D$ containing their ranges and the constants $Q$, such that, for every $\\gamma\\in T(D_0)$, $$\\label{seq:all-trace-model-equation}\n \\gamma\\circ p_i=m\\otimes F(\\gamma|_Q)\n \\quad\\hbox{for every }i.$$ It may also contain any prescribed countable subset of $D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-301.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For a model $p$ and every $s\\in S$, $$\\label{seq:constant-ideal}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(p(s))=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U_s)).$$ Given countably many models $p_i:S\\to D$, there is a separable subalgebra $D_0\\subseteq D$ containing their ranges and the constants $Q$, such that, for every $\\gamma\\in T(D_0)$, $$\\label{seq:all-trace-model-equation}\n \\gamma\\circ p_i=m\\otimes F(\\gamma|_Q)\n \\quad\\hbox{for every }i.$$ It may also contain any prescribed countable subset of $D$.",
      "explanation": "The ideal generated by the image of an element equals the constant ideal of its coarse label, and the model equation holds for all traces of a suitable separable subalgebra.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-seq-all-traces",
          "exact_quote": "For a model $p$",
          "source_id": "PM-301.1"

          "exact_quote": "Given countably many models $p_i:S\\to D$",
          "source_id": "PM-301.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-seq-smallideal-properties",
        "exact_quote": "The definition of $J(U)$ is independent of representatives and makes it a closed two-sided ideal of $D$. The assignment is order preserving, and $$\\label{seq:smallideal-support}\n J(U)\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U)).$$ If $\\rho$ is a regularized limit and its restriction $\\sigma=\\rho|_Q$ is densely finite on $Q(U)$, then $$\\label{seq:smallideal-vanishing}\n \\rho|_{J(U)}=0.$$ All assertions use the same conventions in finite matrix algebras.",
        "source_id": "PM-301.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-seq-smallideal-properties",
          "exact_quote": "The definition of $J(U)$ is independent of representatives and makes it a closed two-sided ideal of $D$.           "source_id": "PM-301.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The definition of $J(U)$ is independent of representatives and makes it a closed two-sided ideal of $D$. The assignment is order preserving, and $$\\label{seq:smallideal-support}\n J(U)\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U)).$$ If $\\rho$ is a regularized limit and its restriction $\\sigma=\\rho|_Q$ is densely finite on $Q(U)$, then $$\\label{seq:smallideal-vanishing}\n \\rho|_{J(U)}=0.$$ All assertions use the same conventions in finite matrix algebras.",
      "explanation": "The local error ideal is a well-defined closed two-sided ideal, monotone in the open set, contained in the ideal generated by the constants of that open set, and killed by every regularized limit that is densely finite there.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-seq-smallideal-properties",
          "exact_quote": "If $\\rho$ is a regularized limit and its restriction $\\sigma=\\rho|_Q$ is densely finite on $Q(U)$",

        }

      "id": "claim-local-error-ideal-properties",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-seq-smallideal-properties"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-seq-diagonal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A$ be a countable dense $\\mathbb Q+i\\mathbb Q$ $*$-subalgebra of $S=S_Y$, containing the localized dense sets to be tested. For each $j$, let $L_j:\\mathcal A\\to D$ be a map, and choose bounded coordinate representatives $L_{j,n}(a)$ for every $a\\in\\mathcal A$. Assume that, for each fixed input, $$\\limsup_{j\\to\\infty}\\|L_j(a)\\|\\leq\\|a\\|,$$ and that the quotient errors in every fixed rational linearity, involution, and multiplicativity relation tend to zero as $j\\to\\infty$. Only bounds on each fixed input are required. Then one can choose $j(n)\\to\\infty$ such that $$L(a)=[(L_{j(n),n}(a))]$$ extends to a contractive homomorphism $L:S\\to D$.*\n\n*The same diagonal choice can retain the following countable data.*\n\n1.  *For each $l$, fix basis opens $V_l\\Subset U_l$ and row errors $e_l^{(j)}\\in J(V_l)$, for all sufficiently large $j$, with chosen representatives $e_{l,n}^{(j)}$. Assume $\\sup_j\\|e_l^{(j)}\\|<\\infty$. Then their diagonal $e_l=[(e_{l,n}^{(j(n))})]$ belongs to $J(U_l)$.*\n\n2.  *For each prescribed comparison, let $a^{(j)},b^{(j)}\\in D_+$ have uniformly bounded norms and chosen bounded positive representatives. Suppose $a^{(j)}\\precsim(b^{(j)}-\\delta)_+$, where $\\delta>0$ is independent of $j$. If $a,b$ are the respective diagonals, then $a\\precsim(b-\\delta')_+$ for any preselected $0<\\delta'<\\delta$.*\n\n*For local errors arising from maps on $S$, it suffices to schedule dense localized inputs in these pairs $V\\Subset U$. The conclusion then extends to every open $U$ and every input in $S(U)$ if the coordinate error maps are uniformly equicontinuous on bounded sets, or if their diagonals are the values of a continuous expression in the resulting quotient maps. This includes differences from fixed equicontinuous coordinate maps and continuous multilinear errors, with the other variables on countable dense lists.*\n\n*In particular, a diagonal of models is again a model when their data agree on the fixed compact tests and their enlarged versions. This also applies to models on increasing intervals with unchanged measure on these tests. Every subsequence of a model retains its model data.",
        "source_id": "PM-301.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-seq-diagonal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A$ be a countable dense $\\mathbb Q+i\\mathbb Q$ $*$-subalgebra of $S=S_Y$, containing the localized dense sets to be tested. For each $j$, let $L_j:\\mathcal A\\to D$ be a map, and choose bounded coordinate representatives $L_{j,n}(a)$ for every $a\\in\\mathcal A$. Assume that, for each fixed input, $$\\limsup_{j\\to\\infty}\\|L_j(a)\\|\\leq\\|a\\|,$$ and that the quotient errors in every fixed rational linearity, involution, and multiplicativity relation tend to zero as $j\\to\\infty$. Only bounds on each fixed input are required. Then one can choose $j(n)\\to\\infty$ such that $$L(a)=[(L_{j(n),n}(a))]$$ extends to a contractive homomorphism $L:S\\to D$.*\n\n*The same diagonal choice can retain the following countable data.*\n\n1.  *For each $l$, fix basis opens $V_l\\Subset U_l$ and row errors $e_l^{(j)}\\in J(V_l)$, for all sufficiently large $j$, with chosen representatives $e_{l,n}^{(j)}$. Assume $\\sup_j\\|e_l^{(j)}\\|<\\infty$. Then their diagonal $e_l=[(e_{l,n}^{(j(n))})]$ belongs to $J(U_l)$.*\n\n2.  *For each prescribed comparison, let $a^{(j)},b^{(j)}\\in D_+$ have uniformly bounded norms and chosen bounded positive representatives. Suppose $a^{(j)}\\precsim(b^{(j)}-\\delta)_+$, where $\\delta>0$ is independent of $j$. If $a,b$ are the respective diagonals, then $a\\precsim(b-\\delta')_+$ for any preselected $0<\\delta'<\\delta$.*\n\n*For local errors arising from maps on $S$, it suffices to schedule dense localized inputs in these pairs $V\\Subset U$. The conclusion then extends to every open $U$ and every input in $S(U)$ if the coordinate error maps are uniformly equicontinuous on bounded sets, or if their diagonals are the values of a continuous expression in the resulting quotient maps. This includes differences from fixed equicontinuous coordinate maps and continuous multilinear errors, with the other variables on countable dense lists.*\n\n*In particular, a diagonal of models is again a model when their data agree on the fixed compact tests and their enlarged versions.       "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathcal A$ be a countable dense $\\mathbb Q+i\\mathbb Q$ $*$-subalgebra of $S=S_Y$, containing the localized dense sets to be tested. If $a,b$ are the respective diagonals, then $a\\precsim(b-\\delta')_+$ for any preselected $0<\\delta'<\\delta$.*\n\n*For local errors arising from maps on $S$, it suffices to schedule dense localized inputs in these pairs $V\\Subset U$. The conclusion then extends to every open $U$ and every input in $S(U)$ if the coordinate error maps are uniformly equicontinuous on bounded sets, or if their diagonals are the values of a continuous expression in the resulting quotient maps. This includes differences from fixed equicontinuous coordinate maps and continuous multilinear errors, with the other variables on countable dense lists.*\n\n*In particular, a diagonal of models is again a model when their data agree on the fixed compact tests and their enlarged versions. This also applies to models on increasing intervals with unchanged measure on these tests. Every subsequence of a model retains its model data.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $S=C_0((0,1])\\otimes P$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sm-coefficient-domination",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that one of the hypotheses of [sm:stabilizers](#sm:stabilizers) holds. Let $E_j$ be countably many bounded multilinear maps $$E_j:S\\times Z_{j,1}\\times\\cdots\\times Z_{j,r_j}\\longrightarrow D,$$ where the parameter spaces are separable normed spaces. Assume that $$E_j(S(U),Z_{j,1},\\ldots,Z_{j,r_j})\\subseteq J(U)\n \\quad\\text{for every open }U.$$ Then the stabilizer may also be chosen so that, for every $x\\in S_+$, $$\\label{sm:all-coefficients}\n E_j(S(U_x),Z_{j,1},\\ldots,Z_{j,r_j})\n \\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\\theta(x)).$$ The same conclusion holds for norm-continuous separably parametrized families of such maps, with local norm bounds, and for any choice of the localized multilinear input.",

        "anchor": "stmt-un-trace-cap",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra and $b\\in(C\\otimes\\mathcal K)_+$. The set $$K_b=\\{\\gamma\\in T(C):d_\\gamma(b)\\leq1\\}$$ is compact and convex. If $x\\in(C\\otimes\\mathcal K)_+$ satisfies $\\sup_{\\gamma\\in K_b}d_\\gamma(x)\\leq L<\\infty$, then $\\gamma\\mapsto\\gamma(x)$ is finite, continuous, and affine on $K_b$. The constant $L$ may depend on $x$.*\n\n*Consequently this applies separately to all the first and square evaluations of a fixed completely positive candidate whose tested values have bounded rank on $K_b$. It also applies to $$\\gamma\\longmapsto\n \\gamma\\bigl(\\lvert u x_1u^*-x_2\\rvert^2\\bigr),\n \\qquad u\\in\\mathcal U(C^{\\sim}),$$ whenever the positive elements $x_1,x_2$ have uniformly bounded ranks there, with a rank bound independent of $u$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-un-finite-traces",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be separable, let $S$ be separable and nuclear, and let $p,q:S\\to C$ be homomorphisms with equal pullbacks on $T(C)$. For a finite list of pairs $(s_i,\\gamma_i)$, where $s_i\\in S_+$ and $$d_{\\gamma_i}(p(s_i))+d_{\\gamma_i}(q(s_i))<\\infty,$$ and any $\\delta>0$, there is $u\\in\\mathcal U(M_2(C)^{\\sim})$ such that $$\\label{un:finite-L2}\n (\\gamma_i\\otimes\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_2)\n \\bigl(\\lvert u(p(s_i)\\oplus0)u^*-(q(s_i)\\oplus0)\\rvert^2\\bigr)<\\delta\n \\quad\\text{for every }i.$$ The added matrix direction is internal and has unnormalized trace.",

      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $I\\subseteq C$ be a separable $C^*$-subalgebra with strictly positive element $e$, and let $k\\in C_+$.         "anchor": "stmt-un-finite-truncation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be sigma-unital and let $p,q:S\\to\\mathcal M(H)$ be representations with difference in $H$. Let $\\theta:S\\to H$ be a homomorphism, compact-valued on the module $H$, and form the diagonal representations $$A_0=p\\oplus\\theta\\oplus\\theta\\oplus\\cdots,\n \\qquad B_0=q\\oplus\\theta\\oplus\\theta\\oplus\\cdots.$$ Identical zero summands may be inserted in these diagonals. If an identity-plus-compact unitary compares $A_0,B_0$ arbitrarily well on finite sets, then, for every finite $\\mathcal F$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, finitely many copies suffice: for some $N,M\\geq0$ there is a unitary $v_N$ equal to the identity outside the initial summand, the first $N$ repeat summands, and finitely many zero slots, with $$\\max_{a\\in\\mathcal F}\n \\|v_N(p\\oplus\\theta^{\\oplus N}\\oplus0^{\\oplus M})(a)v_N^*\n       -(q\\oplus\\theta^{\\oplus N}\\oplus0^{\\oplus M})(a)\\|<\\varepsilon.$$ All resulting errors are compact.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tc-rough-model",
        "exact_quote": "There is a completely positive contraction $h:S\\to D=Q_\\infty$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n\n\n*For every open $U\\subseteq X$, $s\\in S(U)$, and $z\\in S$, $$\\label{tc:mixed}\n h(sz)-h(s)h(z),\\quad h(zs)-h(z)h(s)\\ \\in J(U).$$ If $s=f\\otimes a\\in\\mathcal E$ is controlled inside $U$, and $\\rho$ is a regularized limit trace whose restriction $\\sigma=\\rho|_Q$ is densely finite on $Q(U)$, then $$\\label{tc:square}\n \\rho(h(s)^2)=\\left(\\int_Y f^2\\,dm\\right)F(\\sigma)(a^2).$$ Finally, for every $f\\otimes a\\in\\mathcal E$ with $f\\ne0$, $$\\label{tc:lower-ideal}\n Q(U_a)\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D\\bigl(h(f\\otimes a)\\bigr).$$ The test family may be enlarged in advance by any countable family of the controlled elementary tests just described.",
        "source_id": "PM-301.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-tc-rough-model",
          "exact_quote": "There is a completely positive contraction $h:S\\to D=Q_\\infty$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n\n\n*For every open $U\\subseteq X$, $s\\in S(U)$, and $z\\in S$, $$\\label{tc:mixed}\n h(sz)-h(s)h(z),\\quad h(zs)-h(z)h(s)\\ \\in J(U).$$ If $s=f\\otimes a\\in\\mathcal E$ is controlled inside $U$, and $\\rho$ is a regularized limit trace whose restriction $\\sigma=\\rho|_Q$ is densely finite on $Q(U)$, then $$\\label{tc:square}\n \\rho(h(s)^2)=\\left(\\int_Y f^2\\,dm\\right)F(\\sigma)(a^2).$$ Finally, for every $f\\otimes a\\in\\mathcal E$ with $f\\ne0$, $$\\label{tc:lower-ideal}\n Q(U_a)\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D\\bigl(h(f\\otimes a)\\bigr).$$ The test family may be enlarged in advance by any countable family of the controlled elementary tests just described.",
          "source_id": "PM-301.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a completely positive contraction $h:S\\to D=Q_\\infty$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n\n\n*For every open $U\\subseteq X$, $s\\in S(U)$, and $z\\in S$, $$\\label{tc:mixed}\n h(sz)-h(s)h(z),\\quad h(zs)-h(z)h(s)\\ \\in J(U).$$ If $s=f\\otimes a\\in\\mathcal E$ is controlled inside $U$, and $\\rho$ is a regularized limit trace whose restriction $\\sigma=\\rho|_Q$ is densely finite on $Q(U)$, then $$\\label{tc:square}\n \\rho(h(s)^2)=\\left(\\int_Y f^2\\,dm\\right)F(\\sigma)(a^2).$$ Finally, for every $f\\otimes a\\in\\mathcal E$ with $f\\ne0$, $$\\label{tc:lower-ideal}\n Q(U_a)\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_D\\bigl(h(f\\otimes a)\\bigr).$$ The test family may be enlarged in advance by any countable family of the controlled elementary tests just described.",
      "explanation": "There is a completely positive contraction into the sequence algebra that is multiplicative modulo the local error ideals, has prescribed square moments at weights densely finite on a controlling ideal, and keeps the constant ideals inside the ideal generated by its values.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tc-semifinite-embedding",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be a hyperfinite von Neumann algebra with separable predual and a faithful normal semifinite trace $T$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:Q\\to M$ be a nondegenerate representation with weakly dense range in a separably acting factor. There is a homomorphism $l:S\\to M$ with the following rank data.         "anchor": "stmt-tc-finite-trace-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many controlled tests $s_i=f_i\\otimes a_i$ and finitely many traces $\\tau$ for each $i$, each satisfying $d_\\tau(b_i)<\\infty$. Take a separably acting representation $\\pi$ containing the induced trace representations of their densely finite ideals, as well as any prescribed finite family of representations for bounded functionals on $Q$. The homomorphism $l$ in [tc:measurable-realization](#tc:measurable-realization) may be chosen so that, for the induced normal trace $\\widetilde\\tau$ on $M=\\pi(Q)''$, $$\\label{tc:finite-values}\n \\widetilde\\tau(l(s_i^k))\n   =\\left(\\int f_i^k\\,dm\\right)F(\\tau)(a_i^k),\\qquad k=1,2.$$ This assertion requires no commutation of $F$ with a direct integral of extended weights.",
        "source_id": "PM-301.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cc-defect-support",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h:S\\to D$ be c.p.c. and suppose that, for every open $U\\subset X$, $$\\label{cc:mixed-defects}\n h(sz)-h(s)h(z),\\quad h(zs)-h(z)h(s)\\in J(U)\n \\qquad(s\\in S(U),\\ z\\in S).$$ Let $E=C^*(h(S))$ and let $I\\lhd E$ be the ideal generated by $h(ab)-h(a)h(b)$, for $a,b\\in S$. Then $$\\label{cc:annihilation}\n h(S(U))I+Ih(S(U))\\subset J(U).$$ There is a locally small cone homomorphism $\\theta:S\\to D$ such that, after placing $h$ and $\\theta$ in separate matrix corners, a $\\sigma$-unital hereditary algebra $H\\subset M_2(D)$ has the following properties. The first-corner map $h$ idealizes $H$, its multiplicative defects belong to $H$, and $\\theta(S)\\subset H$. Moreover, for every $x\\in S_+$, $$\\label{cc:strong-support}\n h(S(U_x))H+Hh(S(U_x))\\subset H_x,\n \\qquad H_x:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_H(\\theta(x)).$$ Here $\\theta(S(U))\\subset J(U)$, with the matrix conventions of [st:folding](#st:folding).",
        "source_id": "PM-301.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cc-defect-support",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h:S\\to D$ be c.p.c. and suppose that, for every open $U\\subset X$, $$\\label{cc:mixed-defects}\n h(sz)-h(s)h(z),\\quad h(zs)-h(z)h(s)\\in J(U)\n \\qquad(s\\in S(U),\\ z\\in S).$$ Let $E=C^*(h(S))$ and let $I\\lhd E$ be the ideal generated by $h(ab)-h(a)h(b)$, for $a,b\\in S$. Then $$\\label{cc:annihilation}\n h(S(U))I+Ih(S(U))\\subset J(U).$$ There is a locally small cone homomorphism $\\theta:S\\to D$ such that, after placing $h$ and $\\theta$ in separate matrix corners, a $\\sigma$-unital hereditary algebra $H\\subset M_2(D)$ has the following properties. The first-corner map $h$ idealizes $H$, its multiplicative defects belong to $H$, and $\\theta(S)\\subset H$.         "anchor": "stmt-cc-scaled-dilation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d:S\\to\\mathcal L_H(H)=M(H)$ be the multiplier map induced by $h$ in [cc:defect-support](#cc:defect-support). There is a countably generated Hilbert $H$-module $\\mathcal E'$ and a representation $$\\label{cc:dilation}\n \\Pi:S\\longrightarrow\\mathcal L_H(H\\oplus\\mathcal E'),\n \\qquad\n \\Pi(a)=\n \\begin{pmatrix}\n d(a)&C(a)\\\\ B(a)&d'(a)\n \\end{pmatrix},$$ whose off-diagonal entries are compact. The maps $d,d'$ are c.p.c. The module may be stabilized, retaining the first copy of $H$, so that $\\mathcal E'$ is the standard countable module.*\n\n\n*Give $S$ its tight $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Prim}}(S)$-labels and give $H$ the order-preserving assignment $$\\label{cc:fine-labels}\n H(O)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_H(\\theta(S(O)))\n \\qquad(O\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{Prim}}(S)\\text{ open}).$$ Let $\\alpha_t$ be cone scaling, $$(\\alpha_t a)(r)=a(tr),\\qquad 0\\le t\\le1,$$ where $a(0)=0$. Every $\\Pi_t:=\\Pi\\alpha_t$ is weakly $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Prim}}(S)$-nuclear for <a href=\"#cc:fine-labels\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cc:fine-labels\">[cc:fine-labels]</a>. The same is true of its diagonal compressions $d_t,d'_t$.",

      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The maps $d,d'$ are c.p.c. The module may be stabilized, retaining the first copy of $H$, so that $\\mathcal E'$ is the standard countable module.*\n\n\n*Give $S$ its tight $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Prim}}(S)$-labels and give $H$ the order-preserving assignment $$\\label{cc:fine-labels}\n H(O)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_H(\\theta(S(O)))\n \\qquad(O\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{Prim}}(S)\\text{ open}).$$ Let $\\alpha_t$ be cone scaling, $$(\\alpha_t a)(r)=a(tr),\\qquad 0\\le t\\le1,$$ where $a(0)=0$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Trace cone T(C)** ([source](#intro:trace-topology)): For a $C^*$-algebra $C$, let $T(C)$ consist of all maps $\tau:C_+\to[0,\infty]$ satisfying $$\tau(0)=0,\qquad \tau(a+b)=\tau(a)+\tau(b),\qquad \tau(ra)=r\tau(a)\quad(r>0),$$ together with $\tau(x^*x)=\tau(xx^*)$ and lower semicontinuity for norm-convergent nets in $C_+$.
- **Isomorphism of topological cones** ([source](#pm-h-3)): An isomorphism of these topological cones is a homeomorphism preserving addition, the zero weight, and multiplication by every positive real scalar.
- **Rank at an extended trace** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a positive contraction $a$, its rank at an extended trace is $d_\tau(a)=\lim_k\tau(a^{1/k})$, using unnormalized matrix traces.
- **Cuntz semigroup** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a $C^*$-algebra $C$, the Cuntz semigroup $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)$ consists of Cuntz classes $[a]$ of positive elements in $C\otimes\mathcal K$, with addition by orthogonal sum.
- **Cuntz subequivalence** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The relation $a\precsim b$ means that $v_n^*bv_n\to a$ in norm for some sequence $(v_n)$; it determines the order on these classes.
- **Finite and zero ideals of a weight** ([source](#st:finite-rank-cuts)): For $\tau\in T(C)$, let $\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)$ be the closed ideal generated by the positive elements on which $\tau$ is finite, and let $\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}(\tau)$ be its zero ideal.
- **Product trace** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The product trace $m\otimes\nu$ is the integrated densely finite trace on $C_0(Y)\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\nu)$, extended to be infinity on positive elements outside this ideal.
- **Sequence algebra D** ([source](#sec:sequence-models)): Put $D=Q_\infty=\ell^\infty(Q)/c_0(Q)$, and identify $Q$ with its constant sequences.
- **Model** ([source](#stmt-seq-model)): A model for $(Y,m,F)$ is a homomorphism $p:S_Y\to D$ such that $$\label{seq:model-equation} \rho\circ p=m\otimes F(\sigma),\qquad \sigma=\rho\|_Q,$$ for every regularized limit $\rho$.
- **Local error ideal J(U)** ([source](#stmt-seq-smallideal)): For an open $U\subseteq X$, an element $x=[(x_n)]\in D$ belongs to $J(U)$ if, for every $\varepsilon>0$, there are a positive element $c$ compactly Cuntz supported in $Q(U)$, a sequence $r_n>0$ tending to zero, and an index $N$, such that $$\label{seq:smallideal-bound} d_\tau((\|x_n\|-\varepsilon)_+) \leq r_n d_\tau(c) \qquad(n\geq N,\ \tau\in T(Q)).$$ The controller $c$, the decay sequence, and $N$ may depend on $x,U,\varepsilon$. Once these have been chosen, the same bound holds for all traces and all coordinates in the tail.
- **X-structure on a coefficient algebra** ([source](#pm-h-27)): An $X$-structure on a coefficient algebra $H$ is an order-preserving assignment $U\mapsto H(U)$ of closed ideals.
- **Universal UHF algebra notation** ([source](#pm-h-10)): The universal UHF algebra is denoted by $\mathcal Q$, to distinguish it from the target algebra $Q$.
- **Compact containment of opens** ([source](#pm-h-10)): The notation $V\Subset U$ denotes compact containment in the lattice of open subsets of $X$, without a Hausdorff assumption.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-witness-norm-bound | squared norm of a comparison witness | ≤ | $\frac{\\|a\\|+\eta}{\delta}$ | accuracy eta positive, cut depth delta positive; independent of matrix size |
| q-strict-slack | strict comparison slack alpha | < | $1$ | alpha positive |
| q-lower-trim-threshold | target trimming threshold kappa | < | $\eta\delta$ | trimming parameters eta and delta positive |
| q-perturbation-distance | distance threshold for cut comparison | < | $\varepsilon/4$ | epsilon positive |
| q-stage-lower-cut | fixed spectral cut of the finite cone stage | = | $4h_q$ | lower request q with fixed positive number h_q |
| q-plateau-witness-norm | norm of the plateau witness column | ≤ | $(3h_q)^{-1/2}$ | eigenvalue exceeding the matrix signal bound |
| q-uhf-half-trace | trace of the UHF projections splitting a model | = | $1/2$ | two equivalent projections |
| q-rank-average-constant | constant in the averaged lower rank bound | = | $\frac{2\\|y\\|^2}{\varepsilon^2}$ | independent of the number of candidates and matrix sizes |
| q-square-mass-loss | loss of square trace mass under a spectral cut | ≤ | $\sqrt t\,\tau(x)$ | cut parameter t positive |
| q-finite-correction-norm | norm of the finite corrected map | ≤ | $\\|a\\|+\eta$ | inputs in a finite set |
| q-finite-correction-defect | multiplicativity defect of the finite corrected map | ≤ | $3\eta+2\eta^2$ | products of elements of a finite set |
| q-corner-mass | integral of the squared partition function | = | $1$ | function with squared integer translates summing to one |
| q-intertwining-tolerance | intertwining tolerance at step n | = | $2^{-n}$ | step index n |
| q-nuclear-dimension | nuclear dimension of the stabilization | ≤ | $1$ | separable nuclear complex algebras |
