<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-realification** [claim](#stmt-st-realification) / [proof](#stmt-st-realification): Cite compactness and realification theorems for the trace cone and transport them along the cone isomorphism. -> transported maps on traces and Cuntz classes; pointwise rank order
- **step-ideal-transport** [claim](#stmt-st-ideals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-ideals): Additive idempotents are ideal weights; scalar limits recover finite and zero ideals; primitive ideals give the homeomorphism of primitive spaces. -> common primitive ideal space X
- **step-controllers** [claim](#stmt-st-controllers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-controllers): Countable basis from cuts of a dense sequence; compact containment gives a common controller. -> fixed controllers with finite multiples
- **step-folding** [claim](#stmt-st-folding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-folding): Fold matrix amplifications by Hilbert-space unitaries and form block averages with UHF projections. -> trace-preserving folding and averaging
- **step-regularized** [claim](#stmt-seq-regularized) / [proof](#proof-stmt-seq-regularized): Pull back coordinate weights, take the ultrafilter limit in the compact cone and descend to the quotient. -> regularized limit traces and their ranks
- **step-elementary-tests** [claim](#stmt-seq-elementary-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-seq-elementary-tests): Extend the rank tests, identify the finite ideal, then compare bounded restrictions by spectral integration and Tonelli. -> model equation checked on countable tests
- **step-bounded-witness** [claim](#stmt-seq-uniform-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-seq-uniform-witness): Functional calculus on the target produces a witness with controlled norm. -> dimension-free witness bound
- **step-strict-comparison** [claim](#stmt-seq-strict-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-seq-strict-comparison): Contradiction: failed coordinate comparisons give normalized traces whose regularized limit violates the rank hypothesis. -> Cuntz comparison from rank domination
- **step-sandwich** [claim](#stmt-seq-sandwich) / [proof](#proof-stmt-seq-sandwich): Apply strict comparison with scalar slack to elementary tensors; converse via fixed-cut comparisons and elementary determination. -> cut sandwiches characterizing models
- **step-all-traces** [claim](#stmt-seq-all-traces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-seq-all-traces): Constant ideals from the sandwiches; adjoin comparison witnesses to a separable subalgebra to extend the model equation to all traces. -> constant ideals and all-trace model equation
- **step-local-error-ideal** [claim](#stmt-seq-smallideal-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-seq-smallideal-properties): Cut inequalities for sums, products and perturbations show the ideal properties; bounded controller ranks give vanishing under regularized limits. -> closed ideals J(U) killed by densely finite weights
- **step-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-seq-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-seq-diagonal): Choose fixed controllers first, then delayed coordinate thresholds so errors beat finite multiplicities; row-wise diagonal. -> diagonal homomorphisms retaining ideals and comparisons
- **step-cone-stage** [claim](#stmt-sm-cone-stage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-cone-stage): Fix lower thresholds, cover by plateau elements, use cone quasidiagonality for matrix blocks and sum the blocks. -> finite cone stage with fixed lower cut
- **step-stabilizers** [claim](#stmt-sm-stabilizers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-stabilizers): Slowly vanishing rational UHF fractions of a given model, or of finite cone stages, with delayed coordinates. -> small stabilizer dominating prescribed errors
- **step-coefficient-domination** [claim](#stmt-sm-coefficient-domination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-coefficient-domination): Countable ideal basis; apply the stabilizer theorem to the countable families of requests. -> stabilizer dominating multilinear error families
- **step-trace-cap** [claim](#stmt-un-trace-cap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-trace-cap): Lower semicontinuity of rank gives compactness; cut estimates give continuity and affineness. -> compact convex caps with continuous affine evaluations
- **step-barycenter** [claim](#stmt-un-barycenter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-barycenter): Weak approximation by finitely supported measures and a convergent subnet of finite barycenters in the compact cap. -> barycenters on caps
- **step-finite-weight-alignment** [claim](#stmt-un-finite-traces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-finite-traces): Support equivalence, normal trace representations, von Neumann uniqueness, Kaplansky density and norm equality for square trace convergence. -> unitary alignment at finitely many weights
- **step-tracial-alignment** [claim](#stmt-un-alignment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-alignment): Hahn–Banach on trace caps reduces to finitely many weights; rational convex averaging and coordinate folding give uniform cut estimates; diagonalize. -> models with locally small difference
- **step-absorption** [claim](#stmt-un-absorption) / [proof](#stmt-un-absorption): Cited absorption theorem for ideal-related representations on the stabilized module. -> absorption of weakly equivariant nuclear representations
- **step-kk-nullity** [claim](#stmt-un-kk-nullity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-kk-nullity): Tensor-product ideals split the algebra with a Razak–Jacelon factor whose identity cycle is null. -> vanishing pair classes
- **step-stable-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-un-stable-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-stable-uniqueness): Multiplier-pair theorem plus absorption of the stabilizing representation. -> stable uniqueness with compact unitary
- **step-hereditary-support** [claim](#stmt-un-hereditary-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-hereditary-support): Strictly positive element of the hereditary algebra and approximate-unit compression of ideals. -> sigma-unital hereditary support algebra
- **step-finite-truncation** [claim](#stmt-un-finite-truncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-finite-truncation): Compress the compact part to a finite corner and take the polar unitary. -> finite truncation of the conjugacy
- **step-spare-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-un-spare) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-spare): Dominate difference coefficients by a stabilizer from the spare, work on a hereditary support, apply stable uniqueness, truncate and lift to coordinates. -> norm conjugacy after adding a spare
- **step-norm-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-un-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-un-spare-2): Split each model into UHF halves, change one half at a time using alignment and the spare, and compose. -> norm uniqueness of models
- **step-semifinite-embedding** [claim](#stmt-tc-semifinite-embedding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tc-semifinite-embedding): Central decomposition, absorption of the density into the measure, and trace-preserving embeddings of abelian and factor parts. -> trace-preserving embedding into the type II-infinity factor
- **step-factor-realization** [claim](#stmt-tc-factor-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tc-factor-realization): Type-by-type construction, using hyperfiniteness of the nuclear quotient and the semifinite embedding. -> homomorphism into a factor with prescribed ranks
- **step-measurable-realization** [claim](#stmt-tc-measurable-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tc-measurable-realization): Encode the homomorphism and witness partial isometries as a Borel relation and apply measurable uniformization. -> measurable assembly over the central decomposition
- **step-finite-weight-realization** [claim](#stmt-tc-finite-trace-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tc-finite-trace-realization): Normal trace from the trace representation; equal supports evaluate rectangle ranks; monotone approximation gives moments. -> exact moments at selected weights
- **step-finite-convexity** [claim](#stmt-tc-finite-convexity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tc-finite-convexity): Separation argument reduced to finitely many weights by barycenters, then central sectors and Choi–Effros factorizations approximate the realized data. -> target vector in the closed convex hull
- **step-rank-average** [claim](#stmt-tc-rank-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tc-rank-average): Positivity and traciality in the bidual bound the support rank by the average of candidate ranks. -> averaging-stable lower rank bound
- **step-rough-model** [claim](#stmt-tc-rough-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tc-rank-average-2): Fix comparisons first, average candidates at each stage, diagonalize, then localize defects and recover square moments and lower ideal support. -> rough completely positive model with lower ideal signal
- **step-defect-support** [claim](#stmt-cc-defect-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-defect-support): Annihilation modulo local ideals, a stabilizer dominating defect coefficients, and a hereditary support algebra. -> hereditary support of the defects
- **step-scaled-dilation** [claim](#stmt-cc-scaled-dilation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-scaled-dilation): Hilbert-module Stinespring dilation and stabilization; coefficient estimates give weak equivariance along cone scaling. -> dilation respecting fine ideal labels
- **step-finite-correction** [claim](#stmt-cc-finite-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-finite-correction): Absorb a finite chain of dilations, shift summands along the chain, compare with a representation and compress the compact correction. -> finite matrix correction keeping the original corner
- **step-cone-correction** [claim](#stmt-cc-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-correction): Apply the finite correction at each stage with shrinking tolerance and diagonalize against fixed controllers. -> true homomorphism close to the rough model modulo local ideals
- **step-finite-ideal-determination** [claim](#stmt-cc-finite-trace-determination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-finite-trace-determination): Second moments give finite cut ranks; polynomial approximation and spectral integration give equality on tests, then on the whole ideal. -> equality of traces on a finite ideal
- **step-cone-existence** [claim](#stmt-cc-cone-existence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-cone-existence): Correct the rough model, add a second stabilizer to retain the lower signal, identify the trace on the finite ideal and exclude any larger finite region. -> model on the cone
- **step-line-model** [claim](#stmt-pt-suspension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pt-suspension): Cone models on exhausting intervals, diagonalized, then uniqueness gives asymptotic covariance for translation. -> covariant line model
- **step-corner** [claim](#stmt-pt-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pt-corner): Explicit projection from a partition of unity; a finite positive partition identity equates traces of the corner and of the squared function. -> full trace-normalized corner projection
- **step-point-model** [claim](#stmt-pt-point-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pt-point-model): Integrate the covariant pair to the crossed product and compress by the corner. -> point model
- **step-actual-map** [claim](#stmt-pt-actual-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pt-actual-map): Uniqueness along subsequences gives conjugated Cauchy lifts converging to an actual homomorphism; trace and Cuntz data follow by cuts. -> actual injective homomorphism
- **step-intertwining** [claim](#stmt-pt-intertwining) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pt-intertwining): Apply the actual-map result in both directions and run an approximate back-and-forth intertwining with unitary adjustments from uniqueness. -> isomorphism realizing the cone map
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pt-intertwining): Substitute the stabilizations into the final isomorphism. -> classification of stabilizations by the trace cone

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-cone-isomorphism-gives-stabilized-isomorphism**: For separable nuclear complex algebras, an isomorphism of the topological trace cones forces an isomorphism of the stabilizations by the Razak–Jacelon algebra and the compact operators. No simplicity or density assumption on the finite domains of the weights is needed.
- **inv-ideal-lattice-transport**: The cone isomorphism induces compatible maps on traces and on the Cuntz semigroup that identify the ideal lattices of the two stabilized algebras, and these identifications agree. Finite ideals and zero ideals of corresponding weights correspond.
- **inv-model-uniqueness**: Two sequence-algebra models for the same data have coordinate lifts that are conjugate by multiplier unitaries up to errors tending to zero on each input.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-nuclear-input**: Apply the classification to a C*-algebra that is not nuclear, expecting the trace cone to determine its stabilization. -> The construction relies on nuclearity of the algebras, so nothing is asserted outside that class.
- **anti-unstabilized-isomorphism**: Conclude that the algebras themselves are isomorphic from an isomorphism of their trace cones. -> The theorem only yields an isomorphism after tensoring with the Razak–Jacelon algebra and the compact operators; the K-theoretic part is removed by that tensoring, not by the cone.
- **anti-uniqueness-different-data**: Use norm uniqueness to conjugate two models that carry different trace data. -> Uniqueness is stated for models for the same data; for different data no conjugacy is provided.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves existence and uniqueness of homomorphisms through ultrafilter limits in sequence algebras, completely positive lifts and approximate intertwining. No finite procedure with explicit steps computes the isomorphism, so there is no finite constructive object to execute or test.

| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a classification theorem in pure operator algebras. Its main use is as a theoretical reference point for later classification work, not as an engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-separable-nuclear** (hypothesis): The classification theorem is stated only for separable nuclear complex algebras. The existence and uniqueness machinery uses nuclearity through completely positive lifts and approximations, and separability through countable test families. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to a non-nuclear or non-separable algebra and concluding that the trace cone determines its stabilization', 'Treating nuclearity as removable when the proof lifts maps into sequence algebras']
- **limit-stabilised-conclusion** (formal_scope): The conclusion is an isomorphism of the stabilizations by the Razak–Jacelon algebra and the compact operators, realizing the prescribed cone map. It is not an isomorphism of the original algebras. Blocked use: ['Deducing that the unstabilized algebras are isomorphic from a cone isomorphism', 'Reading a K-theoretic invariant of the original algebras off the trace cone']
- **limit-full-cone-no-density** (hypothesis): The invariant is the full cone of extended weights, including weights whose finite domain is not dense. Dropping the zero and infinity ideal weights, or restricting to densely finite traces, changes the invariant and leaves the theorem unsupported. Blocked use: ['Using a cone of densely finite traces only as the hypothesis of the theorem', 'Discarding ideal weights when comparing two trace cones']
- **limit-realification-exactness** (hypothesis): Identification of the trace cone with the cone of functionals on the Cuntz semigroup is asserted for exact algebras. The transport of Cuntz classes and rank order depends on it. Blocked use: ['Transporting Cuntz classes along a cone isomorphism for a non-exact algebra', 'Reading Cuntz order as pointwise rank order without exactness']
- **limit-uniqueness-given-models** (formal_scope): Norm uniqueness concerns two models that are given for the same data. The existence theorem is separate and is not needed in the uniqueness argument. Uniqueness says nothing about models with different data. Blocked use: ['Conjugating two models that carry different trace data', 'Citing uniqueness as evidence that a model exists']
- **limit-ultrafilter-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): Regularized limits are taken along free ultrafilters in a compact cone. Together with the diagonal choices and the measurable selection, this makes the construction non-constructive, with no explicit isomorphism, rate or finite procedure. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or a complexity bound for the isomorphism', 'Implementing the regularized limit as a computable operation on coordinate weights']
- **limit-absorption-imported** (formal_scope): Ideal-related absorption is imported from the literature and needs separability, exactness, lower semicontinuity over X, a sigma-unital coefficient algebra and an X-full nuclear homomorphism. Uniqueness uses it only under those hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Invoking absorption for a map that is not X-full', 'Invoking absorption when the coefficient algebra is not sigma-unital']
- **limit-nuclear-dimension-unused** (formal_scope): The nuclear dimension bound for the stabilizations is a separate consequence taken from another source. The classification argument does not use it, so it does not follow from the proof here. Blocked use: ["Citing this paper's argument as the proof of the nuclear dimension bound", 'Using the bound as a hypothesis of the classification steps']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 301 (`lean/docs/301.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The trace cone classifies Razak–Jacelon stabilizations](../../preprints/The-trace-cone-classifies-Razak-Jacelon-stabilizations-September-25-2026/The-trace-cone-classifies-Razak-Jacelon-stabilizations-September-25-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper classifies separable nuclear complex $C^*$-algebras after tensoring with the Razak–Jacelon algebra and the compact operators, using their cones of extended lower-semicontinuous traces. The linked formalization proves the ideal-transport part: a trace-cone equivalence induces an order isomorphism of closed two-sided ideals, carrying the ideals determined by finiteness and vanishing of each trace to those of its image.
>
> For each closed ideal $I$, it also constructs the trace that is zero on positive elements of $I$ and infinite elsewhere. These traces are exactly the additive idempotents, meaning $\tau+\tau=\tau$, and their addition recovers ideal inclusion. Spatial classification, existence of the stabilization models, norm uniqueness, and final intertwining are outside this supporting comparison.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
open Filter Topology Classical
open scoped NNReal ENNReal

namespace TraceConeClassification
variable (A : Type*) [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra A] [PartialOrder A] [StarOrderedRing A]

abbrev Positive := {a : A // 0 ≤ a}

namespace Positive
variable {A}
def zero : Positive A := ⟨0, le_rfl⟩
def add (a b : Positive A) : Positive A := ⟨a.1 + b.1, add_nonneg a.2 b.2⟩
def scale (r : ℝ≥0) (a : Positive A) : Positive A :=
  ⟨(r : ℝ) • a.1, smul_nonneg r.2 a.2⟩
def starMul (x : A) : Positive A := ⟨star x * x, star_mul_self_nonneg x⟩
def mulStar (x : A) : Positive A := ⟨x * star x, mul_star_self_nonneg x⟩
def cut (a : Positive A) (ε : ℝ≥0) : Positive A :=
  ⟨cfcₙ (fun t : ℝ => max (t - (ε : ℝ)) 0) a.1,
    cfcₙ_nonneg (fun _ _ => le_max_right _ _)⟩
end Positive

structure ExtendedTrace where
  toFun : Positive A → ℝ≥0∞
  map_zero : toFun Positive.zero = 0
  map_add : ∀ a b, toFun (Positive.add a b) = toFun a + toFun b
  map_scale : ∀ (r : ℝ≥0), 0 < r → ∀ a,
    toFun (Positive.scale r a) = (r : ℝ≥0∞) * toFun a
  tracial : ∀ x : A, toFun (Positive.starMul x) = toFun (Positive.mulStar x)
  lowerSemicontinuous : LowerSemicontinuous toFun

instance : CoeFun (ExtendedTrace A) (fun _ => Positive A → ℝ≥0∞) :=
  ⟨ExtendedTrace.toFun⟩

def CanonicalConvergence {ι : Type*} (l : Filter ι) (τ : ι → ExtendedTrace A)
    (σ : ExtendedTrace A) : Prop :=
  ∀ (a : Positive A) (ε : ℝ≥0), 0 < ε →
    Filter.limsup (fun i => τ i (Positive.cut a ε)) l ≤ σ a ∧
    σ a ≤ Filter.liminf (fun i => τ i a) l

variable {A}
namespace ExtendedTrace

def scale (r : ℝ≥0) (τ : ExtendedTrace A) : ExtendedTrace A where
  toFun a := (r : ℝ≥0∞) * τ a
  map_zero := by rw [τ.map_zero, mul_zero]
  map_add a b := by rw [τ.map_add, mul_add]
  map_scale s hs a := by rw [τ.map_scale s hs]; ac_rfl
  tracial x := by rw [τ.tracial x]
  lowerSemicontinuous :=
    (ENNReal.continuous_const_mul ENNReal.coe_ne_top).comp_lowerSemicontinuous
      τ.lowerSemicontinuous (fun _ _ h => mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left h bot_le)

end ExtendedTrace

-- The proof obligations below make the ideal and equivalence constructions well typed.
theorem closed_ideal_weight_exists
    (I : NonUnitalStarSubalgebra ℂ A) (hI : IsClosed (I : Set A))
    (hleft : ∀ (x y : A), y ∈ I → x * y ∈ I)
    (hright : ∀ (x y : A), x ∈ I → x * y ∈ I) :
    ∃ τ : ExtendedTrace A, ∀ a : Positive A, τ a = if a.1 ∈ I then 0 else ∞ := by
  sorry

namespace ExtendedTrace
variable (τ : ExtendedTrace A)
def finiteSquareSet : Set A := {x | τ (Positive.starMul x) < ∞}
lemma zero_mem_finiteSquareSet : (0 : A) ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet := by sorry
lemma star_mem_finiteSquareSet {x : A} (hx : x ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet) :
    star x ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet := by sorry
lemma smul_mem_finiteSquareSet (c : ℂ) {x : A} (hx : x ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet) :
    c • x ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet := by sorry
lemma add_mem_finiteSquareSet {x y : A}
    (hx : x ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet) (hy : y ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet) :
    x + y ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet := by sorry
lemma mul_mem_finiteSquareSet_left (x : A) {y : A} (hy : y ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet) :
    x * y ∈ τ.finiteSquareSet := by sorry

def finiteSquareAlgebra : NonUnitalStarSubalgebra ℂ A where
  carrier := τ.finiteSquareSet
  zero_mem' := τ.zero_mem_finiteSquareSet
  add_mem' := τ.add_mem_finiteSquareSet
  smul_mem' := τ.smul_mem_finiteSquareSet
  mul_mem' := fun _ hy => τ.mul_mem_finiteSquareSet_left _ hy
  star_mem' := τ.star_mem_finiteSquareSet

def finiteIdeal : NonUnitalStarSubalgebra ℂ A := τ.finiteSquareAlgebra.topologicalClosure
lemma isClosed_finiteIdeal : IsClosed (τ.finiteIdeal : Set A) := by sorry
lemma mul_mem_finiteIdeal_left (x : A) {y : A} (hy : y ∈ τ.finiteIdeal) :
    x * y ∈ τ.finiteIdeal := by sorry
lemma mul_mem_finiteIdeal_right {x : A} (hx : x ∈ τ.finiteIdeal) (y : A) :
    x * y ∈ τ.finiteIdeal := by sorry
end ExtendedTrace

end TraceConeClassification

namespace TraceConeClassification
universe u v
variable {A : Type u} [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra A] [PartialOrder A] [StarOrderedRing A]

namespace ExtendedTrace
def add (τ σ : ExtendedTrace A) : ExtendedTrace A where
  toFun a := τ a + σ a
  map_zero := by rw [τ.map_zero, σ.map_zero, add_zero]
  map_add a b := by rw [τ.map_add, σ.map_add]; ac_rfl
  map_scale r hr a := by rw [τ.map_scale r hr, σ.map_scale r hr, mul_add]
  tracial x := by rw [τ.tracial x, σ.tracial x]
  lowerSemicontinuous := τ.lowerSemicontinuous.add σ.lowerSemicontinuous

def zero : ExtendedTrace A where
  toFun _ := 0
  map_zero := rfl
  map_add _ _ := (add_zero _).symm
  map_scale _ _ _ := (mul_zero _).symm
  tracial _ := rfl
  lowerSemicontinuous := lowerSemicontinuous_const

end ExtendedTrace

def ClosedIdeal (A : Type u) [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra A] [PartialOrder A]
    [StarOrderedRing A] :=
  {I : NonUnitalStarSubalgebra ℂ A // IsClosed (I : Set A) ∧
    (∀ (x y : A), y ∈ I → x * y ∈ I) ∧ (∀ (x y : A), x ∈ I → x * y ∈ I)}

namespace ExtendedTrace

def finIdeal (τ : ExtendedTrace A) : ClosedIdeal A :=
  ⟨τ.finiteIdeal, τ.isClosed_finiteIdeal, fun x _ hy => τ.mul_mem_finiteIdeal_left x hy,
    fun _ y hx => τ.mul_mem_finiteIdeal_right hx y⟩

lemma lowerSemicontinuous_supportWeight (τ : ExtendedTrace A) :
    LowerSemicontinuous (fun a : Positive A => if τ a = 0 then (0 : ℝ≥0∞) else ∞) := by sorry

def supportWeight (τ : ExtendedTrace A) : ExtendedTrace A where
  toFun a := if τ a = 0 then 0 else ∞
  map_zero := by simp [τ.map_zero]
  map_add a b := by
    rw [τ.map_add]
    by_cases ha : τ a = 0 <;> by_cases hb : τ b = 0 <;> simp [ha, hb]
  map_scale r hr a := by
    rw [τ.map_scale r hr]
    by_cases ha : τ a = 0 <;> simp [ha, ENNReal.coe_ne_zero.mpr hr.ne']
  tracial x := by rw [τ.tracial x]
  lowerSemicontinuous := τ.lowerSemicontinuous_supportWeight

def zeroIdeal (τ : ExtendedTrace A) : ClosedIdeal A := τ.supportWeight.finIdeal
end ExtendedTrace


namespace ClosedIdeal
instance : PartialOrder (ClosedIdeal A) := inferInstanceAs (PartialOrder (Subtype _))
def weight (I : ClosedIdeal A) : ExtendedTrace A :=
  (closed_ideal_weight_exists I.1 I.2.1 I.2.2.1 I.2.2.2).choose
theorem weight_idempotent (I : ClosedIdeal A) : I.weight.add I.weight = I.weight := by sorry
theorem finiteIdeal_weight (I : ClosedIdeal A) : I.weight.finiteIdeal = I.1 := by sorry
theorem le_iff_add_weight (I J : ClosedIdeal A) :
    I ≤ J ↔ I.weight.add J.weight = I.weight := by sorry
end ClosedIdeal

namespace ExtendedTrace
theorem idempotent_eq_weight {τ : ExtendedTrace A} (hτ : τ.add τ = τ) :
    τ.finIdeal.weight = τ := by sorry
end ExtendedTrace

def IdempotentTrace (A : Type u) [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra A] [PartialOrder A]
    [StarOrderedRing A] := {τ : ExtendedTrace A // τ.add τ = τ}

def closedIdealEquivIdempotent : ClosedIdeal A ≃ IdempotentTrace A where
  toFun I := ⟨I.weight, I.weight_idempotent⟩
  invFun τ := τ.1.finIdeal
  left_inv I := Subtype.ext (ClosedIdeal.finiteIdeal_weight I)
  right_inv τ := Subtype.ext (ExtendedTrace.idempotent_eq_weight τ.2)

variable {B : Type v} [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra B] [PartialOrder B] [StarOrderedRing B]

def idempotentTraceEquiv (e : ExtendedTrace A ≃ ExtendedTrace B)
    (he : ∀ τ σ, e (τ.add σ) = (e τ).add (e σ)) : IdempotentTrace A ≃ IdempotentTrace B where
  toFun τ := ⟨e τ.1, by rw [← he, τ.2]⟩
  invFun σ := ⟨e.symm σ.1, by
    apply e.injective
    rw [he, e.apply_symm_apply, σ.2]⟩
  left_inv τ := Subtype.ext (e.symm_apply_apply τ.1)
  right_inv σ := Subtype.ext (e.apply_symm_apply σ.1)

def closedIdealEquivOfAdditive (e : ExtendedTrace A ≃ ExtendedTrace B)
    (he : ∀ τ σ, e (τ.add σ) = (e τ).add (e σ)) : ClosedIdeal A ≃ ClosedIdeal B :=
  closedIdealEquivIdempotent.trans ((idempotentTraceEquiv e he).trans
    closedIdealEquivIdempotent.symm)

lemma closedIdealEquivOfAdditive_weight (e : ExtendedTrace A ≃ ExtendedTrace B)
    (he : ∀ τ σ, e (τ.add σ) = (e τ).add (e σ)) (I : ClosedIdeal A) :
    (closedIdealEquivOfAdditive e he I).weight = e I.weight := by sorry

def closedIdealOrderEquivOfAdditive (e : ExtendedTrace A ≃ ExtendedTrace B)
    (he : ∀ τ σ, e (τ.add σ) = (e τ).add (e σ)) : ClosedIdeal A ≃o ClosedIdeal B where
  toEquiv := closedIdealEquivOfAdditive e he
  map_rel_iff' := by
    intro I J
    rw [ClosedIdeal.le_iff_add_weight, ClosedIdeal.le_iff_add_weight,
      closedIdealEquivOfAdditive_weight, closedIdealEquivOfAdditive_weight, ← he]
    exact e.injective.eq_iff

def PreservesCanonicalConvergence (f : ExtendedTrace A → ExtendedTrace B) : Prop :=
  ∀ (l : Filter (ExtendedTrace A)) (σ : ExtendedTrace A),
    CanonicalConvergence A l id σ → CanonicalConvergence B l f (f σ)

structure TraceConeEquiv (A : Type u) [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra A] [PartialOrder A]
    [StarOrderedRing A] (B : Type v) [NonUnitalCStarAlgebra B] [PartialOrder B]
    [StarOrderedRing B] where
  toEquiv : ExtendedTrace A ≃ ExtendedTrace B
  map_add : ∀ τ σ, toEquiv (τ.add σ) = (toEquiv τ).add (toEquiv σ)
  map_zero : toEquiv ExtendedTrace.zero = ExtendedTrace.zero
  map_scale : ∀ (r : ℝ≥0), 0 < r → ∀ τ, toEquiv (τ.scale r) = (toEquiv τ).scale r
  continuous_toFun : PreservesCanonicalConvergence toEquiv
  continuous_invFun : PreservesCanonicalConvergence toEquiv.symm

namespace TraceConeEquiv
variable (F : TraceConeEquiv A B)
def idealOrderIso : ClosedIdeal A ≃o ClosedIdeal B :=
  closedIdealOrderEquivOfAdditive F.toEquiv F.map_add

theorem idealOrderIso_finIdeal (τ : ExtendedTrace A) :
    F.idealOrderIso τ.finIdeal = (F.toEquiv τ).finIdeal := by
  sorry

theorem idealOrderIso_zeroIdeal (τ : ExtendedTrace A) :
    F.idealOrderIso τ.zeroIdeal = (F.toEquiv τ).zeroIdeal := by
  sorry

end TraceConeEquiv
end TraceConeClassification
end

end OAI
```

      "statement_lean": "def idempotentTraceEquiv (e : ExtendedTrace A ≃ ExtendedTrace B)\n    (he : ∀ τ σ, e (τ.add σ) = (e τ).add (e σ)) : IdempotentTrace A ≃ IdempotentTrace B where\n  toFun τ := ⟨e τ.1, by rw [← he, τ.2]⟩\n  invFun σ := ⟨e.symm σ.1, by\n    apply e.injective\n    rw [he, e.apply_symm_apply, σ.2]⟩\n  left_inv τ := Subtype.ext (e.symm_apply_apply τ.1)\n  right_inv σ := Subtype.ext (e.apply_symm_apply σ.1)"

    "family": "301",
    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [The trace cone classifies Razak–Jacelon stabilizations](../../preprints/The-trace-cone-classifies-Razak-Jacelon-stabilizations-September-25-2026/The-trace-cone-classifies-Razak-Jacelon-stabilizations-September-25-2026.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe paper classifies separable nuclear complex $C^*$-algebras after tensoring with the Razak–Jacelon algebra and the compact operators, using their cones of extended lower-semicontinuous traces. The linked formalization proves the ideal-transport part: a trace-cone equivalence induces an order isomorphism of closed two-sided ideals, carrying the ideals determined by finiteness and vanishing of each trace to those of its image.\n\nFor each closed ideal $I$, it also constructs the trace that is zero on positive elements of $I$ and infinite elsewhere. These traces are exactly the additive idempotents, meaning $\\tau+\\tau=\\tau$, and their addition recovers ideal inclusion. Spatial classification, existence of the stabilization models, norm uniqueness, and final intertwining are outside this supporting comparison."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the canonical topological cone of all extended lower-semicontinuous tracial weights determines a separable nuclear <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra after tensoring with the Razak–Jacelon algebra and the compact operators, answering Robert's trace-cone classification question positively. The isomorphism realizes the prescribed cone map, with arbitrary ideal structure and without a density assumption on the finite domains of the weights.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Razak–Jacelon algebra $\mathcal W$ is a simple, monotracial, stably projectionless nuclear $C^*$-algebra (Jacelon 2013) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Jacelon](#ref-Jacelon)]. Tensoring by $\mathcal W$ removes the $K$-theoretic part of the classification problem and makes the order on the Cuntz semigroup visible through traces. The question is whether the resulting trace data determine the algebra even when its ideal structure is unrestricted. We prove that they do.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The invariant and the main theorem

<a id="intro:ideal-weight"></a>
<a id="intro:trace-topology"></a>
For a $C^*$-algebra $C$, let $T(C)$ consist of all maps $\tau:C_+\to[0,\infty]$ satisfying $$\tau(0)=0,\qquad
 \tau(a+b)=\tau(a)+\tau(b),\qquad
 \tau(ra)=r\tau(a)\quad(r>0),$$ together with $\tau(x^*x)=\tau(xx^*)$ and lower semicontinuity for norm-convergent nets in $C_+$. Arithmetic is extended nonnegative arithmetic. We impose no density condition on the finite domain. In particular, every closed two-sided ideal $I$ contributes the weight $$\label{intro:ideal-weight}
 \tau_I(a)=
 \begin{cases}
  0,&a\in I_+,\\
  \infty,&a\in C_+\setminus I_+.
 \end{cases}$$ The canonical topology is specified by $$\label{intro:trace-topology}
 \tau_i\longrightarrow\tau
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \limsup_i\tau_i((a-\varepsilon)_+)
 \leq\tau(a)\leq\liminf_i\tau_i(a)
 \quad(a\in C_+,\ \varepsilon>0).$$ An isomorphism of these topological cones is a homeomorphism preserving addition, the zero weight, and multiplication by every positive real scalar.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $A$ and $B$ be separable nuclear complex $C^*$-algebras. If $T(A)$ and $T(B)$ are isomorphic as topological cones, then $$A\otimes\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal K\ \cong\ B\otimes\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal K,$$ where $\mathcal K=\mathcal K(\ell^2(\mathbb N))$ and all tensor products are spatial.*

</div>

The conclusion is an actual $C^*$-algebra isomorphism. The algebras may be nonunital, may have both finite and infinite subquotients, and may have an arbitrary primitive ideal space. Their only regularity assumptions are separability and nuclearity. Moreover, the isomorphism can realize the prescribed cone map: after transporting the given map to $F:T(B\otimes\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal K)\to T(A\otimes\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal K)$, we obtain an isomorphism $\Phi$ with $\tau\circ\Phi=F(\tau)$ for every extended trace $\tau$. This refinement is proved at the end of [pt:intertwining](#pt:intertwining).

The presence of <a href="#intro:ideal-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:ideal-weight">[intro:ideal-weight]</a> makes the invariant sensitive to this ideal structure. For example, $$I\subseteq J
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \tau_I+\tau_J=\tau_I.$$ The finite and zero ideals of a general weight are also encoded by the two limiting operations obtained by letting its positive scalar multiple tend to zero and to infinity. These features will let us localize each approximation on the ideal where its controlling trace is finite.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### History and significance

Razak’s classification of simple inductive limits of stably projectionless building blocks made the cone of traces, together with its scale, an effective classification invariant (Razak 2002, Theorems 1.1 and 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Razak](#ref-Razak)]. Jacelon’s construction singled out the simple monotracial algebra $\mathcal W$ in this setting (Jacelon 2013) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Jacelon](#ref-Jacelon)]. The existence, uniqueness, and approximate-intertwining organization of those classification arguments also provides the broad method used here, with new local estimates needed for arbitrary ideals.

The trace-cone question is due to Leonel Robert. It was recorded in Santiago’s 2012 conference abstract (Santiago 2012) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[SantiagoWStable](#ref-SantiagoWStable)] and appears as Problem LXVIII in the survey of Schafhauser, Tikuisis, and White (Schafhauser, Tikuisis, and White 2026) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[STW](#ref-STW)]. Their formulation retains every extended trace, including the zero/infinity ideal weights. Their May 2026 discussion describes the known simple and traceless cases and the difficulty already presented by one proper nonzero ideal. [thm:main](#thm:main) gives a positive answer to that problem for the full cone and the unrestricted class of separable nuclear algebras in its statement.

Elliott, Robert, and Santiago developed the compact topological cone of extended lower-semicontinuous traces and its relation to functionals on the Cuntz semigroup (Elliott, Robert, and Santiago 2011) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS)]. Robert subsequently identified tensoring with $\mathcal W$ with realification of that semigroup (Robert 2013, Theorem 5.1.2) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Robert](#ref-Robert)]. Thus the cone in <a href="#intro:trace-topology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:trace-topology">[intro:trace-topology]</a> determines the Cuntz semigroup of the stabilized algebra, including its ideals and positive real scalar multiplication. The remaining task is to realize this invariant by algebra maps and prove their uniqueness.

In the tracial simple case, the work of Elliott and Niu (Elliott and Niu 2016) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ElliottNiu](#ref-ElliottNiu)] and the published classification of Elliott, Gong, Lin, and Niu (Elliott et al. 2020, Theorem 7.5) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[EGLN](#ref-EGLN)] establish classification by scaled traces under the stated $KK$-contractibility and finite-nuclear-dimension hypotheses. Castillejos and Evington’s nuclear-dimension theorem for simple separable nuclear $\mathcal Z$-stable algebras supplies the regularity bridge for the simple stabilized case (Castillejos and Evington 2020, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CastillejosEvington](#ref-CastillejosEvington)]. Nawata gave another proof that $A\otimes\mathcal W\cong\mathcal W$ when $A$ is simple, separable, nuclear, has a unique tracial state, and has no unbounded densely defined traces (Nawata 2023, Corollary 6.2(i)) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Nawata](#ref-Nawata)]. These simple-algebra hypotheses are not imposed on $A$ and $B$ in [thm:main](#thm:main). As a separate regularity consequence, every stabilization $A\otimes\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal K$ in [thm:main](#thm:main) has nuclear dimension at most one, by (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[OpenAINuclearDimension](#ref-OpenAINuclearDimension)] together with $\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal Z\cong\mathcal W$ from (Jacelon 2013, Corollary 6.3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Jacelon](#ref-Jacelon)]. The classification argument below does not use this nuclear-dimension bound.

The traceless case has a different structure: when every extended trace takes only zero and infinity, the trace cone reduces to ideal information. Rørdam’s absorption criterion (Rørdam 2004, Theorem 5.2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[RordamZ](#ref-RordamZ)] and Kirchberg’s ideal-lattice classification, with a complete proof in (Gabe 2020, Theorem 6.13) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[GabeO2](#ref-GabeO2)], explain the known traceless branch. The present argument must also retain finite trace data and distinguish the regions where a weight is finite from those where it is infinite. That distinction is necessary when finite and infinite subquotients occur in the same algebra.

Restricted nonsimple precedents include Lin and Ng’s classification of certain essential extensions by $\mathcal W$, with a universal coefficient theorem assumption, a simple quotient, and an invariant containing $K$-theory and trace data (Lin and Ng 2023, Theorem 9.6) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[LinNg](#ref-LinNg)]. The general cone-only question allows arbitrary ideals.

The main analytic tools have separate roles. Ciuperca, Giordano, Ng, and Niu give unital von Neumann algebra uniqueness from support-rank data (Ciuperca et al. 2013) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CGNN](#ref-CGNN)]; we combine this with trace caps and rational averaging to obtain local rank estimates. The stable-uniqueness methods of Dadarlat and Eilers (Dadarlat and Eilers 2001) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[DadarlatEilers](#ref-DadarlatEilers)] and the ideal-related absorption and multiplier results of Gabe (Gabe 2024, secs. 11–13) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Gabe](#ref-Gabe)] remove the resulting errors in norm. Those intermediate results apply here without pure infiniteness of the auxiliary targets. In the existence argument, Connes’s hyperfiniteness theorem (Connes 1976a) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Connes](#ref-Connes)], nuclear completely positive approximation (Choi and Effros 1978) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[ChoiEffros](#ref-ChoiEffros)], and measured-field methods (Vaes and Wouters 2025) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[VaesWouters](#ref-VaesWouters)] provide the classical inputs. The fixed controllers, lower ideal-support estimates, and fine-label correction that connect these tools are proved below.

The strategy of lifting tracial data and then correcting a map through an extension has a methodological precedent in Schafhauser’s proof of the Tikuisis–White–Winter theorem (Schafhauser 2020) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Schafhauser](#ref-Schafhauser)]. His setting uses a faithful amenable trace and the universal coefficient theorem (UCT); the present local error ideals and support estimates address a different invariant, with no UCT assumption. The use of quotient cone approximations and carriers in prescribed ideals also has a precedent in Bosa–Gabe–Sims–White (Bosa et al. 2022, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[BGSW](#ref-BGSW)]. Here the small UHF fractions must satisfy both upper trace bounds and lower comparisons into fixed positive cuts. The suspension-and-corner passage follows the method in (Gabe 2020, Remark 6.4) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[GabeO2](#ref-GabeO2)] and (Gabe 2024, proof of Theorem 14.1) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Gabe](#ref-Gabe)]; its trace normalization is proved below for all extended weights.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The construction

Fix $$P=A\otimes\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal K,\qquad
 Q=B\otimes\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal K.$$ For a $C^*$-algebra $C$, the Cuntz semigroup $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)$ consists of Cuntz classes $[a]$ of positive elements in $C\otimes\mathcal K$, with addition by orthogonal sum. The relation $a\precsim b$ means that $v_n^*bv_n\to a$ in norm for some sequence $(v_n)$; it determines the order on these classes. Two elements define the same class when each is Cuntz subequivalent to the other. For a positive contraction $a$, its rank at an extended trace is $d_\tau(a)=\lim_k\tau(a^{1/k})$, using unnormalized matrix traces. This depends only on $[a]$. Two obstacles distinguish this setting from one with a single normalized trace. A positive element generating a stable algebra may have infinite rank at every nonzero densely finite trace, so making its rank a small scalar multiple gives no useful finite error bound. Also, a weight can be finite only on a proper ideal. We must control errors separately on these ideals and preserve enough lower support to retain the infinite values outside them.

<a id="intro:model"></a>
The given cone isomorphism yields compatible isomorphisms $$F:T(Q)\longrightarrow T(P),\qquad
 G:\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(P)\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q),
 \qquad
 d_\tau(G[a])=d_{F\tau}(a).$$ We identify the primitive ideal spaces through this correspondence and denote the common space by $X$. For an open $U\subseteq X$, write $P(U)$ and $Q(U)$ for the corresponding closed ideals. We first construct maps into a sequence algebra, where errors may tend to zero along the coordinates. Norm uniqueness will later allow their coordinate maps to be conjugated into convergent sequences in $Q$. Our intermediate maps take values in $$D=Q_\infty=\ell^\infty(Q)/c_0(Q).$$ We regard $Q$ as the constant sequences in $D$. If $\omega$ is a free ultrafilter on $\mathbb N$ and $\tau_n\in T(Q)$, their regularized limit on bounded positive sequences is $$\rho([(x_n)])=\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\lim_{n\to\omega}
                    \tau_n((x_n-\varepsilon)_+),\qquad x_n\geq0.$$ Section <a href="#sec:sequence-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sequence-models">3</a> proves that this defines an extended trace. We use $Y$ equal to a point, a half-open interval, or $\mathbb R$, with a full-support Radon measure $m$, finite on the interval. For $\nu\in T(P)$, write $\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\nu)$ for the closed ideal generated by the positive elements on which $\nu$ is finite. The product trace $m\otimes\nu$ is the integrated densely finite trace on $C_0(Y)\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\nu)$, extended to be infinity on positive elements outside this ideal. On positive elementary tensors it has value $(\int f\,dm)\nu(a)$, with $0\cdot\infty=0$. A *model* is a homomorphism $p:C_0(Y)\otimes P\to D$ satisfying $$\label{intro:model}
 \rho\circ p=m\otimes F(\rho|_Q)$$ for every such regularized limit $\rho$. The quantifier includes nondensely finite weights. An ideal of $C_0(Y)\otimes P$ has a fine support, an open subset of $Y\times X$; its projection to $X$ is the coarse support used to index local estimates.

The main difficulty is to preserve both the finite and the infinite parts of <a href="#intro:model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:model">[intro:model]</a>. Fix a regularized limit $\rho$, put $\sigma=\rho|_Q$, and write $Q(V)=\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\sigma)$. On $C_0(Y)\otimes P(V)$, controlled trace moments will identify the pullback of $\rho$. This alone does not prevent the pullback from becoming finite on a larger ideal. A separate lower ideal comparison rules that out. Once the finite ideal is exactly $C_0(Y)\otimes P(V)$, both sides of <a href="#intro:model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:model">[intro:model]</a> are infinite on every positive element outside it. This two-part recovery is the reason for the following constructions.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Local error ideals and stabilizers.

For each open $U\subseteq X$, we define an ideal $J(U)\subseteq D$ using uniform bounds on the ranks of positive cuts. The error at each cut is a scalar tending to zero times the rank of a fixed positive controller $c$ satisfying $c\precsim(b-\delta)_+$ for some $b\in Q(U)_+$ and $\delta>0$. Such errors are invisible to every regularized weight that is densely finite on $Q(U)$. We construct small homomorphisms whose values belong to these ideals and dominate any prescribed countable family of smaller errors. Compact containment of open ideals supplies a common controller when different approximation stages are combined. The two versions in Section <a href="#sm:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:section">4</a> have different inputs: one takes a small fraction of an existing model; the other builds a small cone homomorphism before any model is available.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Tracial approximation with lower ideal support.

Convex separation and von Neumann algebra approximation construct completely positive cone maps with the required first and second trace moments. The construction simultaneously imposes norm tests whose positive averages force lower rank comparisons. The comparison constant is independent of the averaging multiplicities. This lower signal determines the part of the cone on which a weight must remain infinite. Factor representations and their measurable assembly are used only through cut tests controlled by fixed finite-rank constants.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Correction with all ideal labels retained.

A small stabilizer places the multiplicative errors in a sigma-unital hereditary target. Module Stinespring dilation and absorption then correct the cone map. The support estimate is imposed on the entire projected ideal of each input. It therefore survives scaling of the cone variable and makes every dilation weakly equivariant for the finer primitive-space labels. Compact corrections can be compressed to finitely many terms of the original diagonal repeat, with an explicit tail estimate. A second small homomorphism then dominates the correction itself. Adding it back retains the lower ideal comparison, so the finite and infinite parts combine to give the full model equation in Section <a href="#cc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cc:section">7</a>.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### Uniqueness and passage to actual maps.

For two models with the same data, tracial alignment first makes their difference locally small. A stabilizer drawn from a spare model then allows ideal-related stable uniqueness to remove that difference in norm. The spare is supplied by splitting each model into two UHF halves and replacing the halves successively. This argument, in Section <a href="#sec:uniqueness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniqueness">5</a>, assumes that the two models are given; it does not depend on the later existence theorem. Once corrected cone models exist, an exhaustion of the line gives models with Lebesgue data. Translation covariance and a full crossed-product projection give a model for $P$ itself. A finite positive partition identity computes the trace of this corner, including infinite values. Uniqueness for every pair of subsequences gives eventual conjugacy between any two sufficiently late coordinate maps. We use these conjugacies to make a subsequence Cauchy in norm on each input; its limit is an actual homomorphism $P\to Q$. Repeating the construction in the other direction gives a map $Q\to P$. Their compositions have the identity data, so approximate intertwining proves [thm:main](#thm:main).

Figure <a href="#fig:proof-roadmap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-roadmap">1</a> displays these dependencies. In particular, the uniqueness theorem is conditional on a pair of models; it is available when the existence construction later supplies them.

<figure id="fig:proof-roadmap">

<figcaption>Principal model-construction dependencies. The existence and uniqueness arguments meet in the passage from sequence models to actual maps. Shared auxiliary inputs are not drawn separately: the trace-cap and barycenter lemmas in Section <a href="#sec:uniqueness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniqueness">5</a> are used in Section <a href="#tc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tc:section">6</a>; the absorption and hereditary-support lemmas of Section <a href="#sec:uniqueness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniqueness">5</a> are used in Section <a href="#cc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cc:section">7</a>. Norm uniqueness is a statement about models when they are given; its proof does not assume the cone-existence theorem. The dashed arrow records its further use in actual-map extraction and the final two-sided intertwining.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Organization and conventions

collect the structural input. The sequence comparisons and local diagonalization are proved in [seq:sandwich](#seq:sandwich), [seq:diagonal](#seq:diagonal). The stabilizer construction is [sm:stabilizers](#sm:stabilizers), and norm uniqueness is [un:uniqueness](#un:uniqueness). The two existence stages are [tc:rough-model](#tc:rough-model), [cc:cone-existence](#cc:cone-existence); the final passage to an isomorphism is [pt:intertwining](#pt:intertwining).

All algebras and Hilbert spaces are complex. A completely positive contractive map is abbreviated c.p.c. Traces on finite matrix amplifications use the unnormalized matrix trace. The universal UHF algebra is denoted by $\mathcal Q$, to distinguish it from the target algebra $Q$. Rational diagonal averaging uses the normalized UHF trace only in the averaging direction. The notation $V\Subset U$ denotes compact containment in the lattice of open subsets of $X$, without a Hausdorff assumption.

<a id="sec:structure"></a>
### The invariant, its ideals, and matrix conventions

The trace cone supplies three inputs to the construction: pointwise comparison of Cuntz classes in $Q$, a common lattice of ideals for $P$ and $Q$, and positive elements that control finite collections of rank estimates inside those ideals. We establish these inputs before fixing the matrix identifications used in the approximations. All later comparisons are obtained from the coordinate algebra $Q$; passage to a sequence algebra will require bounded witnesses.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Ranks and realification

For $a\in(C\otimes\mathcal K)_+$, extend $\tau\in T(C)$ to matrices by the unnormalized matrix trace and put <a id="st:rank"></a>$$\label{st:rank}
 d_\tau(a)=\lim_{k\to\infty}\tau(a^{1/k})
 \qquad(0\leq a\leq1).$$ Rescaling an element to be contractive does not change its rank. The resulting function depends only on its Cuntz class. We write $d_\tau(x)$ also for the value at $x\in\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)$. The basic cut and integration identities are <a id="st:rank-cuts"></a>$$\label{st:rank-cuts}
 d_\tau(a)=\sup_{\varepsilon>0}d_\tau((a-\varepsilon)_+),
 \qquad
 \tau(a)=\int_0^{\|a\|}d_\tau((a-t)_+)\,dt.$$ These identities use extended nonnegative arithmetic and apply to all the weights in <a href="#intro:trace-topology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:trace-topology">[intro:trace-topology]</a>.

We use the following form of the trace and realification theorems. The assertion about the functional cone is (Elliott, Robert, and Santiago 2011, Proposition 4.2, Remark 4.3, and Theorem 4.4) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS)]; compactness is (Elliott, Robert, and Santiago 2011, Theorem 3.7) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS)]. The realification assertions are (Robert 2013, Proposition 3.1.1 and Theorems 3.2.1 and 5.1.2) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Robert](#ref-Robert)].

<a id="stmt-st-realification"></a>
<div id="st:realification" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Trace realification). *For every $C^*$-algebra $C$, the trace cone with topology <a href="#intro:trace-topology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:trace-topology">[intro:trace-topology]</a> is compact Hausdorff, and it is second countable when $C$ is separable. If $C$ is exact, the assignment $\tau\mapsto d_\tau$ identifies this cone with the topological cone of functionals on $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)$.*

*For every $C^*$-algebra $C$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C\otimes\mathcal W)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)_{\mathbb R}.$$ Realification is determined functorially by the topological functional cone. Its realization as functions on that cone has pointwise order, pointwise addition, and multiplication by positive real scalars. It has the same functional cone as $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)$.*

<a id="st:transport"></a>
<a id="st:pointwise-order"></a>
*Consequently the given trace-cone isomorphism induces isomorphisms $$\label{st:transport}
 F:T(Q)\longrightarrow T(P),\qquad
 G:\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(P)\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q),\qquad
 d_\tau(Gx)=d_{F\tau}(x).$$ In particular, for $x,y\in\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q)$, $$\label{st:pointwise-order}
 x\leq y
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 d_\tau(x)\leq d_\tau(y)\quad\hbox{for every }\tau\in T(Q).$$ Multiplication by every positive real scalar is an order automorphism preserving increasing suprema and compact containment.*

</div>

Here the function realization is Robert’s cone $L(\operatorname{F}(\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C)))$, where $\operatorname{F}(\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(C))$ denotes the functional cone. For a functional cone $T$, the cone $L(T)$ consists of pointwise suprema $f=\sup_n f_n$, where the $f_n:T\to[0,\infty]$ are additive, positively homogeneous, lower semicontinuous, and zero at zero. The approximating sequence satisfies, for some $0<\varepsilon_n<1$, $$f_n\leq(1-\varepsilon_n)f_{n+1},
 \qquad f_n\text{ is continuous at every }\tau\text{ with }f_{n+1}(\tau)<\infty.$$ Continuity here is on all of $T$ at the indicated point, with the usual topology on $[0,\infty]$, rather than continuity only of a restriction to the finite locus. For the exact algebras considered below the realization is $L(T(C))$. This specified approximation condition is essential; arbitrary lower-semicontinuous homogeneous functions are not being identified with Cuntz classes. Composition with a cone homeomorphism preserves all the displayed conditions, which is the functoriality used here. This gives $G$ in <a href="#st:transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:transport">[st:transport]</a>. We fix the normalized trace of $\mathcal W$ when identifying the trace cones before and after tensoring; stabilization uses the unnormalized trace of $\mathcal K$. The equality of extended lower-semicontinuous quasitraces and traces for exact algebras is included in the cited trace theorem. Its ancestry is Haagerup’s theorem for unital exact algebras (Haagerup 2014, Theorem 5.11) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Haagerup](#ref-Haagerup)] and the Blanchard–Kirchberg reduction recalled in (Elliott, Robert, and Santiago 2011, Remark 4.3) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS)]. We use the extended nonunital interface stated there. Thus the functional cone used here is exactly the invariant of the problem.

<a id="st:W-properties"></a>
We also record the tensor-factor properties $$\label{st:W-properties}
 \mathcal W\otimes\mathcal Q\cong\mathcal W,
 \qquad
 \mathcal W\otimes\mathcal W\cong\mathcal W,
 \qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{KK}}(\mathcal W,\mathcal W)=0.$$ The first property follows from (Jacelon 2013, Proposition 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Jacelon](#ref-Jacelon)]; the remaining properties are recorded in (Nawata 2023, sec. 2.1 and Corollary 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Nawata](#ref-Nawata)]. They are properties of the tensor factor $\mathcal W$, so their use places no simplicity restriction on $A,B,P$, or $Q$. The UHF absorption is used in the foldings below, and the ideal-related use of the last equality is proved in [un:KK-nullity](#un:KK-nullity).

For completeness, the tensor-square and approximate-inner-flip properties underlying realification have accessible proofs in these same sources. Choose $\beta:\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal W\to\mathcal W$ by (Nawata 2023, Corollary 6.2(i)) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[Nawata](#ref-Nawata)]. Conjugating the flip by $\beta$ gives a trace-preserving automorphism of $\mathcal W$, which is approximately inner by (Jacelon 2013, Corollary 4.6) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[Jacelon](#ref-Jacelon)]. Pulling the implementing unitaries back through the unitized $\beta$ gives approximate inner flip of $\mathcal W\otimes\mathcal W$, as in (Jacelon 2013, Remark 5.2) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[Jacelon](#ref-Jacelon)]. Thus the tensor-square isomorphism and the approximate inner flip needed for realification follow from properties of the factor itself.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Finite ideals, zero ideals, and open supports

<a id="st:finite-rank-cuts"></a>
For $\tau\in T(C)$, let $\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)$ be the closed ideal generated by the positive elements on which $\tau$ is finite, and let $\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}(\tau)$ be its zero ideal. The restriction of $\tau$ to $\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)$ is densely finite, whereas $\tau(a)=\infty$ for $a\in C_+\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)$. A useful characterization is $$\label{st:finite-rank-cuts}
 a\in\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 d_\tau((a-\varepsilon)_+)<\infty\quad\hbox{for every }\varepsilon>0.$$ To verify the forward direction, approximate $a$ in norm by a finite-trace positive element $b$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)$. If $\|a-b\|<\varepsilon/2$, cut comparison gives $$d_\tau((a-\varepsilon)_+)
 \leq d_\tau((b-\varepsilon/2)_+)
 \leq \frac{2}{\varepsilon}\tau(b)<\infty.$$ The finite-trace positives are dense in this ideal: finite sums of terms $x^*bx$, with $\tau(b)<\infty$, still have finite trace and generate its positive cone densely. Conversely, finite rank of all positive cuts implies finite trace of those cuts, since $\tau((a-\varepsilon)_+)\leq\|a\|d_\tau((a-\varepsilon)_+)$. Their norm limit is $a$, proving <a href="#st:finite-rank-cuts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:finite-rank-cuts">[st:finite-rank-cuts]</a>.

<a id="stmt-st-ideals"></a>
<div id="st:ideals" class="proposition">

**Proposition 1** (Transport of ideals). *The maps $F,G$ identify the complete ideal lattices of $P$ and $Q$, and these identifications agree. They identify the finite and zero ideals of corresponding weights. The resulting homeomorphism of primitive ideal spaces will be used to regard $\mathop{\mathrm{Prim}}(P)=\mathop{\mathrm{Prim}}(Q)=X$. For an open $U\subseteq X$, write $P(U)$ and $Q(U)$ for the corresponding ideals. Then $$G(\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(P(U)))=\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q(U)),$$ and $$\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)=Q(U)\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(F\tau)=P(U),
 \qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}(\tau)=Q(U)\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}(F\tau)=P(U).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-st-ideals"><summary>Proof of stmt-st-ideals</summary>

*Proof.* The additive idempotents of the trace cone are exactly the ideal weights $\tau_I$. Indeed $\tau+\tau=\tau$ forces every value to be zero or infinity, and the zero set is a closed ideal. Moreover $$I\subseteq J\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \tau_I+\tau_J=\tau_I.$$ Thus $F$ determines an order isomorphism of ideal lattices. If $F(\tau_I)=\tau_J$, then $$[a]\in\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(J)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 d_{F\tau_I}(a)=0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 d_{\tau_I}(G[a])=0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 G[a]\in\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(I).$$ This proves compatibility with $G$.

The topology recovers the two other ideals through <a id="st:scalar-limits"></a>$$\label{st:scalar-limits}
 \lim_{r\downarrow0}r\tau=\tau_{\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)},
 \qquad
 \lim_{r\uparrow\infty}r\tau=\tau_{\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}(\tau)}.$$ These are (Elliott, Robert, and Santiago 2011, Equation (3.4)) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS)]. They can also be checked directly in <a href="#intro:trace-topology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:trace-topology">[intro:trace-topology]</a>: all positive cuts of an element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\tau)$ have finite trace by <a href="#st:finite-rank-cuts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:finite-rank-cuts">[st:finite-rank-cuts]</a>, whereas an element outside that ideal has infinite trace; for the second limit use whether its trace is zero or positive. Continuity and positive homogeneity of $F$ therefore preserve both limits.

An ideal-lattice isomorphism preserves prime ideals and their hull–kernel topology. In a separable $C^*$-algebra prime ideals are primitive (Dixmier 1960, Corollary 1) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[DixmierPrime](#ref-DixmierPrime)], which gives the asserted homeomorphism. Finally, the zero algebra is distinguished by the cone: every nonzero algebra has the nonzero weight $\tau_{\{0\}}$. We may henceforth assume that $P,Q\ne0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="st:compact-controller"></a>
For a positive element $c\in Q(U)$, we say that $c$ is *compactly Cuntz supported in $Q(U)$* if $$\label{st:compact-controller}
 [c]\leq[(b-\delta)_+]
 \quad\hbox{for some }b\in Q(U)_+,\ \delta>0.$$ Equivalently, $[c]$ is below an element compactly contained in a Cuntz class from $Q(U)$. This concerns the class of $c$, not compactness of a subset of a possibly non-Hausdorff space.

The next lemma permits one controller to be chosen before an approximation sequence is constructed. Its finite multiplicity may depend on the element being controlled. Later we will compensate for that multiplicity by choosing a sufficiently late coordinate.

<a id="stmt-st-controllers"></a>
<div id="st:controllers" class="lemma">

<a id="st:controller-multiple"></a>
**Lemma 1** (Countable supports and common controllers). *The open-set lattice of $X$ has a countable basis closed under finite unions such that every open $U$ is the union of basis opens $V\Subset U$. If $V\Subset U$, there is a compactly Cuntz supported $b_{V,U}\in Q(U)_+$ with $$Q(V)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_Q(b_{V,U}).$$ Every compactly Cuntz supported $c\in Q(V)_+$ then satisfies $$\label{st:controller-multiple}
 [c]\leq N[b_{V,U}]
 \quad\hbox{for some finite }N\in\mathbb N.$$ The element $b_{V,U}$ is independent of $c$; the integer $N$ need not be.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-st-controllers"><summary>Proof of stmt-st-controllers</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a norm-dense sequence $(a_j)$ of positive contractions in $Q$. The supports of $(a_j-r)_+$, for positive rational $r$, form a countable open basis, after taking finite unions. For example, to find such a support inside $U$ and containing $x\in U$, choose $a\in Q(U)_+$ nonzero in the quotient at $x$, and approximate it sufficiently closely by some $a_j$. A rational cut larger than the approximation error belongs to $Q(U)$, and a cut smaller than the quotient norm remains nonzero at $x$. The support of $(a_j-r)_+$ is compactly contained, in the ideal lattice, in the support of $(a_j-r/2)_+$. Indeed, if a directed union of ideals contains the latter element, norm approximation puts the more deeply cut element in one member of that union. Choosing two rational cut levels inside the preceding construction gives a basis open compactly contained in the prescribed $U$.

Let $h$ be strictly positive in $Q(U)$. The ideals generated by $(h-1/k)_+$ increase with dense union $Q(U)$. Compact containment gives $k$ such that $Q(V)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_Q((h-1/k)_+)$; take this cut as $b_{V,U}$. If $c$ satisfies <a href="#st:compact-controller" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:compact-controller">[st:compact-controller]</a> inside $Q(V)$, then $[c]\ll[z]$ for some $z\in Q(V)_+$. The Cuntz-semigroup ideal generated by $[b_{V,U}]$ consists of the elements below $\sup_N N[b_{V,U}]$. Thus $[z]\leq\sup_N N[b_{V,U}]$, and compact containment proves <a href="#st:controller-multiple" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:controller-multiple">[st:controller-multiple]</a>. ◻

</details>

For the domains used below, $$S_Y=C_0(Y)\otimes P,
 \qquad S_Y(U)=C_0(Y)\otimes P(U).$$ Their primitive spaces are $Y\times X$. For $s\in S_Y$, let $U_s$ be the projection to $X$ of the open support of $\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_{S_Y}(s)$. Projection is open, and <a id="st:smallest-label"></a>$$\label{st:smallest-label}
 s\in S_Y(U)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad U_s\subseteq U.$$ Thus every element has a smallest coarse label. This is the lower-semicontinuity condition on the domain ideal assignment used in absorption. The fine labels on $S_Y$, when used later, are its actual open subsets of $Y\times X$.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Folding and rational averaging

There are two matrix operations in the proof. Stabilization adds internal matrix corners, whose ranks add with no normalization. Convex averaging instead uses a separate UHF factor, whose normalized trace supplies the convex coefficients. The following identifications allow both operations to have range $Q$ while retaining this distinction and aligning any already chosen finite tests.

<a id="stmt-st-folding"></a>
<div id="st:folding" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Trace-preserving folding). *Finite matrix amplifications, including amplifications whose sizes vary with the coordinate, may be folded into $Q$ so as to preserve all trace and rank calculations with unnormalized internal matrix traces. The folding of a fixed first corner can be made arbitrarily close to the identity on any prescribed finite subset of $Q$, by a multiplier-unitary adjustment.*

<a id="st:average-traces"></a>
<a id="st:average-ranks"></a>
*If $h_1,\ldots,h_l$ are c.p.c. maps with values in finite matrix amplifications of $Q$, and $\lambda_i>0$ are rational with $\sum_i\lambda_i=1$, their normalized diagonal average is a c.p.c. map $h$ into $Q$ satisfying, for $s\geq0$, $$\begin{aligned}
 \tau(h(s))&=\sum_i\lambda_i\tau(h_i(s)),
 &\tau(h(s)^2)&=\sum_i\lambda_i\tau(h_i(s)^2),
 \label{st:average-traces}\\
 d_\tau((h(s)-\varepsilon)_+)
 &=\sum_i\lambda_i d_\tau((h_i(s)-\varepsilon)_+).
 \label{st:average-ranks}
\end{aligned}$$ The traces on the right include the unnormalized internal matrices. All equalities include zero and infinite values.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-st-folding"><summary>Proof of stmt-st-folding</summary>

*Proof.* Use <a href="#st:W-properties" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:W-properties">[st:W-properties]</a> once to exhibit $Q=C\otimes\mathcal Q\otimes\mathcal K$. For a matrix amplification choose a Hilbert-space unitary $U_k:\mathbb C^k\otimes\ell^2\to\ell^2$ and fold by $\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_{C\otimes\mathcal Q}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(U_k)$. This preserves the unnormalized matrix extension of every trace. For a finite set $E\subset Q$, choose a finite-rank projection $e$ in the last factor such that $\|a-eae\|$ is small for $a\in E$. A unitary of $B(\ell^2)$ can send the folded first-corner copy of $e\ell^2$ back to $e\ell^2$, agreeing with the specified identification there. Conjugating the folding by this unitary fixes the compressed finite tests. The two compression errors give the desired approximation on $E$.

Write $\lambda_i=l_i/L$. In an additional UHF factor choose orthogonal projections $e_i$ with normalized traces $l_i/L$ and sum one. After folding the internal matrices, form $$h(s)=\sum_i h_i(s)\otimes e_i.$$ Orthogonality makes its norm the maximum of the block norms, and functional calculus is blockwise for functions vanishing at zero. This proves complete positivity, contractivity, and <a href="#st:average-traces" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:average-traces">[st:average-traces]</a>–<a href="#st:average-ranks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:average-ranks">[st:average-ranks]</a> before the final UHF identification.

Choose that identification by interleaving tensor factors of a fixed infinite tensor-product realization of $\mathcal Q$. Finite tensor permutation unitaries identify its first-factor embedding with the identity to any prescribed accuracy on finite tests. For a finite matrix direction the trace identity on matrix units gives $\widetilde\tau(a\otimes e)=\operatorname{tr}(e)\tau(a)$, where $\tau(a)=\widetilde\tau(a\otimes1)$ and $\operatorname{tr}$ is normalized. Increasing finite matrix algebras and positive cuts give this factorization for the UHF direction, including extended weights. Thus the identification preserves the displayed formulas even when $Q$ is nonunital. Multiplier conjugation also preserves every extended trace, by the trace identity applied to $a^{1/2}u$. The choices are made separately at each coordinate; finite sets, matrix sizes, and accuracies may therefore vary with that coordinate. ◻

</details>

We always distinguish the internal matrix directions from the normalized candidate-averaging direction. In particular, an internal sector sum is not divided by its number of sectors. All subsequent padding and foldings use [st:folding](#st:folding) with the existing constant and finite test elements included among the elements to be aligned.

<a id="sec:sequence-models"></a>
### Sequence models and local error ideals

Put $D=Q_\infty=\ell^\infty(Q)/c_0(Q)$, and identify $Q$ with its constant sequences. We use square brackets for sequence classes when the distinction from coordinates matters. The two purposes of this section are to turn trace identities into uniform cut comparisons, and to retain local error bounds when approximation stages and matrix sizes vary.

<a id="pm-h-16"></a>
#### Regularized limits and their model data

<a id="stmt-seq-regularized"></a>
<div id="seq:regularized" class="proposition">

<a id="seq:regularization-formula"></a>
<a id="seq:regularized-rank"></a>
**Proposition 2** (Regularized limits). *Let $\omega$ be a free ultrafilter on $\mathbb N$, and let $\tau_n\in T(Q)$ be arbitrary. For a bounded positive sequence $(x_n)$, the formula $$\label{seq:regularization-formula}
 \rho([(x_n)])=
 \sup_{\varepsilon>0}\lim_{n\to\omega}\tau_n((x_n-\varepsilon)_+)$$ defines a lower-semicontinuous tracial weight on $D$. Its rank functional is $$\label{seq:regularized-rank}
 d_\rho([(x_n)])=
 \sup_{\varepsilon>0}\lim_{n\to\omega}d_{\tau_n}((x_n-\varepsilon)_+).$$ If $\sigma=\lim_{n\to\omega}\tau_n$ in the canonical topology on $T(Q)$, then $\rho|_Q=\sigma$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-seq-regularized"><summary>Proof of stmt-seq-regularized</summary>

*Proof.* Pull $\tau_n$ back to $\ell^\infty(Q)$ by its $n$-th coordinate homomorphism. The compact Hausdorff trace cone has a unique limit along $\omega$, say $\widetilde\rho$. For every positive $x$ in the product, the defining topology gives $$\lim_\omega\tau_n((x_n-\varepsilon)_+)
 \leq\widetilde\rho(x),\qquad
 \widetilde\rho((x-\varepsilon)_+)
 \leq\lim_\omega\tau_n((x_n-\varepsilon)_+).$$ Taking suprema over $\varepsilon$ proves <a href="#seq:regularization-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:regularization-formula">[seq:regularization-formula]</a> on the product. Every fixed cut of an element of $c_0(Q)_+$ is eventually zero, so $\widetilde\rho$ vanishes on that ideal and descends to a lower-semicontinuous trace $\rho$ on the quotient. This also proves independence of the representative. The same two inequalities for constant sequences give $\rho|_Q=\sigma$.

For completeness, rescale the representatives so that $0\leq x_n\leq1$, and let the right side of <a href="#seq:regularized-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:regularized-rank">[seq:regularized-rank]</a> be $L$. For every $k$ and $t>0$, $$\tau_n((x_n^{1/k}-t)_+)
 \leq d_{\tau_n}((x_n-t^k)_+).$$ Regularization and then the supremum over $k$ show $d_\rho(x)\leq L$. Conversely, choose a continuous function $g_\varepsilon$ with values in $[0,1]$, zero on $[0,\varepsilon/2]$, and one on $[\varepsilon,1]$. For $0<t<1$, $$\tau_n((g_\varepsilon(x_n)-t)_+)
 \geq(1-t)d_{\tau_n}((x_n-\varepsilon)_+).$$ The support of $g_\varepsilon(x)$ is below the support of $x$. Thus $d_\rho(x)\geq\rho(g_\varepsilon(x))$, and regularization followed by $t\downarrow0$ gives $d_\rho(x)\geq\lim_\omega d_{\tau_n}((x_n-\varepsilon)_+)$. Taking the supremum over $\varepsilon$ proves the reverse inequality. ◻

</details>

We call the weights in [seq:regularized](#seq:regularized)*regularized limits*. The coordinate weights need not be bounded, normalized, or densely finite. Passing to a subsequence is included by taking an ultrafilter concentrated on its indices.

<a id="seq:product-rank"></a>
Let $Y$ be a point, a half-open interval, or $\mathbb R$, with a full-support Radon measure $m$. The measure is finite on a cone interval and locally finite on $\mathbb R$. Write $S=S_Y$. For $\nu\in T(P)$, the product $m\otimes\nu$ is the integrated densely finite trace on $C_0(Y)\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\nu)$, extended to be infinity on positives outside that ideal. In particular, $$\label{seq:product-rank}
 d_{m\otimes\nu}(f\otimes a)
 =m(\{f>0\})d_\nu(a)
 \qquad(f\in C_0(Y)_+,\ a\in P_+).$$ Here a zero factor makes the product zero, including the expression $0\cdot\infty$. Full support of $m$ ensures that a nonzero positive continuous function has support of positive measure.

<a id="stmt-seq-model"></a>
<div id="seq:model" class="definition">

<a id="seq:model-equation"></a>
**Definition 1** (Model). A model for $(Y,m,F)$ is a homomorphism $p:S_Y\to D$ such that $$\label{seq:model-equation}
 \rho\circ p=m\otimes F(\sigma),\qquad \sigma=\rho|_Q,$$ for every regularized limit $\rho$.

</div>

We fix a countable collection of positive elementary tensors for testing this identity. In each basis ideal $P(V)$, choose a countable dense positive set, include its positive rational cuts, and include the less deeply cut elements needed as enlarged tests. In $C_0(Y)$, choose countable dense positive sets on a compact exhaustion and their rational cuts, with enlarged compact supports. Take all tensor products of these choices, including the zero tensor and all positive rational cuts of each chosen factor. A cut above a factor’s norm is simply zero, and its rank identity is automatic. For the line or a varying interval, a compact test and its enlarged support are chosen inside one common open interval before an exhaustion index is advanced.

<a id="stmt-seq-elementary-tests"></a>
<div id="seq:elementary-tests" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Elementary determination). *A trace $\theta\in T(S_Y)$ equals $m\otimes\nu$ if its ranks on the preceding countable test collection satisfy <a href="#seq:product-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:product-rank">[seq:product-rank]</a>. In particular, checking those ranks for every regularized limit suffices for <a href="#seq:model-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:model-equation">[seq:model-equation]</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-seq-elementary-tests"><summary>Proof of stmt-seq-elementary-tests</summary>

*Proof.* We first extend the rank identities to all elementary tensors, then identify the ideal where the weights are densely finite, and finally compare their bounded restrictions inside that ideal.

*Extension of the tests.* Suppose that positive contractions $f_j,a_i$ approximate $f,a$ within $\eta$. The perturbation cut inequalities and their tensor products give $$(f-3\eta)_+\otimes(a-3\eta)_+
 \precsim(f_j-2\eta)_+\otimes(a_i-2\eta)_+
 \precsim f\otimes a.$$ The middle tensors belong to the test collection when $\eta$ is rational; their enlarged versions give the same comparison with any fixed positive cuts of $f$ and $a$ on the left. Ranks preserve increasing suprema of such cuts. Applying these comparisons to both $\theta$ and $m\otimes\nu$, and letting $\eta\downarrow0$, proves <a href="#seq:product-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:product-rank">[seq:product-rank]</a> for every elementary tensor with compact function support. Exhaustion gives the remaining elementary tensors.

*Identification of the finite ideal.* Set $I=\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\nu)$. The finite ideal of $\theta$ is exactly $C_0(Y)\otimes I$. For one inclusion, a compactly supported function and a compactly Cuntz supported positive element of $I$ give a tensor of finite $\theta$-rank. Their ideals generate $C_0(Y)\otimes I$. For the other inclusion, let an open rectangle $V\times U$ be contained in the support of $\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\theta)$. Take nonzero $f\in C_c(V)_+$ and cut-supported $a\in P(U)_+$. Choose $f_0\in C_c(V)_+$ equal to one on $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(f)$, and $b\in P(U)_+$, $\delta>0$, with $a\precsim(b-\delta)_+$. Then $$f\otimes a\precsim(f_0\otimes b-\delta/2)_+.$$ The larger tensor belongs to $\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\theta)$, so $f\otimes a$ is compactly Cuntz supported there and has finite $\theta$-rank by <a href="#st:finite-rank-cuts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:finite-rank-cuts">[st:finite-rank-cuts]</a>. Formula <a href="#seq:product-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:product-rank">[seq:product-rank]</a> and $0<m(f>0)<\infty$ imply $d_\nu(a)<\infty$. Such elements generate $P(U)$, so $P(U)\subseteq I$. Rectangles exhaust the open support of any ideal, proving the claim.

*Equality on the finite ideal.* It remains to identify the densely finite traces on this common ideal; there is nothing to prove if $I=0$. Choose a strictly positive $h\in I$, put $e_n=(h-1/n)_+$, and choose increasing positive compactly supported functions $f_n$ whose open supports exhaust $Y$. The hereditary algebras $$H_n=\mathop{\mathrm{Her}}(f_n\otimes e_n)
     =C_0(\{f_n>0\})\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Her}}(e_n)$$ increase with dense union in $C_0(Y)\otimes I$. Both traces have finite support rank on $H_n$, because $m(f_n>0)d_\nu(e_n)<\infty$. Their restrictions are bounded and extend normally to $H_n^{**}$. For a positive elementary tensor in $H_n$, two-variable spectral integration and the already established elementary rank identities give $$\begin{aligned}
 \theta(f\otimes a)
 &=\int_0^{\|f\|}\int_0^{\|a\|}
   d_\theta((f-t)_+\otimes(a-u)_+)\,du\,dt\\
 &=\int_0^{\|f\|}\int_0^{\|a\|}
   m(f>t)d_\nu((a-u)_+)\,du\,dt
 =\Bigl(\int_Y f\,dm\Bigr)\nu(a).
\end{aligned}$$ The first integrand is the value of the normal extension on the product of the two spectral projections. Tonelli’s theorem applies to these nonnegative functions. Boundedness and algebraic tensor density now give equality of the two traces on $H_n$. Choose a positive contractive approximate unit $(v_n)$ of the common ideal with each $v_n$ in the union of the $H_j$. For every $x\geq0$ in that ideal, $v_nxv_n$ belongs to one of these hereditary algebras, and $$\theta(v_nxv_n)
 =\theta(x^{1/2}v_n^2x^{1/2})\leq\theta(x),
