 \qquad v_nxv_n\longrightarrow x.$$ Lower semicontinuity gives $\theta(x)=\lim_n\theta(v_nxv_n)$, and the same argument holds for $m\otimes\nu$. Equality on the localizations therefore gives equality on the common finite ideal. Both weights are infinity outside it. Thus they agree everywhere. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-17"></a>
#### Uniform comparison and cut sandwiches

The following elementary estimate is the source of the uniformity needed when rows of models are combined.

<a id="stmt-seq-uniform-witness"></a>
<div id="seq:uniform-witness" class="lemma">

<a id="seq:witness-bound"></a>
**Lemma 4** (Bounded witnesses into a fixed cut). *Let $a,b$ be positive elements in a matrix algebra over any $C^*$-algebra, and suppose that $a\precsim(b-\delta)_+$, where $\delta>0$. For every $\eta>0$ there is a comparison element $w$ such that $$\label{seq:witness-bound}
 \|w^*bw-a\|<\eta,
 \qquad
 \|w\|^2\leq\frac{\|a\|+\eta}{\delta}.$$ The bound is independent of the matrix size.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-seq-uniform-witness"><summary>Proof of stmt-seq-uniform-witness</summary>

*Proof.* Choose $v$ with $\|v^*(b-\delta)_+v-a\|<\eta$, and put $$g_\delta(t)=
 \begin{cases}
  \sqrt{(t-\delta)_+/t},&t>0,\\
  0,&t=0,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad w=g_\delta(b)v.$$ Then $w^*bw=v^*(b-\delta)_+v$, while $g_\delta(b)^2\leq\delta^{-1}(b-\delta)_+$. This proves <a href="#seq:witness-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:witness-bound">[seq:witness-bound]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-seq-strict-comparison"></a>
<div id="seq:strict-comparison" class="lemma">

<a id="seq:strict-rank-hypothesis"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Strict comparison from regularized limits). *Let $x,y\in D_+$. If there is $0<\alpha<1$ such that $$\label{seq:strict-rank-hypothesis}
 d_\rho(x)\leq\alpha d_\rho(y)
 \quad\hbox{for every regularized limit }\rho,$$ then $x\precsim y$. The same assertion holds after finite matrix amplification and after coordinatewise folding.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-seq-strict-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-seq-strict-comparison</summary>

<a id="seq:failed-coordinate-comparison"></a>
*Proof.* Choose bounded positive representatives $x_n,y_n$. If comparison fails, there is $\varepsilon>0$ with $(x-2\varepsilon)_+\not\precsim y$. For every fixed $\delta>0$, there must be arbitrarily late coordinates with $$\label{seq:failed-coordinate-comparison}
 (x_n-\varepsilon)_+\not\precsim(y_n-\delta)_+.$$ Otherwise [seq:uniform-witness](#seq:uniform-witness), applied for each approximation accuracy, supplies bounded sequences of witnesses showing $(x-\varepsilon)_+\precsim y$, a contradiction.

Choose increasing indices $n_k$ where <a href="#seq:failed-coordinate-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:failed-coordinate-comparison">[seq:failed-coordinate-comparison]</a> holds for $\delta=1/k$. Pointwise order in $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q)$, from <a href="#st:pointwise-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:pointwise-order">[st:pointwise-order]</a>, gives a trace whose rank on the source is strictly larger than its rank on the target. The smaller rank is finite. If the larger rank is finite, divide the trace by that rank; if it is infinite, divide by one plus the smaller rank. Thus we may choose $\tau_k$ with $$d_{\tau_k}((x_{n_k}-\varepsilon)_+)\geq1,
 \qquad
 d_{\tau_k}((y_{n_k}-1/k)_+)\leq1.$$ Take a free ultrafilter on these indices and the corresponding regularized limit. Formula <a href="#seq:regularized-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:regularized-rank">[seq:regularized-rank]</a> gives $d_\rho(x)\geq1$. For each fixed $t>0$, the cut $(y_{n_k}-t)_+$ is eventually below $(y_{n_k}-1/k)_+$, so the same formula gives $d_\rho(y)\leq1$. This contradicts <a href="#seq:strict-rank-hypothesis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:strict-rank-hypothesis">[seq:strict-rank-hypothesis]</a>. All comparisons used to select $\tau_k$ took place in $Q$. Matrix folding preserves those comparisons and ranks by [st:folding](#st:folding). ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-seq-sandwich"></a>
<div id="seq:sandwich" class="proposition">

<a id="seq:uncut-sandwich"></a>
**Proposition 3** (Sandwich comparisons). *Let $p:S_Y\to D$ be a model, let $s=f\otimes a\geq0$, and suppose that $0<m_0:=m(f>0)<\infty$. For $r>0$, choose $c_r\in Q_+$ representing $rG[a]$. Then $$\label{seq:uncut-sandwich}
 c_r\precsim p(s)\quad(0<r<m_0),
 \qquad
 p(s)\precsim c_r\quad(r>m_0).$$ The following versions have fixed target cuts.*

<a id="seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich"></a>
<a id="seq:upper-trimmed-sandwich"></a>
*If $\eta,\delta>0$, $0<r<m(f>\eta)$, and $c$ represents $rG[(a-\delta)_+]$, then, for every $0<\kappa<\eta\delta$, $$\label{seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich}
 c\precsim(p(f\otimes a)-\kappa)_+.$$ If $r'>m_0$ and $\varepsilon>0$, then a representative $b$ of $r'G[a]$ has a positive cut $(b-\kappa)_+$ such that $$\label{seq:upper-trimmed-sandwich}
 (p(f\otimes a)-\varepsilon)_+\precsim(b-\kappa)_+.$$ The choices in these two displays can be made independently of the model $p$. The complete collection of such comparisons on the countable compact tests characterizes the model equation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-seq-sandwich"><summary>Proof of stmt-seq-sandwich</summary>

*Proof.* For a regularized limit $\rho$, put $\sigma=\rho|_Q$ and $t=d_{F\sigma}(a)$. The ranks of $c_r$ and $p(s)$ are $rt$ and $m_0t$. Their ratio has the required strict scalar slack in the appropriate direction, so [seq:strict-comparison](#seq:strict-comparison) proves <a href="#seq:uncut-sandwich" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:uncut-sandwich">[seq:uncut-sandwich]</a>. This reasoning includes $t=0$ and $t=\infty$; the separating normalization in that lemma forces a finite nonzero value whenever it is needed for a contradiction.

Apply the lower comparison to $s'=(f-\eta)_+\otimes(a-\delta)_+$. Its support lies in the spectral region $f\otimes a>\eta\delta$, so elementary functional calculus gives $s'\precsim(f\otimes a-\kappa)_+$ whenever $0<\kappa<\eta\delta$. Applying $p$ proves <a href="#seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich">[seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich]</a>.

For the upper comparison, choose $m_0<r<r'$ and $\delta>0$ with $\|f\|\delta<\varepsilon/2$. The norm perturbation from $f\otimes a$ to $f\otimes(a-\delta)_+$ gives $$(p(f\otimes a)-\varepsilon)_+
 \precsim p(f\otimes(a-\delta)_+)
 \precsim c,
 \qquad [c]=rG[(a-\delta)_+].$$ Since positive scalar multiplication preserves compact containment, $$[c]\ll rG[a]\leq r'G[a]=[b].$$ There is therefore $\kappa>0$ with $[c]\leq[(b-\kappa)_+]$. This choice uses only the indicated constant classes, not $p$.

Conversely, apply any regularized rank to the fixed-cut comparisons. Let $\delta,\eta\downarrow0$ and let the rational lower and upper scalars approach $m_0$. The resulting inequalities give <a href="#seq:product-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:product-rank">[seq:product-rank]</a> for the pullback trace on every compact test. Supremum of cuts handles zero or infinite ranks, and compact exhaustion handles infinite-measure supports. [seq:elementary-tests](#seq:elementary-tests) now gives <a href="#seq:model-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:model-equation">[seq:model-equation]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-seq-all-traces"></a>
<div id="seq:all-traces" class="corollary">

<a id="seq:all-trace-model-equation"></a>
**Corollary 1** (Constant ideals and all traces). *For a model $p$ and every $s\in S$, <a id="seq:constant-ideal"></a>$$\label{seq:constant-ideal}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(p(s))=\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U_s)).$$ Given countably many models $p_i:S\to D$, there is a separable subalgebra $D_0\subseteq D$ containing their ranges and the constants $Q$, such that, for every $\gamma\in T(D_0)$, $$\label{seq:all-trace-model-equation}
 \gamma\circ p_i=m\otimes F(\gamma|_Q)
 \quad\hbox{for every }i.$$ It may also contain any prescribed countable subset of $D$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-seq-all-traces"><summary>Proof of stmt-seq-all-traces</summary>

*Proof.* For a nonzero elementary tensor of finite function support, the upper sandwiches put its image in the ideal generated by the corresponding constants. The lower trimmed sandwiches recover the ideals of all cuts of $a$, hence the whole constant ideal $Q(U_a)$. For general $s$, the ideal generated by $s$ in $S$ is generated by positive elementary tensors whose rectangle supports lie in its open support. Their projected supports exhaust $U_s$. Applying the elementary lower bounds gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U_s))\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(p(s))$. Approximating $s$ by finite sums of elementary tensors from $S(U_s)$ and applying the upper bounds gives the reverse inclusion.

For the second assertion, start with the separable algebra generated by the stated ranges, constants, and extra elements. For every countable test, every rational cut and scalar in [seq:sandwich](#seq:sandwich), and every model, adjoin the matrix entries of comparison witnesses at accuracies tending to zero. The resulting algebra $D_0$ is separable, and all these comparisons hold in $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(D_0)$, not just in $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(D)$. For $\gamma\in T(D_0)$, write $\sigma=\gamma|_Q$. The rank of a constant representing $rG[a]$ is $r d_{F\sigma}(a)$. Applying $d_\gamma$ to the retained sandwiches therefore gives the elementary rank formula for each $\gamma\circ p_i$. The proof of the converse in [seq:sandwich](#seq:sandwich) and [seq:elementary-tests](#seq:elementary-tests) applies without requiring $\gamma$ to be a regularized limit. This proves <a href="#seq:all-trace-model-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:all-trace-model-equation">[seq:all-trace-model-equation]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-18"></a>
#### The ideals of locally small elements

<a id="stmt-seq-smallideal"></a>
<div id="seq:smallideal" class="definition">

<a id="seq:smallideal-bound"></a>
**Definition 2** (Local error ideal). For an open $U\subseteq X$, an element $x=[(x_n)]\in D$ belongs to $J(U)$ if, for every $\varepsilon>0$, there are a positive element $c$ compactly Cuntz supported in $Q(U)$, a sequence $r_n>0$ tending to zero, and an index $N$, such that $$\label{seq:smallideal-bound}
 d_\tau((|x_n|-\varepsilon)_+)
 \leq r_n d_\tau(c)
 \qquad(n\geq N,\ \tau\in T(Q)).$$ The controller $c$, the decay sequence, and $N$ may depend on $x,U,\varepsilon$. Once these have been chosen, the same bound holds for all traces and all coordinates in the tail.

</div>

The scalar $r_n$ is strictly positive; consequently the right side of <a href="#seq:smallideal-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:smallideal-bound">[seq:smallideal-bound]</a> is unambiguously infinity when $d_\tau(c)=\infty$. No estimate on the uncut element is part of this definition.

For example, fix a nonzero positive contraction $a$ compactly Cuntz supported in $Q(U)$. In an additional UHF factor choose projections $e_n$ of positive rational traces $t_n\to0$. After the trace-preserving identification of [st:folding](#st:folding), the elements $x_n=a\otimes e_n$ satisfy $$\|x_n\|=\|a\|,\qquad
 d_\tau((x_n-\varepsilon)_+)
 =t_n d_\tau((a-\varepsilon)_+)\leq t_n d_\tau(a).$$ Thus $[(x_n)]$ belongs to $J(U)$ and is nonzero. This is the kind of smallness that allows a stabilizing map to retain its norm while the coefficient in its local rank bound tends to zero.

<a id="stmt-seq-smallideal-properties"></a>
<div id="seq:smallideal-properties" class="proposition">

<a id="seq:smallideal-support"></a>
**Proposition 4** (Properties of the local error ideals). *The definition of $J(U)$ is independent of representatives and makes it a closed two-sided ideal of $D$. The assignment is order preserving, and $$\label{seq:smallideal-support}
 J(U)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U)).$$ If $\rho$ is a regularized limit and its restriction $\sigma=\rho|_Q$ is densely finite on $Q(U)$, then <a id="seq:smallideal-vanishing"></a>$$\label{seq:smallideal-vanishing}
 \rho|_{J(U)}=0.$$ All assertions use the same conventions in finite matrix algebras.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-seq-smallideal-properties"><summary>Proof of stmt-seq-smallideal-properties</summary>

<a id="seq:sum-cut"></a>
<a id="seq:product-cut"></a>
<a id="seq:perturbation-cut"></a>
*Proof.* We recall the cut estimates that will be used. For bounded operators in a $C^*$-algebra and every tracial rank, the following weaker forms of the spectral cut inequalities suffice: $$\begin{aligned}
 d_\tau((|x+y|-\varepsilon)_+)
 &\leq d_\tau((|x|-\varepsilon/4)_+)
       +d_\tau((|y|-\varepsilon/4)_+),
 \label{seq:sum-cut}\\
 d_\tau((|bx|-\varepsilon)_+),\ d_\tau((|xb|-\varepsilon)_+)
 &\leq d_\tau((|x|-\varepsilon/(2M))_+)
 \quad(\|b\|\leq M, M>0).
 \label{seq:product-cut}
\end{aligned}$$ Also $d_\tau((|x^*|-t)_+)=d_\tau((|x|-t)_+)$, and $$\label{seq:perturbation-cut}
 \|x-y\|<\varepsilon/4
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 d_\tau((|x|-\varepsilon)_+)
 \leq d_\tau((|y|-\varepsilon/2)_+).$$ Here is an explicit cut reduction. Set $$x_t=x f_t(|x|),\qquad
 f_t(s)=\begin{cases}(s-t)_+/s,&s>0,\\0,&s=0.\end{cases}$$ Then $\|x-x_t\|\leq t$ and $|x_t|=(|x|-t)_+$. Use the perturbation comparison $\|z-w\|<\varepsilon\Rightarrow(|z|-\varepsilon)_+\precsim|w|$. For the sum take $w=x_{\varepsilon/4}+y_{\varepsilon/4}$, and use $|w|\precsim|x_{\varepsilon/4}|\oplus|y_{\varepsilon/4}|$. For a product take $w=bx_{\varepsilon/(2M)}$, whose absolute value is Cuntz below $|x_{\varepsilon/(2M)}|$. For <a href="#seq:perturbation-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:perturbation-cut">[seq:perturbation-cut]</a> take $w=y_{\varepsilon/2}$, so that $\|x-w\|<3\varepsilon/4$. The identities for adjoints give the other product order. These comparisons follow from the positive cut perturbation lemma and $[v^*v]=[vv^*]$; applying the additive rank functional proves the displays for extended weights as well.

If $x,y\in J(U)$, choose their controllers at tolerance $\varepsilon/4$. A folded finite block sum of the controllers is compactly Cuntz supported in $Q(U)$. Taking the maximum of the two decay sequences proves the bound for $x+y$ by <a href="#seq:sum-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:sum-cut">[seq:sum-cut]</a>. Equation <a href="#seq:product-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:product-cut">[seq:product-cut]</a> proves closure under multiplication on either side by an arbitrary bounded sequence. The equality for adjoints proves self-adjointness. Equation <a href="#seq:perturbation-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:perturbation-cut">[seq:perturbation-cut]</a> proves independence of the representative. For norm closure, first fix $\varepsilon$, then choose one element of the approximating sequence within $\varepsilon/8$ in quotient norm. Its representatives are eventually within $\varepsilon/4$, so its controller at tolerance $\varepsilon/2$ works for the desired output cut. Monotonicity in $U$ follows directly from the definition.

To prove <a href="#seq:smallideal-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:smallideal-support">[seq:smallideal-support]</a>, fix a cut of $|x|$, choose its controller $c\precsim(b-\delta)_+$ in $Q(U)$, and wait until $r_n\leq1$. Pointwise order in $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q)$ gives $(|x_n|-\varepsilon)_+\precsim c\precsim(b-\delta)_+$. The bounded witnesses of [seq:uniform-witness](#seq:uniform-witness) give comparison with the constant $b$ in the quotient. Every positive cut of $|x|$ is consequently in $\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U))$, and so is $x$.

<a id="seq:controller-trace-bound"></a>
For vanishing, fix $\varepsilon$ and the same type of controller. For each coordinate weight, $$\label{seq:controller-trace-bound}
 d_{\tau_n}(c)
 \leq\frac4\delta\,
       \tau_n((b-3\delta/4)_+).$$ Indeed, on the spectral region $b>\delta$, the function $(b-3\delta/4)_+$ is at least $\delta/4$. Put $h=(b-\delta/2)_+$. Dense finiteness on $Q(U)$ and <a href="#st:finite-rank-cuts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="st:finite-rank-cuts">[st:finite-rank-cuts]</a> give $\sigma(h)<\infty$, while the trace-cone convergence inequalities give $$\lim_\omega\tau_n((b-3\delta/4)_+)
 =\lim_\omega\tau_n((h-\delta/4)_+)
 \leq\sigma(h).$$ The ranks in <a href="#seq:controller-trace-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:controller-trace-bound">[seq:controller-trace-bound]</a> are therefore bounded on an $\omega$-large set. Multiplying by $r_n\to0$ in <a href="#seq:smallideal-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:smallideal-bound">[seq:smallideal-bound]</a> and using <a href="#seq:regularized-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:regularized-rank">[seq:regularized-rank]</a> yields $d_\rho(|x|)=0$. Hence $\rho$ vanishes on $J(U)_+$. The proof uses only finite matrix sums and unnormalized ranks, so it also proves the matrix assertions, with foldings as in [st:folding](#st:folding). ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-19"></a>
#### A diagonalization with fixed controllers

The diagonalizations used below retain two different kinds of control. A fixed positive controller bounds a local error’s rank, while a fixed positive target cut bounds the norm of a comparison witness. Both must be chosen before advancing through the approximation stages. We call each stage’s sequence of coordinate approximations a *row*. Its finite matrix size may depend on the row and on the coordinate; all matrices are folded by [st:folding](#st:folding).

<a id="stmt-seq-diagonal"></a>
<div id="seq:diagonal" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (Local diagonalization). *Let $\mathcal A$ be a countable dense $\mathbb Q+i\mathbb Q$ $*$-subalgebra of $S=S_Y$, containing the localized dense sets to be tested. For each $j$, let $L_j:\mathcal A\to D$ be a map, and choose bounded coordinate representatives $L_{j,n}(a)$ for every $a\in\mathcal A$. Assume that, for each fixed input, $$\limsup_{j\to\infty}\|L_j(a)\|\leq\|a\|,$$ and that the quotient errors in every fixed rational linearity, involution, and multiplicativity relation tend to zero as $j\to\infty$. Only bounds on each fixed input are required. Then one can choose $j(n)\to\infty$ such that $$L(a)=[(L_{j(n),n}(a))]$$ extends to a contractive homomorphism $L:S\to D$.*

*The same diagonal choice can retain the following countable data.*

1.  *For each $l$, fix basis opens $V_l\Subset U_l$ and row errors $e_l^{(j)}\in J(V_l)$, for all sufficiently large $j$, with chosen representatives $e_{l,n}^{(j)}$. Assume $\sup_j\|e_l^{(j)}\|<\infty$. Then their diagonal $e_l=[(e_{l,n}^{(j(n))})]$ belongs to $J(U_l)$.*

2.  *For each prescribed comparison, let $a^{(j)},b^{(j)}\in D_+$ have uniformly bounded norms and chosen bounded positive representatives. Suppose $a^{(j)}\precsim(b^{(j)}-\delta)_+$, where $\delta>0$ is independent of $j$. If $a,b$ are the respective diagonals, then $a\precsim(b-\delta')_+$ for any preselected $0<\delta'<\delta$.*

*For local errors arising from maps on $S$, it suffices to schedule dense localized inputs in these pairs $V\Subset U$. The conclusion then extends to every open $U$ and every input in $S(U)$ if the coordinate error maps are uniformly equicontinuous on bounded sets, or if their diagonals are the values of a continuous expression in the resulting quotient maps. This includes differences from fixed equicontinuous coordinate maps and continuous multilinear errors, with the other variables on countable dense lists.*

*In particular, a diagonal of models is again a model when their data agree on the fixed compact tests and their enlarged versions. This also applies to models on increasing intervals with unchanged measure on these tests. Every subsequence of a model retains its model data.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-seq-diagonal"><summary>Proof of stmt-seq-diagonal</summary>

*Proof.* *The common controller and the coordinate choice.* Enumerate all algebraic relations and positive rational cut tests. Reindex the rows so that row $j$ includes the first $j$ requirements and their quotient norm errors tend to zero. Include the bounds on each prescribed error and comparison element among these requirements. Set any as yet unspecified early-row errors to zero. In the following construction let $l$ enumerate pairs consisting of a prescribed local error and a positive rational tolerance $\varepsilon_l$; repeat that error’s opens and representatives with each tolerance. Choose once and for all the controller $b_l=b_{V_l,U_l}$ from [st:controllers](#st:controllers).

In row $j$, the $l$-th error has, after folding, a bound $$d_\tau((|e^{(j)}_{l,n}|-\varepsilon_l)_+)
 \leq r^{(j)}_{l,n}d_\tau(c^{(j)}_l),
 \qquad r^{(j)}_{l,n}\longrightarrow0,$$ uniformly for $\tau\in T(Q)$ eventually in $n$. The controller $c^{(j)}_l$ is compactly Cuntz supported in $Q(V_l)$. There is therefore a finite integer $N_{j,l}$ with $$[c^{(j)}_l]\leq N_{j,l}[b_l].$$ This integer absorbs internal matrix sizes, repeated blocks, and any other finite multiplicity at that stage. No bound on it as $j\to\infty$ is required.

<a id="seq:diagonal-delay"></a>
Fix a sequence $\eta_j\downarrow0$. After the controllers and the integers $N_{j,l}$ have been chosen for $l\leq j$, choose strictly increasing coordinate thresholds $n_j\geq j$ so large that $$\label{seq:diagonal-delay}
 N_{j,l}r^{(j)}_{l,n}\leq\eta_j
 \qquad(n\geq n_j,\ l\leq j).$$ At the same threshold impose the first $j$ norm and algebraic requirements, with an additional coordinate error at most $\eta_j$. This is possible because a quotient norm is the limsup of the coordinate norms. Choose representatives for the countable values; linearity of those representatives is unnecessary, since the linearity relations themselves are among the tests.

For $n_j\leq n<n_{j+1}$, put $j(n)=j$ and use row $j$. The diagonal norm bounds make each prescribed value a bounded sequence. For any fixed local test $l$, <a href="#seq:diagonal-delay" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:diagonal-delay">[seq:diagonal-delay]</a> then gives, eventually, $$d_\tau((|e_{l,n}|-\varepsilon_l)_+)
 \leq\eta_{j(n)}d_\tau(b_l),
 \qquad j(n)\longrightarrow\infty.$$ The controller on the right is fixed, so this is precisely the required $J(U_l)$ bound. Enumerating all rational cut tolerances proves membership in that ideal. The diagonal norm requirements give $\|L(a)\|\leq\|a\|$, and all algebraic errors vanish. Thus the quotient values extend uniquely to a contractive homomorphism on $S$.

*Extension of local errors.* For an arbitrary open $U$, the ideals $S(V)$, with basis opens $V\Subset U'\subseteq U$, have dense union in $S(U)$. Indeed they generate $S(U)$ as a closed sum, and finite sums already lie in one member: replace finitely many pairs $V_i\Subset U'_i$ by their finite unions, using the basis from [st:controllers](#st:controllers). Thus the union of the scheduled localized dense sets is dense in $S(U)$. Each retained error on those sets lies in $J(U')\subseteq J(U)$. Closedness of $J(U)$ and the assumed continuity extend this containment to every localized input. For errors with several variables, approximate each variable on its scheduled dense set, using the global lists for unrestricted variables. The continuity used here is either uniform equicontinuity of the coordinate errors or continuity of their expression in the completed quotient maps. Continuity of each row separately would not suffice.

*Comparison witnesses with a fixed target cut.* Suppose a row comparison has uniformly bounded source $a^{(j)}$ and uniformly bounded $b^{(j)}$, with target $(b^{(j)}-\delta)_+$ and $\delta>0$ independent of $j$. Include both source and target norms in the coordinate requirements, so their diagonals belong to the sequence algebra. For a chosen positive source cut and comparison accuracy, use [seq:uniform-witness](#seq:uniform-witness) with uncut target $(b^{(j)}-\delta')_+$ and cut size $\delta-\delta'$. Its squared norm is bounded by the source norm plus the accuracy, divided by $\delta-\delta'$. Lift the witness to a bounded coordinate sequence with arbitrarily small additional norm and equation errors, and put its finite-test equation among the requirements imposed at $n_j$. This retained target slack gives bounded witnesses on the diagonal. Enumerating source cuts and accuracies proves the desired Cuntz comparisons there. Thus the order of choices is: fixed tests and target cuts, row controllers and witnesses, comparison multiplicities, and finally the coordinate thresholds.

For rows of models, the lower bounds <a href="#seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich">[seq:lower-trimmed-sandwich]</a> use target thresholds $\kappa<\eta\delta$ determined by fixed trimmed input factors. The upper bounds <a href="#seq:upper-trimmed-sandwich" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="seq:upper-trimmed-sandwich">[seq:upper-trimmed-sandwich]</a> use cuts of fixed constant representatives, determined by the scalar slack and the fixed source tolerance. Both kinds of target threshold are independent of the row. They can therefore be retained by the preceding procedure. The converse part of [seq:sandwich](#seq:sandwich) identifies the diagonal model data. On increasing intervals the same argument uses the common compact tests and their unchanged measure; no condition is imposed on the moving endpoints. Restriction to any subsequence retains all fixed-cut comparisons, which proves the final assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-seq-lifts"></a>
<div id="seq:lifts" class="remark">

*Remark 1*. When the resulting quotient map is a homomorphism with nuclear separable domain, the completely positive lifting theorem provides c.p.c. coordinate lifts (Choi and Effros 1976, Theorem 3.10) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[ChoiEffrosLifting](#ref-ChoiEffrosLifting)]. Those lifts represent the same quotient values. Consequently all fixed-cut comparisons and all memberships in $J(U)$ are unchanged. This permits the diagonal construction to be carried out first on countable algebraic data, while later arguments use actual c.p.c. coordinate maps.

</div>

<a id="sm:section"></a>
### Small stabilizers

We construct homomorphisms whose values belong to the local error ideals, but are large enough to absorb a prescribed countable family of errors. All matrix comparisons in this section are stable comparisons. The identifications of [st:folding](#st:folding) are understood; in particular, a rational UHF fraction changes ranks, not operator norms.

<a id="stmt-sm-stabilizers"></a>
<div id="sm:stabilizers" class="theorem">

<a id="sm:local-and-lower"></a>
**Theorem 3** (Small stabilizers). *Let $S=C_0(Y)\otimes P$, and prescribe countably many pairs $$x_l\in S_+,
 \qquad e_l\in J(U_{x_l}),
 \qquad l\in\mathbb N.$$ There is a homomorphism $\theta:S\to D$ satisfying $$\label{sm:local-and-lower}
 \theta(S(U))\subseteq J(U),
 \qquad
 e_l\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\theta(x_l))
 \quad(U\subseteq X\text{ open},\ l\in\mathbb N),$$ in either of the following situations.*

<a id="sm:coarse-fullness"></a>
1.  *An existing model $\chi:S\to D$ is given. In this case $\theta$ can be a slowly vanishing rational UHF fraction of $\chi$, and it also satisfies $$\label{sm:coarse-fullness}
     \theta(S(U_x))\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\theta(x))
     \qquad(x\in S_+).$$ For every fixed $N$, $N$ copies of its defining fraction fit in the defining copy of $\chi$ at all sufficiently late coordinates.*

2.  *$Y=(0,1]$. No model is needed in this case, and <a href="#sm:coarse-fullness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:coarse-fullness">[sm:coarse-fullness]</a> is not asserted.*

<a id="sm:fixed-lower-cut"></a>
*In both cases, for every $l$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\delta_{l,\varepsilon}>0$ such that $$\label{sm:fixed-lower-cut}
 [ (|e_l|-\varepsilon)_+]
 \leq[(\theta(x_l)-\delta_{l,\varepsilon})_+]
 \quad\text{in }\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(D).$$ The construction permits the prescribed errors and the stabilizer to occupy separate matrix corners.*

</div>

The cut in <a href="#sm:fixed-lower-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:fixed-lower-cut">[sm:fixed-lower-cut]</a> will remain fixed while the accuracy, matrix sizes, and finite lists change. We first describe the countable tests and the comparison convention used to ensure this.

<a id="pm-h-21"></a>
#### Fixed tests and comparison bounds

<a id="sm:upper-tests"></a>
<a id="sm:upper-goal"></a>
Choose a countable compact-containment basis for the open ideals as in [st:controllers](#st:controllers). For every pair $V\Subset U$ in this basis, choose a norm-dense sequence of positive contractions in $S(V)$, and include all positive rational cut tolerances. Enumerate the resulting upper tests as $$\label{sm:upper-tests}
 (a_i,\varepsilon_i,V_i,U_i),\qquad
 a_i\in S(V_i)_+,\quad V_i\Subset U_i,
 \quad \varepsilon_i>0.$$ For each test fix a compactly Cuntz-supported positive $b_i\in Q(U_i)$ whose generated ideal contains $Q(V_i)$. These controllers are chosen once, before the approximation stages. Showing $$\label{sm:upper-goal}
 d_\tau((\theta_n(a_i)-\varepsilon_i)_+)
 \leq s_{i,n}d_\tau(b_i),
 \qquad s_{i,n}\longrightarrow0,$$ uniformly in $\tau$, suffices for local smallness. Indeed, [seq:smallideal-properties](#seq:smallideal-properties) first gives the conclusion on the dense positive tests in $S(V_i)$. Linearity and norm closure then give it on $S(V_i)$, and the ideals with $V_i\Subset U$ exhaust $S(U)$.

<a id="sm:error-controllers"></a>
Choose bounded representatives $e_{l,n}$ for the errors. Enumerate every error together with a sequence of positive cut tolerances decreasing to zero. Write a lower request as $q=(l,\eta)$, and put $$\label{sm:error-controllers}
 A_{q,n}=(|e_{l,n}|-\eta)_+,
 \qquad
 d_\tau(A_{q,n})\leq r_q(n)d_\tau(c_q),
 \qquad r_q(n)>0,\quad r_q(n)\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $c_q$ is a fixed compactly Cuntz-supported positive in $Q(U_{x_l})$, and the inequality holds eventually for every $\tau\in T(Q)$. Enlarging finitely many values of $r_q$ will not matter. A request with zero controller has eventually zero source cut and can be omitted.

<a id="sm:witness-bound"></a>
We will use the fixed-cut comparison bound from [seq:uniform-witness](#seq:uniform-witness). Its elementary form is worth recording. If $A\precsim(B-\delta)_+$, choose $v$ with $v^*(B-\delta)_+v$ close to $A$, and set $$w=g_\delta(B)v,
 \qquad
 g_\delta(t)=\sqrt{\frac{(t-\delta)_+}{t}},
 \qquad g_\delta(0)=0.$$ Then $$\label{sm:witness-bound}
 w^*Bw=v^*(B-\delta)_+v,
 \qquad
 \|w\|^2\leq
 \frac{\|A\|+\|v^*(B-\delta)_+v-A\|}{\delta}.$$ Thus coordinate comparisons into a fixed positive cut have bounded witnesses into the uncut target. This conclusion is independent of matrix size.

<a id="pm-h-22"></a>
#### Fractions of an existing model

<details id="proof-stmt-sm-stabilizers"><summary>Proof of stmt-sm-stabilizers</summary>

<a id="sm:model-lower"></a>
*Proof of <span class="upright">(i)</span>.* Take c.p.c. lifts $\chi_n:S\to Q$ of $\chi$, using nuclearity and the Choi–Effros lifting theorem (Choi and Effros 1976, Theorem 3.10) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[ChoiEffrosLifting](#ref-ChoiEffrosLifting)]. The constant-ideal assertion of [seq:all-traces](#seq:all-traces) gives $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\chi(x_l))=\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(U_{x_l})).$$ Compact support of the controller in <a href="#sm:error-controllers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:error-controllers">[sm:error-controllers]</a>, with an extra cut before passing to coordinates, consequently gives an integer $M_q$ and $d_q>0$ such that $$\label{sm:model-lower}
 d_\tau(c_q)
 \leq M_q d_\tau((\chi_n(x_l)-d_q)_+)
 \quad\text{eventually, for every }\tau.$$ Here is the cut detail. Write $[c_q]\leq[(z-\gamma)_+]$, with $z\in Q(U_{x_l})_+$. The constant $(z-\gamma/2)_+$ lies in the ideal generated by $\chi(x_l)$. Its suitable smaller cut is therefore below finitely many copies of a positive cut of $\chi(x_l)$. Leave a further cut between $(z-\gamma)_+$ and that source before lifting the comparison. The source perturbation lemma then gives <a href="#sm:model-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:model-lower">[sm:model-lower]</a> at every late coordinate. Both its multiplicity and target tolerance depend only on the request, not on the coordinate.

<a id="sm:model-upper"></a>
For an upper test, choose a strictly positive $h_i\in Q(V_i)$. The constant-ideal assertion gives $\chi(a_i)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(Q(V_i))=\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(h_i)$. Compact containment between two positive cuts of $\chi(a_i)$ therefore gives a finite integer $N_i$ and $\kappa_i>0$ with $$[(\chi(a_i)-\varepsilon_i/2)_+]\leq N_i[(h_i-\kappa_i)_+].$$ Let $c_i\in Q(V_i)_+$ be the folded sum of these $N_i$ cuts. It is compactly Cuntz supported in $Q(V_i)$. The common-controller lemma then gives $[c_i]\leq L_i[b_i]$ for a finite integer $L_i$. Lift a comparison for the less deeply cut source with error smaller than the remaining source slack. The positive perturbation lemma gives $$\label{sm:model-upper}
 d_\tau((\chi_n(a_i)-\varepsilon_i)_+)
 \leq L_i d_\tau(b_i)
 \quad\text{eventually, for every }\tau.$$ Thus both the controller $b_i$ and its multiplicity are fixed before the UHF fractions are selected.

At stage $j$, choose a positive rational $t_j$ with $$t_j\leq j^{-1},
 \qquad t_jL_i\leq j^{-1}\quad(i\leq j).$$ Only after choosing $t_j$, choose an increasing sequence of coordinate thresholds $N_j$ so that all the first $j$ coordinate comparisons hold and <a id="sm:model-delay"></a>$$\label{sm:model-delay}
 M_qr_q(n)\leq t_j
 \qquad(q\leq j, n\geq N_j).$$ For $N_j\leq n<N_{j+1}$, let $p_n$ be a projection of normalized trace $t_j$ in an extra UHF factor and use $$\theta_n(a)=\chi_n(a)\otimes p_n.$$ After the fixed trace-preserving identifications these are c.p.c. maps into $Q$. Their multiplicative defects tend to zero because those of $\chi_n$ do, so they define a homomorphism $\theta:S\to D$. Equation <a href="#sm:model-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:model-upper">[sm:model-upper]</a> gives <a href="#sm:upper-goal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:upper-goal">[sm:upper-goal]</a> with $s_{i,n}\leq j^{-1}$ on the $j$-th stage. Moreover, <a id="sm:fraction-lower"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 d_\tau(A_{q,n})
 &\leq r_q(n)d_\tau(c_q)\notag\\
 &\leq M_qr_q(n)d_\tau((\chi_n(x_l)-d_q)_+)
 \leq d_\tau((\theta_n(x_l)-d_q)_+).
 \label{sm:fraction-lower}
\end{aligned}$$ All inequalities use extended nonnegative arithmetic; the scalar factors are strictly positive. Pointwise order in $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q)$ gives the coordinate comparison. Apply <a href="#sm:witness-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:witness-bound">[sm:witness-bound]</a> to the target $(\theta_n(x_l)-d_q/2)_+$, cut further at $d_q/2$. Bounded witnesses now pass to $D$, proving <a href="#sm:fixed-lower-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:fixed-lower-cut">[sm:fixed-lower-cut]</a> for the enumerated tolerances, and hence for every tolerance.

It remains to check <a href="#sm:coarse-fullness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:coarse-fullness">[sm:coarse-fullness]</a>. If $y\in S(U_x)_+$, then $\chi(y)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\chi(x))$. For every source tolerance, a slightly larger source cut is below finitely many copies of a fixed cut of $\chi(x)$. Passing to coordinates with slack gives this rank inequality eventually. Tensoring both sides with the same $p_n$ multiplies both ranks by its positive trace, so the inequality remains true. Pointwise coordinate order and <a href="#sm:witness-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:witness-bound">[sm:witness-bound]</a> give $(\theta(y)-\varepsilon)_+\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\theta(x))$. Letting $\varepsilon\downarrow0$, then using linearity and closure, proves <a href="#sm:coarse-fullness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:coarse-fullness">[sm:coarse-fullness]</a>. Finally $Nt_j\leq1$ eventually for every fixed $N$, so $N$ equal rational subprojections fit in the original UHF unit. They give the stated copies inside the spare model. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-23"></a>
#### Cone blocks with a fixed lower signal

Without a model to cut down, we must first build finite matrix maps whose values detect each requested controller. The construction has three ingredients: a fixed quotient-norm threshold for the controlling input, finitely many positive support elements with plateaux, and finite-dimensional approximations of the corresponding quotient cones. The plateaux convert a matrix norm lower bound into a Cuntz comparison at the fixed threshold. Stage-dependent matrix sizes will change only the rank multiplicities.

We use the following finite approximation form of cone quasidiagonality: for a separable $C^*$-algebra $R_0$, a finite subset of $C_0((0,1])\otimes R_0$, and a positive tolerance, there is a c.p.c. map to a matrix algebra which is multiplicative to that tolerance and preserves the norms of the specified elements to that tolerance. This is Voiculescu’s cone theorem (Voiculescu 1991) <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[Voiculescu](#ref-Voiculescu)]; a direct proof in precisely this form is (Shulman 2026, Proposition 33) <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[Shulman](#ref-Shulman)].

The ideal-covering strategy in the finite cone stage is inspired by Bosa, Gabe, Sims, and White; compare (Bosa et al. 2022, Lemma 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[BGSW](#ref-BGSW)]. Their target is $\mathcal O_\infty$-stable. The fixed-cut estimates and UHF trace-small controls for the present target are proved here, not imported from that lemma.

<a id="stmt-sm-cone-stage"></a>
<div id="sm:cone-stage" class="lemma">

<a id="sm:stage-bounds"></a>
**Lemma 7** (A finite cone stage). *Let $S=C_0((0,1])\otimes P$. Fix finitely many upper tests <a href="#sm:upper-tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:upper-tests">[sm:upper-tests]</a>, finitely many lower requests <a href="#sm:error-controllers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:error-controllers">[sm:error-controllers]</a>, and a finite multiplicativity test set. For each lower request one can fix a number $h_q>0$, independently of all later stages, such that the following holds. For arbitrarily small multiplicativity error there is a c.p.c. map $\Phi:S\to M_k(Q)$, with finite constants $L_i$ and integers $M_q\geq1$, satisfying $$\label{sm:stage-bounds}
 \begin{split}
 d_\tau(\Phi(a_i))&\leq L_i d_\tau(b_i),\\
 d_\tau(c_q)&\leq
 M_q d_\tau((\Phi(x_l)-4h_q)_+)
 \qquad(q=(l,\eta)),
 \end{split}$$ for every $\tau\in T(Q)$, with the unnormalized matrix extension of $\tau$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-sm-cone-stage"><summary>Proof of stmt-sm-cone-stage</summary>

<a id="sm:envelope"></a>
*Proof.* We first choose the lower threshold, once for each request. Write $$[c_q]\leq[(z_q-\gamma_q)_+],
 \qquad z_q\in Q(U_{x_l})_+,\quad\gamma_q>0.$$ For $z\in X$, denote by $(x_l)_z$ the image in $C_0((0,1])\otimes(P/P_z)$, where $P_z$ is the primitive ideal labeled by $z$. The sets $$O_q(h)=\{z:\|(x_l)_z\|>8h\}$$ are open and increase to $U_{x_l}$ as $h\downarrow0$. Approximation in this increasing union of ideals allows us to choose $h_q>0$ such that $$\label{sm:envelope}
 z'_q=(z_q-\gamma_q/2)_+\in Q(O_q(h_q)),
 \qquad [c_q]\ll[z'_q].$$ Keep $h_q$ fixed from now on.

<a id="sm:plateau"></a>
<a id="sm:finite-cover"></a>
For a point $z\in O_q(h_q)$, choose an open neighborhood $$N_z\subseteq O_q(h_q)\cap
 \bigcap_{\{i:(a_i)_z\ne0\}}\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}_X(b_i).$$ It exists because $(a_i)_z\ne0$ implies $z\in V_i$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}_X(b_i)$ contains $V_i$. Functional calculus on a positive element of $Q(N_z)$ nonzero at $z$ gives positive contractions $h_z,k_z$ with $$\label{sm:plateau}
 h_zk_z=k_z,
 \qquad (k_z)_z\ne0,
 \qquad h_z\text{ compactly Cuntz-supported in }Q(N_z).$$ For example, first take a nonzero positive cut, make $h_z$ equal to one on a smaller spectral region, and support $k_z$ inside that region. The ideal supports of all these $k_z$’s cover $O_q(h_q)$. By <a href="#sm:envelope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:envelope">[sm:envelope]</a>, finitely many of them suffice for $$\label{sm:finite-cover}
 [c_q]\leq M_q\sum_{t=1}^{\ell_q}[k_{qt}]$$ for some integer $M_q$. This uses compact containment in the Cuntz semigroup: the ideal generated by all the $k_z$’s contains $z'_q$, and $[c_q]\ll[z'_q]$ is captured by a finite sum with finite multiplicity. No compactness of the whole open set $O_q(h_q)$ is assumed.

For each selected center $z$, put $S_z=C_0((0,1])\otimes(P/P_z)$, and let $\alpha_t$, $0\leq t\leq1$, be cone scaling: $$(\alpha_t f)(r)=f(tr),
 \qquad \alpha_0=0.$$ The scaled images of a finite test set form norm-compact families. Choose finite nets in those families and their products. Cone quasidiagonality, followed by contractivity to pass from the nets to the families, gives a c.p.c. map $\phi_z:S_z\to M_{d_z}$ with the required uniform multiplicativity error and <a id="sm:matrix-signal"></a>$$\label{sm:matrix-signal}
 \|\phi_z((x_l)_z)\|>7h_q.$$ Define a matrix-valued cone function by $$t\longmapsto\phi_z(\alpha_t(s_z)).$$ It is norm-continuous and zero at $t=0$. Evaluate its entries at $h_z$. The resulting map $\psi_z:S\to M_{d_z}(Q)$ is c.p.c.; it has the same multiplicativity bound because the evaluation map is a homomorphism. Its range lies in $M_{d_z}(\mathop{\mathrm{Her}}(h_z))$.

For the lower estimate, choose a unit vector $v\in\mathbb C^{d_z}$ which is an eigenvector of the positive matrix $\phi_z((x_l)_z)$ for an eigenvalue $\lambda>7h_q$. Equation <a href="#sm:plateau" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:plateau">[sm:plateau]</a> implies that evaluation on $k_z^{1/2}$ is evaluation at parameter one. Consequently the rectangular column $$w=(\lambda-4h_q)^{-1/2}\,v\otimes k_z^{1/2}$$ satisfies <a id="sm:plateau-witness"></a>$$\label{sm:plateau-witness}
 w^*(\psi_z(x_l)-4h_q)_+w=k_z,
 \qquad \|w\|\leq(3h_q)^{-1/2}.$$ Combining these comparisons with <a href="#sm:finite-cover" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:finite-cover">[sm:finite-cover]</a> gives the second inequality of <a href="#sm:stage-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:stage-bounds">[sm:stage-bounds]</a> for the direct sum of all the selected blocks.

For an upper test, a block is zero if $(a_i)_z=0$. Otherwise $h_z$ is compactly Cuntz-supported in an ideal contained in $\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_Q(b_i)$. It is therefore below finitely many copies of $b_i$. Since the block range is supported by $1_{d_z}\otimes h_z$, its rank is at most $d_zd_\tau(h_z)$. Summing these finite bounds gives the first inequality of <a href="#sm:stage-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:stage-bounds">[sm:stage-bounds]</a>. The direct sum is contractive, since its norm is the maximum of the block norms. Its internal matrix trace is the sum of the block traces. This proves the lemma. ◻

</details>

The finite stage now has a lower spectral cut independent of the accuracy. To turn these maps into a locally small homomorphism, we choose their UHF fractions next and delay the coordinates until the prescribed errors are smaller than those fractions.

<details id="proof-stmt-sm-cone-stage-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-sm-cone-stage</summary>

<a id="sm:cone-fraction"></a>
*Proof of <span class="upright">(ii)</span>.* At stage $j$, apply [sm:cone-stage](#sm:cone-stage) to the first $j$ upper tests, lower requests, and an increasing dense list of algebra tests, with multiplicativity error at most $j^{-1}$. Write the resulting map as $\Phi_j$, and its constants as $L_{ij}$ and $M_{qj}$. Choose a positive rational $t_j$ satisfying $$\label{sm:cone-fraction}
 t_j\leq j^{-1},
 \qquad t_jL_{ij}\leq j^{-1}\quad(i\leq j),
 \qquad \beta_{qj}:=t_j/M_{qj}>0.$$ After all the stage maps and fractions have been chosen, choose increasing coordinate thresholds $N_j$ with <a id="sm:cone-delay"></a>$$\label{sm:cone-delay}
 r_q(n)\leq\beta_{qj}
 \qquad(q\leq j, n\geq N_j).$$ On $N_j\leq n<N_{j+1}$, tensor $\Phi_j$ with a UHF projection of trace $t_j$, and fold its finite matrices to obtain $\theta_n:S\to Q$. These maps are c.p.c. and asymptotically multiplicative. Their quotient is therefore a homomorphism $\theta$.

For a fixed upper test, <a href="#sm:stage-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:stage-bounds">[sm:stage-bounds]</a> and <a href="#sm:cone-fraction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:cone-fraction">[sm:cone-fraction]</a> give <a href="#sm:upper-goal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:upper-goal">[sm:upper-goal]</a> eventually with bound $j^{-1}$. Thus $\theta(S(U))\subseteq J(U)$. For a lower request $q=(l,\eta)$, <a id="sm:cone-coordinate-comparison"></a>$$\label{sm:cone-coordinate-comparison}
 d_\tau(A_{q,n})
 \leq r_q(n)d_\tau(c_q)
 \leq d_\tau((\theta_n(x_l)-4h_q)_+)$$ eventually at every coordinate. Pointwise trace order in $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(Q)$ converts this into Cuntz comparison. Put $B_{q,n}=(\theta_n(x_l)-2h_q)_+$, choose comparison witnesses with error at most $n^{-1}$, and apply <a href="#sm:witness-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:witness-bound">[sm:witness-bound]</a> with tolerance $2h_q$. The resulting witnesses have squared norms at most $$\frac{\sup_n\|A_{q,n}\|+1}{2h_q},$$ independently of the stage, its centers, and its matrix size. They prove $$[(|e_l|-\eta)_+]
 \leq[(\theta(x_l)-2h_q)_+]
 \quad\text{in }\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(D).$$ This establishes <a href="#sm:fixed-lower-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:fixed-lower-cut">[sm:fixed-lower-cut]</a> and then <a href="#sm:local-and-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:local-and-lower">[sm:local-and-lower]</a>. A very small $\beta_{qj}$ changes only the delay $N_j$; it never changes the fixed cut $h_q$. All comparisons may be made in the common block sequence algebra before folding. Include the finitely many prescribed error representatives in each stage’s folding tests in [st:folding](#st:folding). The coordinate conjugacies then preserve their original quotient classes, and the norm bounds above are unchanged. This proves the separate-corner assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-24"></a>
#### Coefficient families

<a id="stmt-sm-coefficient-domination"></a>
<div id="sm:coefficient-domination" class="corollary">

<a id="sm:all-coefficients"></a>
**Corollary 2** (Simultaneous coefficient domination). *Suppose that one of the hypotheses of [sm:stabilizers](#sm:stabilizers) holds. Let $E_j$ be countably many bounded multilinear maps $$E_j:S\times Z_{j,1}\times\cdots\times Z_{j,r_j}\longrightarrow D,$$ where the parameter spaces are separable normed spaces. Assume that $$E_j(S(U),Z_{j,1},\ldots,Z_{j,r_j})\subseteq J(U)
 \quad\text{for every open }U.$$ Then the stabilizer may also be chosen so that, for every $x\in S_+$, $$\label{sm:all-coefficients}
 E_j(S(U_x),Z_{j,1},\ldots,Z_{j,r_j})
 \subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\theta(x)).$$ The same conclusion holds for norm-continuous separably parametrized families of such maps, with local norm bounds, and for any choice of the localized multilinear input.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-sm-coefficient-domination"><summary>Proof of stmt-sm-coefficient-domination</summary>

<a id="sm:projected-cover"></a>
*Proof.* Choose a countable family $\mathcal B\subseteq S_+$ of positive cuts which is an ideal basis: for every $x\in S_+$, the elements of $\mathcal B\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_S(x)$ generate $\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_S(x)$. Such a family is obtained from a countable norm-dense positive set and rational cuts, using the positive-element perturbation lemma. In particular, $$\label{sm:projected-cover}
 U_x=\bigcup_{y\in\mathcal B\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_S(x)}U_y.$$ For each $y\in\mathcal B$, prescribe the errors $E_j(a,z_1,\ldots,z_{r_j})$, where $a$ runs through a countable dense set in $S(U_y)$, and the other variables run through countable dense sets in their parameter spaces. These are countably many requests with controlling element $y$, to which [sm:stabilizers](#sm:stabilizers) applies. Continuity gives the desired containment for every input in $S(U_y)$ and every parameter value.

Now fix $x$. If $y\in\mathcal B\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_S(x)$, the homomorphism property gives $\theta(y)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(\theta(x))$. By <a href="#sm:projected-cover" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:projected-cover">[sm:projected-cover]</a>, $S(U_x)$ is the closed sum of the ideals $S(U_y)$ in that equation. Linearity in the localized input and closedness of the target ideal give <a href="#sm:all-coefficients" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:all-coefficients">[sm:all-coefficients]</a>. Dense parameter sets and local norm continuity handle the asserted continuous families. Notice that the argument uses $y\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_S(x)$, not merely $U_y\subseteq U_x$. Thus it does not presume coarse fullness of the cone stabilizer. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-sm-multiplier-coefficients"></a>
<div id="sm:multiplier-coefficients" class="remark">

*Remark 2*. Two forms of [sm:coefficient-domination](#sm:coefficient-domination) will be used. If $I$ is a separable error algebra and $d:S\to D$ is bounded linear with $d(S(U))I\subseteq J(U)$, apply the corollary to $E(a,b)=d(a)b$, $b\in I$, and, when needed, to the adjoint and opposite-product maps. This gives the full projected-support bounds on the hereditary support generators.

Alternatively, let $H\subseteq D$ be a given $\sigma$-unital hereditary algebra, possibly nonseparable, and let $d:S\to M(H)$ be a bounded linear map whose localized coefficients lie in $J(U)$. Choose a countable approximate unit $(u_m)$ for $H$ and prescribe $u_md(a)u_m$ by the corollary. For $h,k\in H$, $$h d(a)k=\lim_m h u_m d(a)u_m k.$$ Thus all multiplier coefficients are controlled. The countability needed here comes from $\sigma$-unitality, not from separability of $H$.

</div>

<a id="sec:uniqueness"></a>
### Norm uniqueness of sequence models

We prove uniqueness for the models of [seq:model](#seq:model), allowing any of the spaces $Y$ and measures $m$ specified there. The uniqueness argument uses the coarse labels $$S=S_Y=C_0(Y)\otimes P,\qquad S(U)=C_0(Y)\otimes P(U),
 \qquad U\subseteq X\text{ open}.$$ The same statements apply if the prescribed measure is multiplied by a strictly positive constant. A coordinate unitary will always mean a unitary in $\mathcal M(Q)$; convergence of coordinate conjugacies is in norm on each fixed input.

<a id="stmt-un-uniqueness"></a>
<div id="un:uniqueness" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Norm uniqueness). *Let $p,q:S_Y\to Q_\infty$ be models for the same data. For any c.p.c. lifts $p_n,q_n:S_Y\to Q$, there are $u_n\in\mathcal U(\mathcal M(Q))$ such that $$\lim_{n\to\infty}\|u_np_n(a)u_n^*-q_n(a)\|=0,
 \qquad a\in S_Y.$$*

</div>

There are two steps. Equal trace data first give, after conjugacy and rational averaging, a difference in every local error ideal $J(U)$. A small fraction of a common spare model then absorbs that difference in norm. Splitting each original model into two UHF halves supplies the spare: change the first half while keeping the second, and then change the second while keeping the first. The intermediate absorption statements allow a general labeling space, so that the cone correction can use them with its finer ideal labels.

<a id="pm-h-26"></a>
#### Compact sets of weights and tracial alignment

<a id="stmt-un-trace-cap"></a>
<div id="un:trace-cap" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Controlled trace evaluations). *Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra and $b\in(C\otimes\mathcal K)_+$. The set $$K_b=\{\gamma\in T(C):d_\gamma(b)\leq1\}$$ is compact and convex. If $x\in(C\otimes\mathcal K)_+$ satisfies $\sup_{\gamma\in K_b}d_\gamma(x)\leq L<\infty$, then $\gamma\mapsto\gamma(x)$ is finite, continuous, and affine on $K_b$. The constant $L$ may depend on $x$.*

*Consequently this applies separately to all the first and square evaluations of a fixed completely positive candidate whose tested values have bounded rank on $K_b$. It also applies to $$\gamma\longmapsto
 \gamma\bigl(\lvert u x_1u^*-x_2\rvert^2\bigr),
 \qquad u\in\mathcal U(C^{\sim}),$$ whenever the positive elements $x_1,x_2$ have uniformly bounded ranks there, with a rank bound independent of $u$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-un-trace-cap"><summary>Proof of stmt-un-trace-cap</summary>

*Proof.* Rank evaluation is lower semicontinuous on the compact trace cone, so $K_b$ is closed. Its convexity follows from additivity and positive homogeneity of rank. For $\eta>0$ the assumed rank bound gives <a id="un:cut-loss"></a>$$\label{un:cut-loss}
 0\leq\gamma(x)-\gamma((x-\eta)_+)\leq\eta L,
 \qquad \gamma\in K_b,$$ and $\gamma(x)\leq\|x\|L$. If $\gamma_i\to\gamma$ in $K_b$, the defining cut inequalities for the trace topology imply $$\gamma(x)\leq\liminf_i\gamma_i(x)
 \leq\limsup_i\gamma_i(x)
 \leq\gamma(x)+\eta L.$$ Letting $\eta\downarrow0$ proves continuity. Affineness follows from the cone operations; it does not require cancellation in that cone. Squaring a positive element does not change its support rank. Finally, support subadditivity and unitary invariance give $$d_\gamma\bigl(\lvert u x_1u^*-x_2\rvert^2\bigr)
 \leq d_\gamma(x_1)+d_\gamma(x_2),$$ which proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-un-barycenter"></a>
<div id="un:barycenter" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Barycenters on a trace cap). *Let $K_b$ be as in [un:trace-cap](#un:trace-cap). For every probability measure $\mu$ on $K_b$ there is $\bar\gamma\in K_b$ such that <a id="un:barycenter-formula"></a>$$\label{un:barycenter-formula}
 v(\bar\gamma)=\int_{K_b}v\,d\mu$$ for every finite continuous affine real function $v$ on $K_b$. For a positive measure of finite mass $M>0$, its integral is instead $M v(\bar\gamma)$, with $\bar\gamma$ chosen for $\mu/M$; zero measures contribute zero. Signed measures may be treated by their two positive parts.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-un-barycenter"><summary>Proof of stmt-un-barycenter</summary>

*Proof.* Approximate $\mu$ weakly by finitely supported probability measures $\mu_j=\sum_k t_{jk}\delta_{\gamma_{jk}}$, omitting terms of weight zero. Their finite barycenters $\beta_j=\sum_k t_{jk}\gamma_{jk}$ belong to $K_b$. Take a subnet converging in the compact set $K_b$ to $\bar\gamma$. For every $v$ in the statement, $$v(\bar\gamma)=\lim_jv(\beta_j)
 =\lim_j\int v\,d\mu_j=\int v\,d\mu.$$ The same subnet works for all these functions. Neither uniqueness of $\bar\gamma$ on discontinuous evaluations nor subtraction of extended weights is asserted or used. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-un-finite-traces"></a>
<div id="un:finite-traces" class="lemma">

<a id="un:finite-L2"></a>
**Lemma 10** (Alignment for finitely many weights). *Let $C$ be separable, let $S$ be separable and nuclear, and let $p,q:S\to C$ be homomorphisms with equal pullbacks on $T(C)$. For a finite list of pairs $(s_i,\gamma_i)$, where $s_i\in S_+$ and $$d_{\gamma_i}(p(s_i))+d_{\gamma_i}(q(s_i))<\infty,$$ and any $\delta>0$, there is $u\in\mathcal U(M_2(C)^{\sim})$ such that $$\label{un:finite-L2}
 (\gamma_i\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2)
 \bigl(\lvert u(p(s_i)\oplus0)u^*-(q(s_i)\oplus0)\rvert^2\bigr)<\delta
 \quad\text{for every }i.$$ The added matrix direction is internal and has unnormalized trace.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-un-finite-traces"><summary>Proof of stmt-un-finite-traces</summary>

*Proof.* The equality of pullback weights implies equality of all their ranks. By (Ciuperca et al. 2013, Lemma 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[CGNN](#ref-CGNN)], the support projections of $p(s)$ and $q(s)$ are equivalent in $C^{**}$ for every $s\in S_+$. Their images remain equivalent under the normal extension of every representation of $C$.

The finite-rank assumption puts $p(s_i)$ and $q(s_i)$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\gamma_i)$. After zero padding, $M_2(\mathop{\mathrm{Fin}}(\gamma_i))$ is invariant under $M_2(C)^{\sim}$-unitaries, so the conjugates and squared differences below remain in that finite ideal. For each selected weight, take its trace representation $\pi_i$ on its densely finite ideal after quotienting by its zero ideal, and extend it nondegenerately to $C$. Let $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_i$ be the resulting normal semifinite trace on $M_i=\pi_i(C)''$. Form $\pi=\pi_0\oplus\pi_1\oplus\cdots\oplus\pi_r$, with $\pi_0$ a faithful representation on a separable Hilbert space, and put $M=\pi(C)''$. The trace representations can also be chosen separably acting: their densely finite ideals are separable and have countable approximate units of finite-trace cuts. Thus $M$ has separable predual. Restriction to the $i$th reducing Hilbert summand gives a normal surjective homomorphism $q_i:M\to M_i$. Its kernel is $M(1-z_i)$ for a central projection $z_i$, and $q_i$ identifies $Mz_i$ with $M_i$. Define $$\widehat\gamma_i=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_i\circ q_i.$$ This is the transported normal semifinite trace on $Mz_i$, extended by zero on $M(1-z_i)$. The projections $z_i$ may overlap; the construction is made separately for each weight. On the densely finite ideal of $\gamma_i$ one has $\widehat\gamma_i\pi=\gamma_i$, so it computes all designated finite-rank tests and their squares.

We apply von Neumann uniqueness only after a common zero padding and unitization. Let $S^\dagger$ be the forced unitization, and define unital homomorphisms into $M_2(M)$ by $$\widehat p(a+\lambda1)=(\pi p(a)\oplus0)+\lambda1_{M_2(M)},\qquad
 \widehat q(a+\lambda1)=(\pi q(a)\oplus0)+\lambda1_{M_2(M)}.$$ These maps have equal support ranks on every positive element of $S^\dagger$. If its scalar part is zero, this follows from the support equivalences already proved. If the scalar part is positive, then on the properly infinite central part of $M$ both support projections contain the second matrix corner, which is equivalent to the unit of $M_2(M)$ there. On the finite central part, every normal finite trace has equal pullbacks along $\pi p$ and $\pi q$; this follows by restricting it to $C$ and using the hypothesis. The unital extensions also have equal pullbacks, hence equal ranks by monotone spectral calculus. Normal traces determine equivalence of projections in a finite von Neumann algebra. This proves the claim on both parts.

The source $S^\dagger$ is nuclear. We use the unital form of von Neumann uniqueness in (Ciuperca et al. 2013, Proposition 2.1, Corollary 3.10, and Section 4) <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[CGNN](#ref-CGNN)]. For completeness, the properly infinite factor argument needs only the partial-isometry approximation of (Ciuperca et al. 2013, Lemma 3.6) <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[CGNN](#ref-CGNN)] and the following unital completion. Including $1$ among the inputs gives partial isometries $v$ whose initial projections $e=v^*v$ tend strongly to $1$. For finitely many normal states choose $e_0\leq e$ arbitrarily close to $e$ on those states, with $1-e_0\sim1-ve_0v^*\sim1$: take $e_0=e$ if $e$ is finite, and otherwise remove an infinite tail decreasing strongly to zero. Complete $ve_0$ to a unitary $u$. Since $(u-v)e_0=0$, Cauchy–Schwarz gives $$|\rho(u^*xu-v^*xv)|\leq4\|x\|\rho(1-e_0)^{1/2}.$$ Thus the partial-isometry conjugacies can be replaced by unitary conjugacies in the weak star topology; (Ciuperca et al. 2013, Lemma 3.9) <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[CGNN](#ref-CGNN)] makes them strong star. Section 4 then assembles these unital factor conclusions by central decomposition; separability lets one impose the support comparisons on one countable dense set of positive cuts before selecting the factorwise unitaries. We obtain $w_j\in M_2(M)$ with $$w_j(\pi p(a)\oplus0)w_j^*\longrightarrow\pi q(a)\oplus0
 \quad\text{strong star},\qquad a\in S.$$ The unital extensions are needed here: support ranks of nonunital maps alone do not compare the complements of their support projections.

For clarity, the passage to $L^2$ uses more than strong convergence. Put $x_j=w_j(\pi p(s_i)\oplus0)w_j^*$ and $x=\pi q(s_i)\oplus0$. Write $T_i=\widehat\gamma_i\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2$. Their $T_i$-norms are finite and satisfy $$\|x_j\|_{2,T_i}^2
 =\gamma_i(p(s_i^2))
 =\gamma_i(q(s_i^2))
 =\|x\|_{2,T_i}^2.$$ For a bounded test $y\in L^1(T_i)\cap
L^2(T_i)$ supported on trace-finite projections, its pairing with $x_j-x$ is the normal bounded functional $t\mapsto T_i(y^*t)$ and tends to zero. Such tests are dense in $L^2(T_i)$; the common $L^2$ bound therefore gives weak $L^2$ convergence. Equality of norms now gives $\|x_j-x\|_{2,T_i}\to0$.

Choose one $w_j$ meeting the finite list to sufficiently small tolerance. A bounded self-adjoint logarithm of this unitary can be approximated strong star, within its norm bound, by self-adjoint elements of $M_2(\pi(C))^{\sim}$, by Kaplansky density (Kaplansky 1951, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[KaplanskyDensity](#ref-KaplanskyDensity)]. Exponentiation gives unitaries from $M_2(C)^{\sim}$ converging strong star to $w_j$. Their conjugates again have the same designated $L^2$ norms, so the preceding finite-trace-test argument gives $L^2$ convergence for this approximation too. On these conjugates the normal traces compute the original weights, since their finite ideals are invariant under $M_2(C)^{\sim}$-unitaries. This proves <a href="#un:finite-L2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="un:finite-L2">[un:finite-L2]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-un-alignment"></a>
<div id="un:alignment" class="lemma">

<a id="un:local-difference"></a>
**Lemma 11** (Tracial alignment). *If $p,q:S\to D$ are models with the same data, then rational diagonal averaging, coordinate multiplier conjugacy, and the foldings of [st:folding](#st:folding) give models $\widetilde p,\widetilde q:S\to D$ with $$\label{un:local-difference}
 (\widetilde p-\widetilde q)(S(U))\subseteq J(U)
 \qquad(U\subseteq X\text{ open}).$$ Each new model is coordinate approximately unitarily equivalent to its respective original model. The operations may be performed in one changing block while a prescribed spare block is carried along unchanged, apart from its common amplification and folding.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-un-alignment"><summary>Proof of stmt-un-alignment</summary>

*Proof.* By [seq:all-traces](#seq:all-traces), choose a separable subalgebra $D_0$ containing the two ranges, the required constant representatives, and the comparison witnesses, so that $p$ and $q$ agree on all traces of $D_0$. Work in a common stabilized corner when needed.

<a id="un:controller-rank"></a>
Choose a countable local positive test family $s_i$, dense in linear span in each of the basis ideals used in [seq:diagonal](#seq:diagonal). Each test is cut-supported in both factors and has compact support in $Y$. Choose an enlarged elementary test $\bar s_i$ and a fixed $\kappa_i>0$ with $s_i\precsim(\bar s_i-\kappa_i)_+$, using the positive cut in each factor. Hence $p(s_i)\precsim(p(\bar s_i)-\kappa_i)_+$, and the same holds for $q$. The model-independent upper sandwich, applied to this fixed cut of $\bar s_i$, supplies a common compact-Cuntz-supported constant; dominating it in the chosen containing open gives $b_i\in Q(U_i)_+$ and a finite constant $L_i$ such that $$\label{un:controller-rank}
 d_\gamma(p(s_i))+d_\gamma(q(s_i))
 \leq L_i d_\gamma(b_i),\qquad\gamma\in T(D_0),$$ for a finite $L_i$. The constants and the comparisons are included in $D_0$. One can retain $b_i$ whenever later finite lists grow.

Fix a finite initial list and $\delta>0$. For $u\in\mathcal U(M_2(D_0)^{\sim})$, define the nonnegative continuous functions $$f_{u,i}(\gamma)
 =(\gamma\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2)
 \bigl(\lvert u(p(s_i)\oplus0)u^*-(q(s_i)\oplus0)\rvert^2\bigr),
 \qquad \gamma\in K_{b_i}.$$ Here $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2$ is unnormalized: the common zero padding leaves <a href="#un:controller-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="un:controller-rank">[un:controller-rank]</a> unchanged. Continuity follows from [un:trace-cap](#un:trace-cap). We claim that zero lies in the closed convex hull of the tuples $(f_{u,i})_i$ in $\bigoplus_i C(K_{b_i},\mathbb R)$. Otherwise a separating real functional would be bounded below by a positive constant on all these tuples. Represent its components by signed measures. Because $f_{u,i}\geq0$, replacing each measure by its positive part retains that lower bound. By [un:barycenter](#un:barycenter), the resulting functional on these functions is a finite sum of positive multiples of evaluations at individual weights in the corresponding caps. [un:finite-traces](#un:finite-traces) makes all these evaluations simultaneously arbitrarily small, a contradiction.

It follows that a finite convex combination satisfies <a id="un:uniform-square"></a>$$\label{un:uniform-square}
 \sup_{\gamma\in K_{b_i}}
 \sum_{k=1}^r t_k f_{u_k,i}(\gamma)<\delta
 \qquad\text{for every tested }i.$$ The coefficients may be chosen positive and rational: the finite functions being averaged are bounded, so sufficiently small changes of the coefficients preserve the strict inequality. Write $t_k=m_k/l$. Before folding, work in $B_l=M_l(M_2(Q))$ with trace $\tau\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2\otimes\operatorname{tr}_l$: only this last, additional averaging direction is normalized. The two averaged maps are diagonal copies of $\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(u_k)(p\oplus0)$ and of $q\oplus0$, with $m_k$ occurrences of the $k$th block. The original algebra $D_0$ has the fixed corner embedding $$\iota_l:D_0\longrightarrow(B_l)_\infty,
 \qquad x\longmapsto(x\oplus0)\otimes1_l.$$

<a id="un:cap-coordinate-rank"></a>
<a id="un:averaged-limit"></a>
<a id="un:coordinate-rank"></a>
We record explicitly the resulting coordinate estimate. Lift the finitely many unitaries to unitary multiplier sequences, and use c.p.c. representatives of $p,q$. Write $z_{i,n}$ for the difference of their averaged values, after folding into $Q$. For every fixed $t>0$, we first prove the absolute estimate $$\label{un:cap-coordinate-rank}
 d_\tau((|z_{i,n}|-t)_+)
 \leq \frac{4\delta}{t^2}
 \qquad\bigl(d_\tau(b_i)\leq1\bigr)$$ for every sufficiently large $n$, uniformly over this cap. Suppose that this absolute bound fails arbitrarily late, and choose violating coordinates and weights $\tau_n$. The trace identities of [st:folding](#st:folding) pull these weights back, before folding, to $\tau_n\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2\otimes\operatorname{tr}_l$. The subsequent multiplier conjugations preserve these identities. Positive cuts commute with the coordinate folding maps, so regularization commutes with them as well. Let $\rho_l$ be the regularized trace before folding and put $\gamma=\rho_l\circ\iota_l$. It is a trace on the same original $D_0$, and [seq:regularized](#seq:regularized) gives $$d_\gamma(b_i)
 =\sup_{\eta>0}\lim_\omega d_{\tau_n}((b_i-\eta)_+)\leq1.$$ Thus $\gamma\in K_{b_i}$. Put $e_{k,i}=u_k(p(s_i)\oplus0)u_k^*-(q(s_i)\oplus0)\in M_2(D_0)$. If $z_i$ denotes the unfolded diagonal difference, matrix-unit traciality and finite additivity give $$\label{un:averaged-limit}
 \rho_l(z_i^*z_i)
 =\sum_k\frac{m_k}{l}(\gamma\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2)(e_{k,i}^*e_{k,i})<\delta.$$ Indeed, restriction to the first internal corner gives $\gamma$, so the matrix extension on $M_2(D_0)$ is $\gamma\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2$. For every positive $a\in M_2(D_0)$, $\rho_l(a\otimes e_{jj})=(\gamma\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_2)(a)/l$: the $l$ averaging corners are equivalent and sum to $a\otimes1_l$. This uses only finite positive sums, so also holds when a value is infinite. It follows that <a href="#un:averaged-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="un:averaged-limit">[un:averaged-limit]</a> is valid without a bounded-weight assumption. On the other hand, functional calculus gives $$\tau\bigl((|z_{i,n}|^2-t^2/2)_+\bigr)
 \geq \frac{t^2}{2}d_\tau((|z_{i,n}|-t)_+).$$ Regularization and the violations of the absolute estimate therefore give a square trace at least $2\delta$, contrary to <a href="#un:averaged-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="un:averaged-limit">[un:averaged-limit]</a>. This proves <a href="#un:cap-coordinate-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="un:cap-coordinate-rank">[un:cap-coordinate-rank]</a>. If $0<d_\tau(b_i)<\infty$, apply that estimate to $\tau/d_\tau(b_i)$. If $d_\tau(b_i)=0$, apply it to every positive multiple of $\tau$, forcing the left side to be zero. For $d_\tau(b_i)=\infty$ the desired proportional bound is automatic. Thus, at the same sufficiently late coordinates, $$\label{un:coordinate-rank}
 d_\tau((|z_{i,n}|-t)_+)
 \leq \frac{4\delta}{t^2}d_\tau(b_i)
 \qquad\text{for all }\tau\in T(Q).$$

At stage $j$ use the first $j$ local tests, the cut tolerances $1,1/2,\ldots,1/j$, and choose $\delta_j<2^{-j}/(4j^2)$. Then advance far enough in that row for <a href="#un:coordinate-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="un:coordinate-rank">[un:coordinate-rank]</a>, the chosen algebraic tests, and their norm bounds all to hold. Pass through the rows slowly as in [seq:diagonal](#seq:diagonal). The averaging matrix size is fixed while each row’s uniform coordinate estimate is proved. For a fixed local test and any fixed cut tolerance, this gives its $J(U_i)$ estimate against the fixed $b_i$. Closure and the local density of the tests give <a href="#un:local-difference" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="un:local-difference">[un:local-difference]</a> for all open ideals.

For completeness, these operations preserve the asserted relation to the original maps. At each coordinate, a diagonal sum of conjugates of a map is conjugate, by the diagonal of the same unitaries, to its tensor-first copy in the averaging matrix factor. The coordinate folding can be chosen, using [st:folding](#st:folding), to carry that copy within the stage tolerance of the original on every current input. The first-corner clause of [st:folding](#st:folding) removes the common internal zero padding at the same time. Make that tolerance tend to zero during the same diagonal choice. Inner conjugacy preserves every coordinate weight, and vanishing norm changes preserve regularized values by cuts, so the models keep their data. The argument applies to a designated block of a direct sum, amplifying the spare by the same averaging factor. Its unused part can always be placed in a separate stable corner before folding. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-27"></a>
#### Ideal-related absorption and compact perturbations

Tracial alignment has placed the difference of two models in each local error ideal. To remove that difference in norm, we will dominate its coefficients by a small stabilizer and apply stable uniqueness on a hereditary support. We first state the absorption and compact perturbation results used in that step.

We specify the version of the ideal-related machinery used below. An $X$-structure on a coefficient algebra $H$ is an order-preserving assignment $U\mapsto H(U)$ of closed ideals. We do not assume that this assignment preserves intersections. Set $$E_H=\ell^2(\mathbb N)\otimes H,\qquad
 \mathcal K(E_H)=H\otimes\mathcal K,\qquad
 \mathcal K(E_H)(U)=H(U)\otimes\mathcal K.$$ A representation $\phi:S\to\mathcal L(E_H)$ is weakly equivariant if <a id="un:weak-equivariance"></a>$$\label{un:weak-equivariance}
 \langle\xi,\phi(a)\eta\rangle\in H(U)
 \quad(a\in S(U),\ \xi,\eta\in E_H).$$ Equivalently, $\phi(S(U))E_H\subseteq E_HH(U)$. Every adjointable module operator preserves these submodules. The diagonal coefficient maps $a\mapsto\langle\xi,\phi(a)\xi\rangle$ are completely positive. For a nuclear $S$ they and their induced quotient maps are nuclear, since ideals and quotients of a nuclear $C^*$-algebra are nuclear (Choi and Effros 1977, Corollary 4) <a id="pm-cite-49"></a>[[ChoiEffrosInjectivity](#ref-ChoiEffrosInjectivity)]; other coefficients follow by polarization. Thus weak equivariance here implies weak $X$-nuclearity, also for homotopies.

<a id="stmt-un-absorption"></a>
<div id="un:absorption" class="theorem">

**Theorem 5** (Ideal-related absorption). *Let $S$ be separable, exact, and lower semicontinuous over $X$, and let $H$ be sigma-unital. Suppose $\theta:S\to H\otimes\mathcal K$ is a nuclear $X$-full homomorphism, meaning $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_{H\otimes\mathcal K}(\theta(a))=H(U_a)\otimes\mathcal K
 \qquad(a\in S_+).$$ Then its infinite repeat is weakly $X$-nuclear and absorbs every weakly $X$-nuclear representation on the standard module, by unitary equivalences with compact errors tending to zero on each input.*

</div>

This is (Gabe 2024, Theorem 11.14) <a id="pm-cite-50"></a>[[Gabe](#ref-Gabe)], applied to $H\otimes\mathcal K$. Neither separability of $H$ nor an absorption hypothesis on $H$ is required. The repeat is taken on the stabilized module. In particular, if a map $\theta_0:S\to H$ is first placed in the compact corner $\theta_0\otimes e_{11}$, its stable repeat retains the other, zero module slots. After a permutation it has the form $\operatorname{diag}(\theta_0,0,\theta_0,0,\ldots)$. These zero slots remain available when absorbing a degenerate representation.

<a id="un:pair-cycle"></a>
For weakly equivariant representations $\phi,\psi:S\to
\mathcal L(E_H)$ with compact difference, write $[\phi,\psi]_X$ for the class of the graded cycle $$\label{un:pair-cycle}
 \left(E_H\oplus E_H,
 \begin{pmatrix}\phi&0\\0&\psi\end{pmatrix},
 \begin{pmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{pmatrix}\right).$$ The two summands have opposite gradings. The compact-difference condition supplies the compact commutators in this cycle. This notation refers to the pair, without assigning separate classes to the multiplier-valued maps.

<a id="stmt-un-stable-uniqueness"></a>
<div id="un:stable-uniqueness" class="proposition">

<a id="un:proper-uniqueness"></a>
**Proposition 5** (Multiplier stable uniqueness). *Suppose $S$ is separable, nuclear, and lower semicontinuous over $X$, $H$ is sigma-unital, and $\theta:S\to H\otimes\mathcal K$ is an $X$-full homomorphism. Let $\phi,\psi:S\to\mathcal L(E_H)$ be weakly equivariant representations with compact difference and $[\phi,\psi]_X=0$. Writing $\theta^\infty$ for an infinite repeat, for every finite $\mathcal F\subset S$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there is a unitary $v\in1+\mathcal K(E_H\oplus E_H)$ such that $$\label{un:proper-uniqueness}
 \|v(\phi\oplus\theta^\infty)(a)v^*
       -(\psi\oplus\theta^\infty)(a)\|<\varepsilon,
 \qquad a\in\mathcal F.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-un-stable-uniqueness"><summary>Proof of stmt-un-stable-uniqueness</summary>

*Proof.* The multiplier-pair theorem (Gabe 2024, Theorem 13.9) <a id="pm-cite-51"></a>[[Gabe](#ref-Gabe)] supplies a weakly $X$-nuclear representation $\Theta$ and an identity-plus-compact unitary $u$ comparing $\phi\oplus\Theta$ with $\psi\oplus\Theta$ to any prescribed finite-test tolerance $\delta$. Here the pair class may be read in the nuclear theory, since $S$ is nuclear. Put $\sigma=\Theta\oplus\theta^\infty$. By [un:absorption](#un:absorption), there is a module unitary $V$ from the repeat module to the module of $\sigma$ such that $$\|V^*\sigma(a)V-\theta^\infty(a)\|<\eta,
 \qquad a\in\mathcal F.$$ With $W_0=1\oplus V$, set $$v=W_0^*(u\oplus1)W_0.$$ It is a unitary, and $v-1$ is compact because conjugating a compact module operator by a module unitary preserves compactness. The error in <a href="#un:proper-uniqueness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="un:proper-uniqueness">[un:proper-uniqueness]</a> is at most $\delta+2\eta$. Choose these parameters with $\delta+2\eta<\varepsilon$. This is the finite-test common-stabilizer transfer underlying (Dadarlat and Eilers 2001, Lemmas 2.4 and 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-52"></a>[[DadarlatEilers](#ref-DadarlatEilers)]; no initial value of a continuous unitary path is needed here. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-un-kk-nullity"></a>
<div id="un:KK-nullity" class="lemma">

**Lemma 12** (The trivially labeled factor). *For the coarse $X$-structure on $S=C_0(Y)\otimes P$ and every sigma-unital coefficient $X$-algebra $H$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{KK}}(X;S,H)=0.$$ In particular every weakly equivariant multiplier pair with compact difference has zero class in [un:stable-uniqueness](#un:stable-uniqueness).*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-un-kk-nullity"><summary>Proof of stmt-un-kk-nullity</summary>

*Proof.* Since $\mathcal W$ is simple and nuclear, every ideal of its spatial tensor product with another $C^*$-algebra is a tensor-product ideal (Gupta and Jain 2018, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-53"></a>[[GuptaJain](#ref-GuptaJain)]. Together with the transported labels of [st:ideals](#st:ideals), this gives, after reordering the tensor factors, an $X$-algebra $C=C_0(Y)\otimes A\otimes\mathcal K$ such that $$S=\mathcal W\otimes C,\qquad S(U)=\mathcal W\otimes C(U).$$ The identity of $\mathcal W$ has zero ordinary Kasparov class. Exterior tensoring its stabilized cycle relation and its homotopy with $C$ retains weak equivariance. Explicitly, a typical coefficient of the tensor representation, for $c\in C(U)$, is a finite sum $$\sum_{i,j}\langle\xi_i,\pi(w)\xi_j\rangle
                  \otimes c_i^*c c_j\ \in\ \mathcal W\otimes C(U).$$ The same formula applies throughout the homotopy. It follows that the identity cycle of $S$ is zero for these labels. Taking its cycle product with an $S$–$H$ cycle preserves this stabilized null relation. The identity-product homotopy identifies the same product with the original cycle, so that cycle is zero as well.

The cycle relation and mixed product statements used here are (Gabe 2024, Propositions 12.17 and 12.20 and Lemmas 12.18, 12.19, and 12.23) <a id="pm-cite-54"></a>[[Gabe](#ref-Gabe)]. They require the first algebra to be separable and the remaining coefficient algebras to be sigma-unital. Thus they apply to this $H$ even when it is nonseparable. Nuclearity of $S$ also makes the cycles and homotopies weakly $X$-nuclear, as needed above. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-un-hereditary-support"></a>
<div id="un:hereditary-support" class="lemma">

<a id="un:support-bound"></a>
**Lemma 13** (Hereditary support and local multiplication). *Let $I\subseteq C$ be a separable $C^*$-subalgebra with strictly positive element $e$, and let $k\in C_+$. Set $$H=\mathop{\mathrm{Her}}_{M_2(C)}(e\oplus k).$$ Then $H$ is sigma-unital, with no separability assertion required. If $r\in C$ idealizes $I$, then $r\oplus0$ idealizes $H$. If, in addition, $L$ is an ideal of $M_2(C)$ and $$(rI)\oplus0\subseteq L,\qquad (Ir)\oplus0\subseteq L,$$ then $$\label{un:support-bound}
 (r\oplus0)H+H(r\oplus0)\subseteq H\cap L.$$ For $x\in H$, one has $H\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_{M_2(C)}(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_H(x)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-un-hereditary-support"><summary>Proof of stmt-un-hereditary-support</summary>

*Proof.* The defining positive element is strictly positive in its hereditary algebra, so $H$ is sigma-unital. Since $r e^{1/2}$ and $e^{1/2}r$ belong to $I$, multiplication of $(e^{1/2}\oplus k^{1/2})M_2(C)(e^{1/2}\oplus k^{1/2})$ on either side by $r\oplus0$ remains in $H$. These elements are dense in $H$. Under the additional hypothesis, the same products also lie in $L$, proving <a href="#un:support-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="un:support-bound">[un:support-bound]</a>. The last identity is the ideal correspondence for a hereditary subalgebra: an approximate unit of $H$ compresses the ambient ideal generated by $x$ to the ideal it generates in $H$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-un-finite-truncation"></a>
